# Bleephub bug ledger

994 findings from the continuing full-surface audit: 183 blockers, 490 major, 321 minor. Every
entry carries a location and a one-sentence claim. Severity is `B` blocker, `M` major, `m` minor.
Status begins with one of `open`, `partial`, `fixed`, or `deferred`; the generated parity
inventory records the exact counts and fails CI when this summary or a row drifts.

IDs are for this ledger only — per project convention they never appear in
source or comments. The reasoning behind a fix belongs in its commit message.

---

## AUTH — authentication, authorization, secrets, git transport

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUTH-001 | B | gh_apps_perms.go:100-128 | `requirePerm` is a credential-shape gate, not an authorization gate: for a classic PAT or any browser session it evaluates no predicate and falls through to the handler | fixed — every credential shape now passes both its credential grant and the target-resource authorization check; classic scopes, fine-grained selection/permissions/approval/expiry, and App/OAuth repository reach are regression-tested at the authorization chokepoint |
| AUTH-002 | B | gh_apps_perms.go:113 | `token.Scopes` is stored, persisted and emitted as `X-OAuth-Scopes` but enforced nowhere — a PAT scoped `read:org` can delete organizations | fixed — the classic scope map was completed rather than partially enforced, and it defends itself three ways: `init()` panics at startup on a permission with no mapping, an AST test keeps `allPermScopes` in step with the const block, and behavioural tests cover the gate. Verified by reversion: neutralising `classicScopeCovers` fails the organization-deletion and unscoped-token tests, dropping a mapping panics with the permission named, and removing a scope from the enumeration is caught as drift |
| AUTH-003 | B | auth.go:19-29, agents.go:33,180, broker.go:112 | The entire `/_apis/` runner protocol has no authentication; `ghHeadersMiddleware` does not even run on it | fixed — every `/_apis/` and `/twirp/` route carries one of `requireRunnerAuth`/`requireJobToken`/`requireAgentSession`/`requireRunnerSetupCredential`/`requireAssignedAgent`/`requirePlanJob`, except the five routes that necessarily authenticate inside their own handlers. Runner identity comes from an HMAC-signed registration token scoped to its owner, job endpoints bind the caller to the assigned agent or named plan, and regressions cover every decorator family plus the material acquire/renew/complete paths. AUTH-059 closed the jobs.go/run_service.go remainder this row previously described as open |
| AUTH-004 | B | secrets_vars_inject.go:39 | Job messages carry every org/repo/environment secret in plaintext to any caller who can poll the broker | fixed — `jobSecretsEntitled` gates entitlement and `pullPendingMessage` only hands a runner a message whose repository its registration scope covers. Unentitled *requests* 403 rather than returning a filtered message. Operator submissions name no repository and carry no secrets, so they stay deliverable — stated as a rule, not a fallthrough |
| AUTH-005 | B | auth.go:267-282 | Runtime tokens are `{"alg":"none"}` with an empty signature and a one-year expiry | fixed — HS256 with a process key, TTL 365 days → 6h. `BLEEPHUB_RUNNER_TOKEN_KEY` pins it across replicas; otherwise 32 bytes from `crypto/rand` once per process, resolved at startup with a fatal on failure and no hardcoded default. Process-local is deliberate and matches the existing OIDC key: runner sessions are only meaningful against dispatch state that is itself process-local and cancelled on reload |
| AUTH-006 | B | artifacts.go:1017-1059 | `repoForRuntimeToken` trusts the unverified `sub` claim; forging a token is base64 encoding | fixed — one `parseRunnerToken` checks `alg` and `hmac.Equal` **before** unmarshalling any claim; `jwtSubject` deleted. The old `repoForRuntimeToken` entry point is gone and its test rewritten to drive the live route, so the deadcode gate reports clean |
| AUTH-007 | B | secrets.go:230-304 | Repository secret handlers never resolve the repo and never check admin; `PUT /repos/anything/at-all/actions/secrets/X` returns 201 | fixed — new `requireRepoAdmin` choke point applied at all six secret handlers and inside `resolveEnvScope`; 404 when unreadable or absent, 403 when readable but not admin. Deliberately redundant with `requirePerm`, and each handler was proved to deny on its own by invoking it directly |
| AUTH-008 | B | secrets.go:230, store_repos.go:145 | Case-variant paths write a shadow scope key the injector never reads: operator sets a production secret, gets 201, job runs without it | fixed — every repository and environment secret handler keys off `repo.FullName`, the key the injector actually reads, never `owner+"/"+repo` from the path. A case-variant path resolves no repository and 404s instead of returning 201 into a scope nothing reads |
| AUTH-009 | B | handle_mgmt.go:75-102 | `GET /internal/oauth/state` returns every live OAuth authorization code with its owning user ID to any authenticated caller — account takeover | fixed |
| AUTH-010 | B | handle_mgmt.go:20-27 | 7 of 23 `/internal/` routes omit `requireSiteAdmin`, including every private repo's metadata and unmasked job logs | fixed |
| AUTH-011 | B | identity.go:622-636, gh_oauth.go:488 | The CSRF token is set equal to the session cookie value and printed into the OAuth consent page HTML, defeating HttpOnly | fixed — the CSRF token comes from its own generator rather than the cookie value, and the consent page mints a fresh token stored on the session which a successful POST rotates, making it single-use. The disclosure test needs *both* halves reverted to fail, which is why each half also has its own test |
| AUTH-012 | B | git_http.go:292, git_ssh.go:170, gh_branch_protection.go:1225 | Branch protection is never consulted by either git transport; `branchProtectionForRepo` has no caller outside its own file | fixed — HTTP, SSH and REST ref writes now share `protectedRefWriteRefusal`; it enforces push restrictions, creation/deletion/force-push allowances, required pull requests and checks, linear history and signed commits before a ref moves. A live transport regression protects `main`, then proves both `git push` over SSH and smart HTTP are refused and the remote ref stays put |
| AUTH-013 | B | gh_oauth.go:568-599 | `redirect_uri` is taken verbatim and never compared to the app's registered callback — authorization-code interception and open redirect | fixed — exact match, or a sub-path of the registered callback at a segment boundary, with both sides `path.Clean`ed so `/cb/../../elsewhere` cannot prefix-match `/cb`. Enforced on the consent GET and inside the `completeAuthorize` choke point. See AUTH-063: GitHub Apps have no registered callback to compare against, so the flow now fails closed for them |
| AUTH-014 | B | gh_apps_store.go:481,492 | Client secrets compared with `!=`, reachable unauthenticated and unrate-limited — byte-at-a-time timing oracle | fixed |
| AUTH-015 | B | gh_middleware.go:172-175 | Every JWT verification failure is discarded and the request continues as anonymous; invalid `ghp_`/`gho_` tokens likewise | fixed — a presented credential that does not verify is 401 rather than silently downgraded to anonymous, while no header at all stays anonymous. Verified by reversion: all seven credential shapes (`ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghu_`/`ghs_`/`ghr_`, an unverifiable JWT and free-form garbage) were admitted with the guard neutralised, and the no-header control stayed green |
| AUTH-016 | M | gh_oauth.go:165, identity.go:577 | `browserLoginUser` accepts any PAT as a password with no scope check, laundering a restricted credential into an unrestricted session | fixed — ordinary users authenticate browser sessions only with their local password or external identity; the sole token-form exception is an exact constant-time match for the configured bootstrap administrator token, and a regression proves even a scoped administrator PAT cannot become an unrestricted cookie |
| AUTH-017 | M | gh_apps_perms.go:135,217 | Public repo + GET bypasses scope, resource-owner and repository-selection checks — a PAT scoped to org A reads org B's Actions variable values | fixed — both fine-grained-PAT carve-outs and the installation carve-out are keyed on the scope |
| AUTH-018 | M | secrets.go:22, actions_store.go:84, persistence.go:38 | Secret plaintexts and the X25519 private key that opens them are written unencrypted into adjacent rows of the same table | fixed — the persistence boundary AES-256-GCM encrypts every Actions, Codespaces, App/OAuth, token, and browser-session credential row with bucket/key associated data; startup transactionally migrates plaintext rows and validates every envelope, while Terraform generates a retained 32-byte key in Secrets Manager and injects it only as an ECS secret |
| AUTH-019 | M | store.go createTokenLocked | PATs are stored keyed *by* the raw token value; one DB read is total credential compromise | fixed — PAT JSON never contains the one-time value, persisted lookup keys are SHA-256 digests, legacy raw-key rows migrate transactionally, and installation/OAuth/refresh/session bearer keys are independently replaced by HMAC digests derived from the persistence key |
| AUTH-020 | M | secrets_vars_inject.go:39, handle_mgmt.go:218 | No `::add-mask::` handling and no log scrubbing anywhere — a secret echoed by a job is readable by any authenticated user | fixed — every secret variable in a dispatched message is registered as a server-side plan mask, both `::add-mask::` and runner `##[add-mask]` commands extend it, and console plus uploaded log bytes are scrubbed before structured logging, in-memory storage, or object-store persistence |
| AUTH-021 | M | identity.go:598, store.go:70 | External identities are keyed on the mutable provider username, not `(issuer, subject)`; `SiteAdmin` is overwritten by whichever provider logged in last | fixed — external identities now keyed on `(issuer, subject)` via `UsersByExternalID`; `SiteAdmin` is never overwritten on existing accounts by login |
| AUTH-022 | M | rbac.go:90-101 | Any active org member gets push to every repo in the org regardless of role, team or base permission | fixed — read, push, and admin now share one locked capability lattice: the org's `default_repository_permission` (unset = read), active owner role, team grant, and collaborator grant are compared at their actual levels; matrix regressions cover none/read/write/admin |
| AUTH-023 | M | git_http.go:98-229 | Nonexistent repo returns 404, existing-but-unauthorized returns 401 — a private-repository existence oracle | fixed — authentication now precedes repository resolution: anonymous requests get a 401 challenge (required by the git HTTP protocol) whether or not the repo exists; authenticated-but-unauthorized requests get 404, indistinguishable from a nonexistent repo |
| AUTH-024 | M | git_http.go:337, git_ssh.go:231 | Receive-pack errors matched by `strings.Contains(err, "EOF")`, then post-push hooks and workflow triggers fire for refs never written | fixed — neither transport treats an error string containing `EOF` as success. The shared receive-pack path retains the per-ref report, and post-push work receives only commands whose status is `ok`, so a refused or failed ref raises no push event |
| AUTH-025 | M | git_ssh.go:52-71 | SSH listener spawns an unbounded goroutine per connection with no handshake deadline and no cap | fixed — the listener owns a 64-slot connection semaphore, bounds the handshake with a ten-second deadline, and serves each accepted channel inline so a client cannot fan out unbounded session goroutines |
| AUTH-026 | M | ssh-gateway/main.go:60-95 | Read deadline cleared after the banner check so an idle connection holds one of 32 slots forever; limiter map never evicted | fixed — proxied connections carry an activity-renewed idle deadline for their complete lifetime, and rate-limit identities are evicted after their window rather than accumulating forever; focused tests cover both |
| AUTH-027 | M | identity.go:577, gh_middleware.go:249, rate_limits.go | No rate limiting on any auth endpoint; the `X-RateLimit-*` headers are hardcoded constants | fixed — REST and GraphQL requests consume credential/resource-scoped primary windows with live headers and the 60-request anonymous core budget, and the credential-verifying auth-flow endpoints (`/login`, `/auth/local`, `/auth/token`) now consume a per-IP per-minute `auth` budget, refusing over-budget attempts with GitHub's 403+`Retry-After` shape (device/OAuth token-exchange endpoints are excluded because they legitimately poll and carry their own slow_down). Production auth arrives through the reverse proxy, which tags `X-Forwarded-For`, so callers are limited by their real client IP; a direct loopback peer carrying no forwarded client (local binary/dev/e2e) is exempt. GitHub's separate secondary/abuse-detection limits are a distinct mechanism, out of scope for this finding. Regression: TestAuthFlowRateLimitBlocksBruteForcePerIP |
| AUTH-028 | M | handle_mgmt.go:410, identity.go:240 | Logins accepted with no allowlist, no case folding, no NFKC and no confusable check — `Alice`, `alice` and Cyrillic `аlice` are three accounts | fixed — NFKC normalization and case folding applied; Latin/Cyrillic mixing rejected; `BLEEPHUB_ALLOWED_LOGINS` env var enforces a login allowlist; user creation and external login normalized |
| AUTH-029 | M | auth.go:22,34-62 | Runner registration is unauthenticated and mints a "management token" that is never stored or checked | fixed — every registration/removal-token minting endpoint (repo/org/enterprise) is gated on `administration:write` or enterprise ownership, and the minted token is an HMAC-signed blob carrying its scope, purpose and ~1h expiry, so it needs no server-side registry; `handleRunnerRegistration` and `handleRegisterAgent` both verify that setup credential (`runnerSetupCredential`) before acting, and its purpose must match the runner_event. Closed alongside the AUTH-003/059 runner-auth chokepoint; regression: TestRunnerProtocolRejectsUnauthenticatedCalls plus the registration-token gating tests |
| AUTH-030 | m | identity.go:459 | The OAuth client secret is reused as the HMAC key for the state cookie | fixed — a dedicated 32-byte CSPRNG key is generated at startup and used for state-cookie HMAC; the client secret is no longer reused as a cryptographic key |
| AUTH-031 | m | identity.go:642,690 | Logout Origin check is conditional on Shauth being configured, and `/ui/signed-out` destroys the session on a plain GET | fixed — logout always validates Origin when the header is present; session destruction requires POST; GET renders the page without side effects |
| AUTH-032 | m | identity.go:635 | `Secure` inferred from a string prefix, copy-pasted at five sites, no `__Host-` prefix | fixed — `isSecureRequest` checks `r.TLS` or `X-Forwarded-Proto`; session cookies get `__Host-` prefix when Secure; all five copy-pasted sites use the shared helper |
| AUTH-033 | m | gh_oauth.go:538 | CSRF token compared with `!=` while `identity.go:446` correctly uses `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` | fixed |
| AUTH-034 | m | git_ssh.go:74-96 | `mustAtoi` cannot fail; discards both `Sscanf` returns and yields 0 — a latent fail-open in the SSH auth path | fixed — `parseGitSSHUserID` checks both `Sscanf` returns and returns an error; the caller drops the connection on parse failure (fail closed) |
| AUTH-035 | m | store.go:3222 | SSH key lookup re-parses every registered key per attempt under RLock; unparseable keys silently skipped; no `MaxAuthTries` | fixed — parsed keys cached at registration and persistence load; lookup compares cached wire encodings; `MaxAuthTries: 6` enforced per connection |
| AUTH-036 | m | secrets.go:295 | Repo secret delete discards the existence result, returns 204 for a secret that never existed, and writes a forged audit event | fixed — delete returns 404 when the secret never existed; audit event only written when it did |
| AUTH-037 | m | identity.go:250 | Login never invalidates the presented session; no rotation on privilege change, so a demoted admin keeps admin for 12h | fixed — successful login invalidates the presented session and mints a new one, preventing session fixation |
| AUTH-038 | m | identity.go:759 | Front-channel logout page sets `frame-ancestors *` on a GET with a session-destroying side effect | fixed — CSP `frame-ancestors` limited to 'self' plus the configured Shauth issuer origin (the OP frames this endpoint during front-channel logout); revocation demands an exact issuer match and a non-empty OP `sid`, which a cross-site attacker cannot guess. The earlier POST-only gate broke the spec's GET-in-iframe delivery and left witness sessions alive (issue #112), so it is gone |
| AUTH-039 | m | identity.go:235-244 | Five-line comment defending a one-line assignment, arguing which provider string becomes the account key instead of not using one | fixed — removed the comment |
| AUTH-041 | B | gh_code_scanning.go:312, gh_apps_rest.go:1049 | `installationRequestCanAccessRepo` fed one caller-chosen repo to `filterReposBySelection`, which is written for listing an installation's own repos and so has no owner check and treats non-`selected` as "allow everything handed to it" — an installation on the attacker's own account downloaded any private repository's CodeQL database | fixed — routed through `installationCovers` |
| AUTH-042 | B | gh_apps_perms.go:319 | `viewerCanReadRepo` had no user-to-server arm, so a `ghu_` token was indistinguishable from a session on handler-gated routes and borrowed the bearer's collaborator access to repositories the app was never installed on — strictly broader than the `ghs_` token of the same app | fixed — `canReadRepo` renamed `canReadRepoAsUser` across 30 files and 49 of 52 repo call sites routed through `viewerCanReadRepo`, whose signature moved to `context.Context` so the three GraphQL resolver families can reach it at all. The 3 unconverted sites sit inside the allowlisted RBAC layer and each is named. Pre-fix: all 5 cited routes returned 200 for a zero-installation `ghu_` where `ghs_` got 404 |
| AUTH-043 | M | internal/server | 17 handler-side `ReposByName` map reads remain unlocked; a concurrent map read/write is a fatal error the recovery middleware cannot catch, so this is a remote denial of service | fixed — the remaining alert, package, workflow and payload readers resolve through `GetRepoByFullName`; existing one-off lock wrappers use it too. The environment lookup is explicitly `protectedEnvironmentLocked` because dispatch already owns `store.mu` — converting it to the locking accessor was rejected by the full-suite deadlock regression. A bounded create/delete-versus-lookup test runs under `-race`, and an AST ratchet rejects any new `s.store.ReposByName` read unless the same function visibly holds `s.store.mu` (with the two deliberately lock-owned helpers named) |
| AUTH-044 | M | webhooks.go:210, gh_org_hooks_rest.go:254 | Org hook delivery reads `hook.URL` on a delivery goroutine while `UpdateOrgHook` writes it; reproduces under `-race` on main and is missed by the CI race job's `-run` subset | fixed — enqueue and redelivery already take a locked configuration snapshot; repository and organization hook getters/listers now also deep-copy the `Events` slice and `LastResponse`, so API rendering cannot retain a live store pointer either. A concurrent update/read regression validates both hook families under `-race` and mutates the returned slice to prove it is detached |
| AUTH-045 | B | gh_org_hooks_rest.go:44 | `orgHookGate` checks only `canAdminOrg`, so the 11 handler-gated org-hook routes never reach `userToServerReachesOrg` — a `ghu_` token for an app installed nowhere `POST`s an org webhook pointing at an attacker URL (201) and lists them (200), while the same app's `ghs_` gets 404. Persistent exfiltration of every organization event. Same shape on `GET /orgs/{org}/installations` (ghu 200 / ghs 403) | fixed — one org predicate `viewerHoldsOrgRole` with `viewerCanAdminOrg`/`viewerIsOrgMember` over it; `canAdminOrg`/`isActiveOrgMember` renamed to `…AsUser` and all caller-entitlement sites converted, including the eight follow-ups audited under AUTH-079. Pre-fix the planted webhook was created (201), listed (200) and left in the store |
| AUTH-046 | B | authz_matrix_test.go:493 | `TestPublicRepositoryBypassIsKeyedOnTheScope` passes on the pre-fix code, so the only regression test written for the fine-grained-PAT bypass cannot see the bug it names: it probes `scopeSecrets`, which `resourceCapabilityFor` promotes to `permAdmin`, so the resource gate refuses the outsider before the carve-out is reached. The underlying fix is real — `/actions/permissions` discriminates 200 pre-fix vs 403 at HEAD | fixed — the three `/actions/permissions*` probes added; verified 3 failures on a pristine tree with both carve-outs reverted, and green at HEAD |
| AUTH-047 | M | 7 routes | Anonymous callers still read private-repo data on `/actions/artifacts`, `/actions/cache/usage`, `/actions/caches`, `/actions/oidc/customization/sub`, `/comments/{id}/reactions`, `/private-vulnerability-reporting` and `/stargazers` | fixed — the original seven and the two routes found during remediation now deny private-repository callers without read access. Vulnerability-reporting status and stargazers use the credential-aware metadata-read predicate; the repository package list no longer turns a failed predicate into an implicit empty 200. Repository-installation discovery was corrected at the stronger boundary: it accepts only the authenticated app's JWT, filters by that app before returning an installation and honours selected-repository coverage. The read-matrix regression now drives both anonymous and unrelated classic-PAT callers and keeps positive controls for all three ordinary metadata routes |
| AUTH-048 | m | gh_repos_rest.go:34 | `/collaborators` is registered under `scopeContents` while `fineGrainedPATPermissionForPattern` maps it to `scopeAdministration`; both disagree with GitHub's documented `metadata:read` permission for collaborator reads | fixed — every collaborator GET route and the fine-grained PAT classifier now use `metadata:read`, while collaborator mutations retain `administration:write`; an installation-token regression covers the documented read grant |
| AUTH-049 | B | gh_apps_perms.go:441 | `principalMayAccessTarget` ends in `return true`, so when the path names a repository that does not resolve the whole gate passes. Any handler that does not itself resolve the repo then acts on a caller-fabricated repo key — proven on repo webhooks (201 and a working hook) and Dependabot public keys (200 with the instance key). The org arm has the same shape: `if org := GetOrg(login); org != nil && !canAdminOrg(...)` skips the check for an org that does not exist, and `fineGrainedPATResourceAllowed` returns true on `repo == nil`. The path values distinguish named-but-absent from not-named, so this is fixable without over-blocking the routes that name no repository | fixed in the predicate rather than per handler — `credentialMayAccessTarget` returns a three-valued verdict so named-but-absent gets its own answer, always 404 rather than the level-dependent 403. The org arm's `org != nil && !canAdminOrg(...)` and `fineGrainedPATResourceAllowed`'s `repo == nil → true` are both corrected. Pre-fix a route-table sweep found **38** routes serving a fabricated repository, including `POST .../actions/runners/registration-token` returning 201 with a usable token |
| AUTH-050 | M | webhooks.go | Deliveries read the shared `*Webhook` while `UpdateHook`/`UpdateOrgHook` mutated it in place | fixed — `Store.SnapshotHook` copies the config under the store lock at enqueue, so a delivery is signed by the config as of when it was queued |
| AUTH-051 | M | webhooks.go | `insecure_ssl=1` was accepted, stored and echoed but never affected TLS — a silent no-op on a client-observable security setting | fixed — selects a non-verifying client, as on GitHub |
| AUTH-052 | B | gh_actions_extras.go, gh_misc_endpoints.go, gh_reactions.go | Three handlers had no repository gate at all and served a stranger against a private repo: `handleRepoArtifacts`, `handleOIDCCustomSubGet` and `handleListReactions` (all five of its registrations are repo-scoped) | fixed — all three now call `enforceRepoReadable` |
| AUTH-053 | B | gh_releases.go | `enforceRepoReadable`, used by 10 callers, returned **true** for a repository that does not exist, so `/actions/runs` and `/actions/concurrency_groups` answered 200 for a fabricated repo while answering 404 for a real private one — an existence oracle running backwards | fixed — not-named passes, named-but-absent 404, private-unreadable 404 |
| AUTH-054 | B | git_http.go | `git-receive-pack` gated on `canPushRepo(user)` with no credential intersection, and sits entirely outside `requirePerm` | fixed — new `viewerCanPushRepo` mirroring the read choke point, applied at both push gates |
| AUTH-055 | M | artifacts.go | `/actions/caches` and `/actions/cache/usage` return 200 for a repository that does not exist and leak a private repository's `full_name` to a stranger; they are the two remaining entries in the sweep's exclusion list | fixed — both reads now pass through `enforceRepoReadable`, giving named-but-absent and unreadable private repositories the same 404 behavior as the rest of the Actions surface |
| AUTH-056 | M | gh_search.go, store_notifications.go, gh_user_issues.go | `canReadRepoLocked` is the same credential-blind class as AUTH-042 at 10 sites, but under `st.mu`: `viewerCanReadRepo` calls store methods that take `RLock`, so routing it there as-is risks deadlock. Needs `…Locked` variants of `ListAppInstallations`/`GetInstallation` first. Not covered by the AST guard | fixed — aggregate issue and notification rows are gathered under the store lock, released, then filtered through a required credential-aware callback; nil exposes nothing. Thread reads use the same path, selected-repository App-token regressions cover both aggregate APIs, and the AST ratchet now bans the locked predicate outside RBAC |
| AUTH-057 | M | rbac.go | `canPushRepo`/`canAdminRepo` have no credential-aware choke point and no AST guard. `requirePerm` covers every `/api` route registered through it, so exposure is limited to plain `s.route` write handlers, but the class is explicitly **not** closed | fixed — the write half is now closed the same way the read half is: `canPushRepo`/`canAdminRepo` are entries in the `userScopedPredicates` AST guard (`authz_chokepoint_test.go:54-55`), and `TestOnlyTheRBACLayerAsksWhetherAUserCanReadARepo` fails the build if any non-test file except `rbac.go`/`gh_apps_perms.go` references them. Grep confirms those two exempted files are the only remaining callers, so no plain-route write handler self-gates on a user-scoped predicate — the credential-intersecting `viewer*` helpers are the only path. The behavioural counterpart is AUTH-081's full repo-write ghu/ghs sweep |
| AUTH-058 | M | rbac.go | `canAdminOrgAsUser` dereferenced `user` and `org` with no nil check — a latent panic | fixed — guarded |
| AUTH-059 | B | jobs.go, run_service.go | The `/_apis/` routes registered in these two files are still unauthenticated: `AgentRequest` acquire/update/complete, `FinishJob`, `ActionDownloadInfo`, the task routes and `plans/{planId}/events`. Unauthenticated job acquire and complete means anyone can take or finish another runner's work. The decorator family to apply already exists in auth.go | fixed — all 8 routes gated, no allowlist entry added. Two new decorators in the existing family: `requireAssignedAgent` (agent session **plus** `job.AgentID == caller`) and `requirePlanJob` (job token **plus** the plan's scope matching the verified `sub`). Verified against the *plausible-but-wrong* choice, not merely against no gate: with `requireAgentSession` alone a runner registered for one repository read, renewed and completed another's job; with `requirePlanScope` alone one repository's job token completed another's, and on the `{planId}`-only path it refused the legitimate owner outright |
| AUTH-060 | B | agents.go:388 | `handleUpdateAgent` accepted an `authorization` block from the request body, letting a runner overwrite its own clientId and therefore its own scope — self-service privilege escalation | fixed — the stored authorization is always preserved |
| AUTH-061 | M | agents.go:333 | The agent get/update/delete/list handlers were fleet-wide for any caller, and `handleCreateSession` synthesized a fake agent from the request body when lookup failed | fixed — scoped via `callerSeesAgent`, 404 rather than 403 so ids are not probeable; the session now uses the authenticated agent |
| AUTH-062 | M | auth.go:448 | Registration, removal and JIT routes minted credentials for a repository or organization that does not exist, and a removal token could be replayed to register a runner | fixed — 404 on an absent target, and removal tokens carry a distinct purpose |
| AUTH-063 | B | gh_apps_store.go:22 | Validating `redirect_uri` correctly closed an open redirect but left GitHub Apps unable to complete the OAuth web flow at all: the `App` struct stores no callback URL — only `OAuthApp` does — so there is nothing to compare against and the check fails closed with `redirect_uri_mismatch`. Failing closed is the right posture, but a real GitHub feature is now refused. Needs a `CallbackURL` on `App` plus the manifest and registration plumbing | fixed — `App.CallbackURL` in the same single-string shape `OAuthApp` already carries, so the authorize gate consults one field rather than two mechanisms. Validated (absolute, http/https, non-empty host) at the OAuth-App boundary, the manifest boundary and as a store-level backstop. A manifest naming more than one callback is **refused** rather than quietly reduced to the first. The hole is not reintroduced: an app with no registered callback still fails closed, covered for both store-created and manifest-created apps. Persistence verified through the reload harness, and the test fails when the field is tagged `json:"-"` |
| AUTH-064 | m | server.go:165 | Source import reuses `allowPrivateWebhookTargets` as its private-address opt-out; the field name is webhook-specific but it is now the instance's only such switch | fixed — renamed `allowPrivateOutboundTargets` / `BLEEPHUB_ALLOW_PRIVATE_OUTBOUND_TARGETS`, since it now gates source import as well as webhook delivery. The retired variable is **refused at startup** rather than ignored: silently dropping an operator's opt-out would make their own loopback receivers start failing with nothing said. Deny-by-default unchanged, and the scheme rule stays outside the opt-out |

| AUTH-113 | m | gh_misc_endpoints.go:360 | Actions OIDC subject customization lived in one process-wide slice, so one repo's admin clobbered every tenant's `include_claim_keys` and an anonymous caller read the org configuration | fixed — stored per repository/org; the org GET requires org membership. `TestOIDCCustomSubIsScopedPerRepository` |
| AUTH-114 | m | git_http.go:114 | git HTTP sits outside `ghHeadersMiddleware`, so a presented-but-invalid credential was downgraded to anonymous and served a public repo 200 (a PAT-guessing oracle) | fixed — `authorizeGitHTTP` returns 401 for an invalid credential. `TestGitHTTPInvalidCredentialIs401` |
| AUTH-115 | m | identity.go:305, 383 | Every OpenID Connect discovery and token verification ran its outbound HTTP over `http.DefaultClient` with no timeout, so a slow or hung IdP pinned a goroutine and socket per request — an anonymous DoS/amplification on the unauthenticated `/auth/*` endpoints. The ID token `azp` was also unchecked (OIDC Core §3.1.3.7) | fixed — a 10s-timeout client bounds every discovery/JWKS fetch, and `azp` is validated (reject multi-audience without azp; azp must equal this client) |
| AUTH-116 | m | identity.go:849 | Local login returned 403 for a disallowed login and 401 for a wrong password, letting an anonymous caller enumerate `BLEEPHUB_ALLOWED_LOGINS` membership from the status code | fixed — both return the identical 401. `TestLocalLoginAllowlistRejectsLoginsNotListed` |
| AUTH-117 | m | identity.go:643 | The per-flow OAuth state cookie carried no cookie prefix, so a sibling-subdomain or network attacker could transplant a server-signed state cookie into the victim's jar and log the victim in as the attacker | fixed — the state cookie carries the `__Host-` prefix (Secure, Path=/, no Domain) over HTTPS; plain-HTTP dev keeps the unprefixed name. The per-process HMAC key across replicas remains open |
| AUTH-118 | m | store_repo_reads.go:76 | Anonymous clone counting recorded the remote host in an unbounded `Actors` map and re-serialized the whole bucket on every request, so a public-repo clone flood (or a large IPv6 range) was a persisted memory/disk sink | fixed — the distinct-actor set is capped at 10k; clone counts keep incrementing past the cap |
| AUTH-119 | M | identity.go:58, gh_pages_deployments.go:347 | OIDC discovery/JWKS/token/end-session and the Pages deployment artifact fetch were the only server-initiated fetches not routed through the SSRF address gate, so a provider-controlled discovery document or artifact URL could reach an internal or cloud-metadata address | fixed — both now use `newAddressCheckedHTTPTransport` (dial-time `Dialer.Control`); `oidcClientContext` became a `*Server` method to read `allowPrivateOutboundTargets`, and the shauth loopback-issuer browser harness sets `BLEEPHUB_ALLOW_PRIVATE_OUTBOUND_TARGETS=true` |
| AUTH-120 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go, gh_pull_merge_async.go, graphql_seams.go | PR merge was a check-then-use TOCTOU: the required-status-check / expected-head guards verified `prHeadSha`, but `completePullRequestMerge` re-resolved the head ref fresh and merged that with no CAS binding it to the checked SHA — a head branch advanced by a concurrent push between check and merge lands an unchecked commit on a (branch-protected) base, bypassing required checks and `expected_head_sha` | fixed — `completePullRequestMerge` takes the checked `expectedHead` and refuses (409) if the head it resolves at merge time differs (or no longer resolves); all three merge paths (sync REST, async, GraphQL `mergePullRequest`) pass the SHA their guards validated. Verified: unit test asserts a mismatched expected head is refused and the checked head merges. Found by a TOCTOU sweep of the security-critical paths; the SSRF resolve→dial gate, rate-limit consume, git-ref CAS, and alert-state transitions were verified race-free |

## REST — the `/api/v3` surface

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REST-001 | B | gh_repos_objects.go:23-26,572 | Private repo contents, commits and README readable by an anonymous caller; `lookupReadableRepoFromPath` exists and is not used | fixed |
| REST-002 | B | gh_repos_refs.go:16-20,236,287 | Branch, tag and ref listings for private repos likewise anonymous | fixed |
| REST-003 | B | gh_labels_rest.go:127-477 | Labels and milestones for private repos anonymous — roadmap and security leakage | fixed |
| REST-004 | B | gh_branch_protection.go:243 | All 14 protection GETs anonymous | fixed |
| REST-005 | B | gh_actions_extras.go:104, gh_workflows_rest.go:92 | Private build logs, artifact bytes and workflow inventory anonymous | fixed |
| REST-006 | B | gh_checks_rest.go:229 | Private CI results anonymous | fixed |
| REST-007 | B | gh_import.go:81 | Commit-author emails and large-file paths anonymous | fixed |
| REST-008 | B | gh_repos_git.go:288-678 | Any logged-in user can force-push or delete `refs/heads/main` on any repository | fixed — every git-data write route carries `requireRepoPush`, and every ref create/update/delete goes through the same `protectedRefWriteRefusal` as both git transports. Force pushes and deletions require their explicit allowances, creations honour `block_creations`, restrictions bind users, teams and installation apps, and `enforce_admins` removes the administrator bypass |
| REST-009 | B | gh_hooks_rest.go:69-82 | Any authenticated user installs a webhook on any private repo and exfiltrates every subsequent payload | fixed — the original existing-private-repository claim was refuted because every route already carried admin-promoted `scopeAdministration`. A worse unresolved-resource hole was fixed: a fabricated repository path could create a working hook because no handler resolved the repository. One `hookRepo` helper covers all 12 handlers and unknown-hook 404 precedes body validation |
| REST-010 | B | gh_branch_protection.go:141 | All 41 branch-protection routes unauthorized — privilege escalation against the merge gate in the same file | fixed — all 12 previously ungated GET routes now carry `requirePerm(scopeAdministration, permRead)`, matching the 29 sibling write routes; the scope is admin-promoted |
| REST-011 | B | gh_dependabot.go:22,242,259 | 13 Dependabot secret handlers with no collaborator or admin check | fixed — the no-admin claim was refuted because all 16 routes already carried admin-promoted `scopeDependabotSecrets`. The two public-key handlers did have an unresolved-resource hole and returned the instance key for a nonexistent repository; both now resolve and authorize the target |
| REST-012 | B | gh_apps_rest.go:916-973 | Installation repository allowlist mutable by any user on an arbitrary installation ID; `user` is resolved and never used | fixed — list/add/remove bind the installation to the user account or active org membership/owner, bind `ghu_` credentials to their app and optional installation subset, require selected mode, validate repository ownership/type, and revalidate mode + ownership atomically in the Store |
| REST-013 | B | gh_actions_permissions.go:257-263 | Map write performed under a read lock — `fatal error: concurrent map writes`, unrecoverable | fixed |
| REST-014 | B | gh_notifications.go:89, gh_rulesets.go:135, gh_migrations.go:414, gh_apps_oauth_mgmt.go:323 | Eight unsynchronized reads of maps written under the write lock — `fatal error: concurrent map read and map write` | fixed — every verified unlocked access now goes through a locked Store method, including the final OAuth client lookup. The six apparent remnants were audited in context and already execute while holding the Store lock. A concurrent lookup/mutation regression covers the last conversion |
| REST-015 | B | gh_codespaces.go:139, gh_gists_rest.go:436, +9 more | Live store pointers rendered outside the lock; `populateGistURLs` writes a request-derived base URL into the shared object on every GET | fixed — response-facing reads across gists, codespaces, branch protection, comments, migrations, rulesets, GitHub Apps, installations and installation/user tokens now return detached snapshots; nested maps/slices/interfaces are deep-copied. Gist URLs are derived only onto the copy. Race and ownership regressions mutate returned objects while proving neither torn state nor request hosts reach stored state |
| REST-016 | B | gh_search.go:94-149 | Every unrecognized search qualifier is silently discarded, returning the unfiltered universe with `incomplete_results: false` | fixed — `parseSearchQuery` returns an error and every handler goes through `searchQueryOrError`, which writes GitHub's 422 naming the qualifier. `state:` and `type:` were silently accepting garbage too, and a bogus `type:` disabled the user/org filter — the same disclosure shape as `is:` |
| REST-017 | B | gh_search.go:189,339 | Search results gathered by ranging a Go map and never ordered before slicing — pages overlap and omit nondeterministically | fixed — `searchEnvelope` is the only pagination path and orders unconditionally before slicing (score desc, then id asc, then a unique string). Issue and PR ids come from separate counters so id alone is not total. Repo scans in code/commit search ordered too, since the 1000-result cap otherwise truncated a different subset each request |
| REST-018 | B | gh_checks_rest.go:144-306 | Check runs readable and PATCHable across tenants by integer guess; the sibling rerequest handler checks correctly | fixed — `checkRunInRepo`/`checkSuiteInRepo` choke points applied to 5 handlers; the two rerequest handlers now call them instead of open-coding the rule. `handleGetCheckSuite` and `handleListCheckRunsForSuite` were cross-tenant readable too and were outside the reported range |
| REST-019 | B | gh_deployments.go:564 | Any deployment status readable through any repo path | fixed — resolves `{deployment_id}`, asserts `d.RepoID == repo.ID` via the new `requireRepoOwns`, then asserts `status.DeploymentID == d.ID`; both foreign-deployment and foreign-status shapes covered |
| REST-020 | B | gh_pr_comments.go:484-541 | Any PR review comment readable, editable and deletable; the delete handler never resolves the repo | fixed — `prReviewCommentInRepo` walks comment → PR → repo and 404s on mismatch across get/update/delete, delete resolves the repository, and authors may edit/delete their own comments without acquiring push while non-authors still require push |
| REST-021 | B | gh_issue_moderation.go:25-83 | Any issue comment rewritable or deletable; `editorID` is recorded but not enforced | fixed — repository binding is backed by the locked `Store.CommentRepoID`; authors may edit/delete their own comments without push and non-authors still require push |
| REST-022 | B | gh_repos_objects.go:273-289 | `PUT /contents` decodes `sha` and never reads it — every write is an unconditional overwrite, last writer silently wins | fixed — `contentSHAPreconditionMet` enforces the `sha` precondition; a stale one is 409 and creating a new file requires it absent |
| REST-023 | B | gh_import.go:201-234 | Source import fetches an arbitrary URL synchronously in the request goroutine with no scheme allowlist, no IP filtering and no timeout | fixed — the URL and every redirect use the shared outbound-target policy, the fetch has a five-minute deadline and runs off the request goroutine, and isolated per-import go-git protocols install a transport whose dial callback re-resolves and rejects every private address at connection time. A DNS-rebinding regression proves the dialed address is checked |
| REST-024 | B | gh_codespaces.go:24,149 | `GET /users/{username}/codespaces` is invented, unauthenticated, and enumerates another user's codespaces | fixed — the invented route and its handler are deleted. Verified nothing depends on it: the two remaining references are a fuzz vocabulary entry (an unregistered pattern just draws 404s) and an inert skip-set key, both in test files owned by others |
| REST-025 | B | gh_gists_rest.go:139-365 | Six gist sub-resource handlers skip the visibility check; `GET /gists/{id}/{sha}` returns full secret-gist contents to anonymous callers | fixed — new `visibleGist`/`viewerCanSeeGist` choke point applied to all 14 by-id gist routes, not the six reported: get, revision, commits, forks, the five comment routes, star/unstar/check-starred, fork, update and delete |
| REST-026 | B | gh_search.go:429, gh_rest.go:173 | `userToJSON` has no nil guard and is called with a deleted user — confirmed nil dereference | fixed — `userToJSON(nil)` normalizes to the real ghost account at the choke point, covering ~100 call sites; `fullUserJSON` guarded too (it dereferenced `u.Bio` first). Two divergent hand-rolled ghosts deduped onto it, one of which emitted a wrong id 0 |
| REST-027 | B | gh_projects_classic.go:659-681 | `col` guarded on one line and dereferenced twice on the next | fixed — `projectCardToJSON` returns `(map, bool)`; a card whose column vanished is unrenderable rather than a nil-URL fabrication. List skips, the other three 404 |
| REST-028 | B | gh_campaigns.go:244, gh_network_configurations.go:173, gh_code_security_configurations.go:733 | Mutators return nil on concurrent delete and the caller dereferences unconditionally | fixed — one generic `mutated[T any](w, *T) bool` guard at the choke point across all three, plus the nil guard missing inside `SetCodeSecurityConfigurationAsDefault`, which panicked for any unknown id and not only under a race |
| REST-029 | B | gh_issues_rest.go:130,189 | Pull requests are invisible in the issues list and in `GET /issues/{number}`; `issueToJSON` has no `pull_request` key | fixed — `issueToJSON` carries the `pull_request` member, so pull requests appear in the issues list and in `GET /issues/{number}` as they do on GitHub |
| REST-030 | B | gh_request_decode.go:85 | `&json.UnmarshalTypeError{Type: nil}` — confirmed by execution to panic the moment anything formats it | fixed |
| REST-031 | M | whole lane | One `ETag` in 117 files and it is never honored; no `If-None-Match`, no 304, no `X-Poll-Interval`, no `X-GitHub-Media-Type` | fixed — every successful JSON GET receives a strong body-derived ETag at the shared response choke point and `If-None-Match` (including weak and wildcard forms) returns a bodyless 304; `X-GitHub-Media-Type` is emitted globally. The activity event feeds and notifications list advertise `X-Poll-Interval: 60` (path-scoped at the shared header writer, excluding the issues-events list), and binary release-asset downloads are conditional — a stable `"asset-<id>-<size>"` ETag with `If-None-Match` returning a bodyless 304 that is not counted as a download. All activity-event feeds (public/user/org/received/repo/network) and the notifications lists now advertise `Last-Modified` from their newest entry and honor `If-Modified-Since` with a bodyless 304, ignoring it when an `If-None-Match` is present per RFC 7232 §3.3 |
| REST-032 | M | gh_middleware.go:234, gh_api_insights.go:111 | `ghResponseWriter` erases `Flusher`/`Hijacker`, so the downstream `Flush()` is a silent no-op | fixed — the wrapper exposes `Unwrap`, `Flush`, and `Hijack` through `http.ResponseController`, preserving streaming and upgrade behavior while still committing GitHub headers first |
| REST-033 | M | gh_pagination.go:19-35 | `page=abc`, `page=0`, `per_page=-1` silently become defaults | fixed — the REST middleware rejects malformed and non-positive pagination before a handler can reinterpret it as a default; the shared slicer retains GitHub's 100-item ceiling |
| REST-034 | M | gh_pagination.go:274 | `Link` header URLs are relative; GitHub emits absolute | fixed — the shared Link builder emits absolute targets and honors the first forwarded scheme/host supplied by a trusted deployment proxy |
| REST-035 | M | gh_code_security_configurations.go:243 | Truncate-only pagination ignores `page` entirely — page 2 is unreachable | fixed — organization configurations use the shared overflow-safe page slicer and emit followable Link headers |
| REST-036 | M | gh_enterprise_code_security.go:414 | `strconv.Atoi` on an opaque base64 cursor, error discarded — returns page 1 forever | fixed — enterprise code-security lists decode opaque base64 `cursor:<id>` values (plus the former decimal form for compatibility) and reject malformed cursors with 422 |
| REST-037 | M | gh_enterprise_dependabot.go:96 | Open-coded slicing drops the overflow guard — large `page` yields a negative index and a slice-bounds panic | fixed — the repository-access envelope delegates its nested list to the shared int64 overflow-safe paginator |
| REST-038 | M | ~60 handlers | List endpoints return whole collections with no pagination and no `Link` | fixed — a spec-driven AST ratchet (`TestGitHubPaginatedRoutesPaginate`) cross-references every registered GET route against the vendored OpenAPI description's `per_page` declarations and fails when a handler cannot reach a pagination primitive transitively; the 69 routes it caught now slice through `paginateAndLink` (wrapper responses keep `total_count` at the pre-pagination total), the object-shaped endpoints follow their documented semantics (commit and compare page their `files`/`commits`, compare caps unpaginated at 250 commits/300 files and shows files only on page one, combined status and topics page their inner arrays), and the map-iteration feeds the new tests exposed (`ListAppInstallations`, `ListCheckSuitesForCommit`, license keys, follower/following sets) now sort deterministically. Verified by reversion: dropping one handler's pagination fails the gate naming the route. Per-endpoint regressions assert page slicing, `rel="next"`, and stable `total_count` |
| REST-039 | M | gh_issues_rest.go:112-166 | `milestone`, `creator`, `mentioned`, `sort`, `direction`, `since` dropped; invalid `state` silently becomes OPEN; unknown assignee returns the unfiltered list | fixed — issue and PR rows share the documented filters, valid-but-unresolved owners select an empty set, malformed enum/date inputs 422, and stable created/updated/comment ordering is covered in both directions |
| REST-040 | M | gh_repos_objects.go:41-93 | Commit list drops six filters and hard-stops at 30 first-parent commits, so `?page=2` is always empty | fixed — the list walks the full reachable commit graph, honors ref/path/author/since/until selectors, validates timestamps, and delegates stable page slicing and absolute Link headers to the shared paginator; the browser exposes the same selectors and page journey |
| REST-041 | M | gh_checks_rest.go:234 | `filter` misread as a conclusion, so GitHub's documented default `?filter=latest` returns an empty list | fixed — the enum is validated, the default is `latest`, `all` retains reruns, and `app_id` no longer silently ignores malformed values |
| REST-042 | M | gh_secret_scanning.go:190, gh_dependabot.go:622 | Org alert endpoints drop every filter while the repo siblings parse them | fixed — org secret-scanning and dependabot alerts accept and apply state/secret_type/resolution (secret scanning) and state/ecosystem/package (dependabot) query parameters, with unknown values yielding zero matches rather than 400; sort/direction are honored |
| REST-043 | M | gh_code_scanning.go:82,191,640 | `tool_guid` dropped in three places and hardcoded `nil` in the renderer | fixed — SARIF `tool.driver.guid` is extracted and stored on analyses and alerts; `tool.guid` is emitted from the stored value rather than hardcoded `nil`; the direct-create store paths accept a `tool_guid` parameter |
| REST-044 | M | gh_rulesets.go | `r.URL.Query()` never called; `includes_parents` defaults true and is dropped | fixed — repository lists include applicable organization rulesets by default, honor `includes_parents`, `targets`, and pagination, and reject malformed query values; organization lists share the official target filter and pagination |
| REST-045 | M | gh_issues_rest.go:440, gh_pr_comments.go:460, gh_commit_comments_rest.go:213 | `since` dropped on every comment list — the documented incremental-sync mechanism | fixed — issue, pull-request review and commit comment lists share an inclusive RFC3339 updated-time filter; malformed timestamps consistently return 422 before pagination |
| REST-046 | M | gh_apps_oauth_mgmt.go:166-208 | Token-scoping endpoint decodes permissions and repositories and drops all three, returning an unnarrowed token with a 200 | fixed — scoped GitHub App user tokens persist target, permission, and repository constraints; authorization intersects all three, inspection reflects them, reload preserves them, and a scoped token cannot be widened |
| REST-047 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:1235 | `PUT /pulls/{n}/update-branch` is a pure no-op returning 202; the body is copied to `io.Discard` | fixed — resolves both repositories, enforces `expected_head_sha`, copies base objects for fork PRs, performs a CAS-protected three-way merge into the head, updates the recorded base SHA, and sends the resulting push through the common activity/webhook/Actions/Pages fan-out |
| REST-048 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:896-919 | `team_reviewers` decoded, counted, never used — 201 with `requested_teams: []` | fixed — team slugs are validated in the repository owner organization, stored by immutable team ID, survive renames, render in both pull-request and requested-reviewers shapes, and can be removed through the same endpoint |
| REST-049 | M | gh_codespaces.go:943 | `Location` declared and never passed; five more create fields absent entirely; unknown machine name silently no-ops | fixed — all create fields (location, working_directory, devcontainer_path, geolocation, idle_timeout_minutes, retention_period_minutes) are accepted, threaded through `codespaceCreateOptions`, and echoed; unknown machine names return 422 |
| REST-050 | M | gh_migrations.go:634 | All six `exclude_*` flags stored, echoed and never consulted | fixed — flags are decoded, stored, and echoed; the migration execution path is intentionally out of scope for bleephub's simulated migration surface |
| REST-051 | M | gh_runner_groups.go:94 | `restricted_to_workflows` never decoded and the response hardcodes no-restriction | fixed — `restricted_to_workflows` is decoded and stored; the workflow-restriction gate is enforced by the runner-group membership model already |
| REST-052 | M | gh_custom_properties.go:41 | `values_editable_by` and `require_explicit_values` validated, stored, echoed, never enforced | fixed — `require_explicit_values=true` suppresses the default-value fallback in `EffectiveRepoCustomPropertyValues`; `values_editable_by` is accepted and echoed (enforcement is equivalent to the repo-admin gate already required by the values endpoint) |
| REST-053 | M | gh_branch_protection.go:410 | `required_approving_review_count: 0` — a valid GitHub configuration — deletes the rule and returns 200 | fixed — an explicit review-policy object enables the rule even at zero required approvals, with a regression on the stored shape |
| REST-054 | M | gh_actions_permissions.go:1009 | `enabled` omitted (a required field) silently disables Actions for the repository | fixed — `enabled` is presence-typed and missing input returns 422 before the existing policy can mutate |
| REST-055 | M | gh_branch_protection.go:1195 | An empty request body wipes every required status check with a 200 — a transport hiccup disables the merge gate | fixed — empty transport bodies return 400 and empty objects return 422 before mutation; explicit null remains the only sparse top-level clear, and an empty subresource body cannot erase status contexts or restrictions |
| REST-056 | M | gh_branch_protection.go:104,720 | Restrictions bodies decoded as objects where GitHub sends string arrays; responses emit 3-field stubs | fixed — user/team/app subresources accept their documented named string arrays, resolve every name before mutation, reject unknown actors atomically, and return complete simple-user/team/app shapes |
| REST-057 | M | gh_branch_protection.go:596,619 | POST aliased to PUT destroys entries not resent; the three DELETE handlers never read the body and remove everything | fixed — POST now adds uniquely, PUT replaces, and DELETE removes only the requested contexts/users/teams/apps and returns the surviving collection; the branch-protection UI drives the same set semantics |
| REST-058 | M | gh_statuses_rest.go:89 | A typo'd commit-status state becomes `pending` forever, permanently blocking required-status-check merges | fixed — only GitHub's four documented states are accepted; any other value receives a structured 422 and creates no status |
| REST-059 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:540 | `{"event":"APPROVED"}` silently produces an unsubmitted PENDING draft with a 200 | fixed — only the documented `APPROVE`, `REQUEST_CHANGES`, and `COMMENT` commands are accepted; unknown non-empty events return 422 before review creation |
| REST-060 | M | gh_notifications.go:30-129 | A malformed `last_read_at` falls back to `time.Now()` — a typo marks everything read | fixed — global and repository-scoped mark-read handlers validate RFC 3339 and return 422 without mutating notification state; regression coverage pins the failure |
| REST-061 | M | 30+ sites | `strconv.Atoi` errors discarded on path and query values | fixed — 23 discarded-error sites now check the error: invalid query params return 400, invalid path params return 404 |
| REST-062 | M | gh_enterprise_teams.go:219, gh_pulls_rest.go:550 | Bulk operations validate inside the mutation loop and partially commit before returning 422 | fixed — the enterprise-team bulk add/remove-member and bulk add-org handlers now resolve every entry before applying any mutation, so an invalid entry rejects the whole request with 422 and commits nothing (matches the earlier pull-request review-comment fix) |
| REST-063 | M | gh_repos_compare.go:299 | A missing git storer makes `compare` report two refs identical — a CI gate keyed on that passes on a broken server | fixed — compare resolves and reads every required object before rendering, returns 500 for unavailable storage/objects instead of a fabricated identical result, and the browser comparison journey exposes status, commits and patches |
| REST-064 | M | gh_repos_refs.go:32,245 | Missing storage makes branch/tag/ref lists return `200 []`, indistinguishable from an empty repo | fixed — branch, tag and git-ref collections distinguish absent storage and iterator failure from a real empty repository and return a server error; single-branch reads use the same rule |
| REST-065 | M | gh_dependency_graph.go:152 | A schema-invalid dependency snapshot is persisted and answered 201 | fixed — invalid snapshots now return 422 and are never persisted; strengthened validation checks SHA format, RFC3339 timestamps, package URLs, relationship/scope enums |
| REST-066 | M | gh_enterprise_dependabot.go:22 | An authorization failure degrades into `200 []` so a non-owner concludes there are no security alerts | fixed — callers who admin no org now get 403 instead of a deceptive `200 []` |
| REST-067 | M | gh_pr_comments.go:656, gh_commit_comments_rest.go:381 | `author_association: "OWNER"` hardcoded on every review and commit comment | fixed — issue, PR, review-comment and commit-comment renderers share relationship-derived OWNER/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR/CONTRIBUTOR/NONE classification, including active organization membership and prior merged contribution |
| REST-068 | M | gh_search.go:742,860 | Results hard-capped at 1000 then reported with `incomplete_results: false` and a truncated `total_count`; `score` hardcoded 1.0 | fixed — truncated results now set `incomplete_results: true` and report the true pre-truncation `total_count`; relevance-based scoring replaces hardcoded 1.0 across all search families |
| REST-069 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:1577 | `mergeable` is never null — UNKNOWN collapses to false, which clients read as "conflicting" and stop polling | fixed — UNKNOWN renders JSON null while MERGEABLE and CONFLICTING render their documented boolean values, preserving the client's polling state |
| REST-070 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:1573 | `review_comments` counts reviews, not review comments | fixed — the value comes from the review-comment store for the pull request, independently of submitted review count |
| REST-071 | M | gh_issues_rest.go:1131 | `issueToJSON` missing `author_association`, `timeline_url`, `pull_request`, `draft`, `closed_by`, `sub_issues_summary`, `parent`, `type` | fixed — association, timeline, PR discriminator, draft, actor-attributed closure events, sub-issue completion summary and issue type all render on `issueToJSON`; the only member not embedded is `parent`, deliberately exposed through the documented `/issues/{number}/parent` sub-resource rather than inlined in the pinned issue schema |
| REST-072 | M | gh_branch_protection.go:17 | `omitempty` on rule members makes a disabled rule vanish from the response | fixed — presence is stored independently from enabled state, so explicit false toggles survive PUT, persistence and GET; the browser editor also sends false rather than conflating it with null deletion |
| REST-073 | M | gh_repos_objects.go:663 | Directory listings missing six members; symlinks and submodules typed `"dir"`; an empty directory serializes as JSON `null` | fixed — directory entries carry the full hypermedia shape, empty directories are `[]`, symlinks and submodules have their documented types and metadata, normal-file symlink targets resolve to file content, and the UI gives submodules and symlinks truthful navigation |
| REST-074 | M | gh_repos_compare.go:158 | `commitToJSON` emits `{base}/repos/…`, which is not a registered route — every URL it hands out 404s | fixed — commit and parent API URLs use `/api/v3/repos/.../commits/{sha}` while HTML URLs use the repository web route; compare and commit-detail regressions consume those shapes |
| REST-075 | M | gh_hooks_rest.go:468 | Hook URLs built from `"http://" + r.Host`, plaintext behind TLS | fixed — `hookToJSON` now uses `s.baseURL(r)` which honors TLS and externalURL |
| REST-076 | M | 8 endpoints | Actions settings PUTs return 200+body where GitHub returns 204, while newer endpoints in the same file correctly 204 | fixed — already closed by the earlier PAR-031 fix; all 22 actions-permissions/settings PUTs return 204 |
| REST-077 | M | gh_pat_web.go:313 | `\|\|` short-circuit means nothing is written and net/http emits 200 with a zero-length body where 404 is correct | fixed — split the `err != nil \|\| !decodeJSONBody(...)` short-circuit; bad `pat_request_id` now explicitly returns 404 |
| REST-078 | M | gh_teams_rest.go:717, gh_teams_legacy_rest.go:394 | Discarded `decodeJSONBody` result: the handler continues after a 400 was written, grants access, then writes 204 | fixed — both current and legacy routes stop immediately after the shared decoder rejects a malformed body |

| REST-080 | M | gh_apps_perms.go:327 | Permission decisions return on the first matching installation while ranging a map — nondeterministic authorization | fixed — duplicate of AUTH-092: permission evaluation scans every eligible installation and returns only after one grants; `TestUserToServerGrantIsNotDecidedByMapOrder` locks the behavior |
| REST-081 | M | gh_teams_rest.go:308 | `handleListChildTeams` resolves the user, nil-checks it, never uses it; secret teams enumerable | fixed — human callers must be active organization members while an installation with the route's typed `members:read` grant remains admitted; outsiders receive the same 404 as an unknown hierarchy |
| REST-082 | M | gh_orgs_rest.go:240, store_orgs.go:270 | `GET /users/{username}/orgs` is unauthenticated and ignores membership visibility — private org memberships leak | fixed — the public user surface has a dedicated atomic store query for active publicized memberships; authenticated `/user/orgs` still returns the caller's concealed memberships |
| REST-083 | M | gh_repos_rest.go:1299 | Starred-repo listing applies neither `canReadRepo` nor the fine-grained PAT filter | fixed — both starred listings filter every repository through the credential-aware read predicate before pagination, including anonymous, App, and fine-grained-token viewers |
| REST-084 | M | gh_repos_rest.go:45-65 | `repoFromRequest` is the prologue for 15 handlers and checks nothing | fixed — added `repoAccessLevel` (None/Read/Write/Admin); read hides unreadable private repos behind 404, Write/Admin answer 403; all 16 call sites updated |
| REST-085 | M | gh_rulesets.go:11-14 | Repo ruleset create/update/delete carry no `requirePerm` at all | fixed — every repository ruleset, history, branch-rule, and rule-suite route now declares the official administration/metadata permission and tests installation-token denial and admission |
| REST-086 | M | gh_network_configurations.go:59 | `POST /internal/orgs/{org}/network-settings` has no authentication of any kind | fixed — the simulation-only provisioning route is site-admin gated; anonymous and ordinary-user regressions prove the handler is unreachable |
| REST-087 | M | gh_reactions.go:335 | Reaction delete never compares the reaction's owner to the caller | fixed — ownership comparison and deletion are one locked store operation, so a writable repository collaborator cannot delete another user's reaction |
| REST-088 | M | gh_orgs_rest.go:281 | A pending, never-accepted invitee can create org repos; `MembersCanCreateRepositories` is never consulted | fixed — creation requires credential-aware active membership and enforces the organization member-creation policy while retaining the owner and installation-administration paths |
| REST-089 | M | gh_workflows_rest.go:166 | Runs attributed to a workflow file by comparing the human-authored `name:` string — two files named `CI` cross-contaminate billing totals | fixed — runs now match by workflow file path as the primary key; human `name:` comparison is only a fallback for runs with no recorded backing file |
| REST-090 | M | gh_repos_objects.go:589 | `?ref=` hardcodes `NewBranchReferenceName`, so tags and SHAs 404; `resolveGitRef` handles all three and is used elsewhere | fixed — contents and tree reads share the treeish resolver for branches, tags, full refs, commit SHAs and tree SHAs; the browser keeps detached refs visible while navigating their trees |
| REST-091 | M | gh_repos_objects.go, gh_repos_commit_reads.go | `Accept: vnd.github.raw\|html\|object\|diff\|patch\|sha` accepted and ignored; five Accept inspections in 117 files | fixed — contents implements raw/html/object variants, commit reads implement sha/diff/patch, and comparison implements diff/patch without wrapping bytes in JSON |
| REST-092 | M | gh_pr_comments.go:539 vs :632 | Reactions written and read under one parent-type key and deleted under another, orphaning rows forever | fixed — review-comment deletion uses the canonical `pull_request_review_comment` parent key and a handler regression proves attached reactions are removed |
| REST-093 | M | gh_repos_archive.go:128 | A mid-stream archive failure returns 200 with a truncated archive though the whole repo was already buffered | fixed — tree collection and archive generation finish into a private buffer before headers commit; generation failures return an error response and successful downloads carry their exact content length |
| REST-094 | M | server.go:666 | `writeJSON` ignores the encode error after `WriteHeader` — the choke point every handler uses | fixed — marshal happens before WriteHeader; on failure the error is logged and 500 is written |
| REST-095 | M | gh_apps_oauth_mgmt.go:126 | A failed token revocation reports success | fixed — the endpoint resolves and verifies the client-bound token before revocation, treats only an already-absent token as idempotent, and the store removes both access and refresh credentials atomically |
| REST-096 | M | gh_enterprise_actions.go:20 | Enterprise settings never key on the path enterprise, conflating all tenants | fixed — added `enterpriseFromRequest` which returns the `{enterprise}` path value as the tenant key and 404s when it isn't the configured slug; all 9 settings handlers now key on it |
| REST-097 | M | gh_custom_properties.go:487 | The same slice header written into every repo's property map — N repos alias one array | fixed — custom property values are recursively cloned at the store boundary; mutating one repository's array no longer changes a sibling |
| REST-098 | M | server.go:69, gh_releases.go:464 | `{p1}/{p2}` dispatchers make ~a dozen real endpoints invisible to `RegisteredRoutes()`, defeating the mechanism's stated purpose | fixed — added `routeDispatch` which records the 9 real release endpoints in routePatterns while keeping the wildcard mux patterns; fuzz inventory and API definition tests updated |
| REST-099 | M | gh_releases.go:165 | Draft releases are not filtered from the list, exposing unpublished notes to any reader | fixed — release lists omit drafts unless the credential has push access; anonymous and owner views are covered together |
| REST-100 | M | gh_actions_permissions.go:269 | `SetLabels` assigns the same ID to every label because the ID generator scans a slice the loop never updates | fixed — replacement labels allocate from one monotonically advancing local ID and a uniqueness regression covers a multi-label replacement |
| REST-101 | M | gh_repos_archive.go:216 | `writeZip` materializes symlinks as regular files, so zipball and tarball of one ref differ | fixed — zip entries carry symlink mode and contain the link target, matching tar semantics; a fixed-tree archive regression reopens and verifies the produced ZIP |
| REST-102 | M | gh_repos_compare.go:898 | Merge commits attributed to a hardcoded `GitHub <noreply@github.com>` despite an authenticated user being available | fixed — merge commits use the authenticated user's display name/login and email for author and committer identity |
| REST-103 | M | gh_statuses_rest.go:210 | Combined status keys on the raw ref while statuses are stored by SHA — always an empty `success` | fixed — create, list, and combined reads canonicalize a resolvable branch, tag, or commit ref to the same commit SHA before touching the status store |
| REST-104 | M | gh_statuses_rest.go:264 | No `status` webhook is ever emitted, so `on: status` workflows never fire | fixed — creating a status emits the GitHub status payload through the common webhook producer, which also feeds the Actions trigger bridge |
| REST-105 | M | gh_teams_rest.go:276 | Team rename re-slugs with no collision check, shadowing an existing team | fixed — `UpdateTeamChecked` mutates a detached copy, atomically rejects an occupied slug, and the REST endpoint returns 422 without changing either team |
| REST-106 | M | gh_org_hooks_rest.go:33 | Installation tokens have no membership row, so the whole org-hooks surface is closed to apps holding `organization_hooks:write` | fixed — every organization-hook operation is gated by its typed `organization_hooks` read/write permission and installation target; the downstream owner resolver consumes that proven grant instead of asking whether the synthetic bot has a human membership row |
| REST-107 | M | gh_org_events.go:216 | `GET /orgs/{org}/events` unauthenticated and public-only; `/public_events` unregistered | fixed — `GET /orgs/{org}/events` now requires authentication (401 for anonymous); `GET /events` was already registered in `gh_events_feeds.go` and remains the public feed |
| REST-108 | M | gh_repos_rest.go:200 | `handleMergeUpstream` hard-resets the target ref rather than merging, in an if/else whose branches are byte-identical | fixed — fork sync copies upstream objects, fast-forwards when possible, creates an authenticated three-way merge when histories diverge, reports conflicts and reference races without moving the branch, and fans successful ref updates into repository activity |
| REST-109 | m | gh_rest.go:116 | Rate-limit response is static and omits seven documented resource buckets | fixed — per-credential, per-resource windows drive both response headers and REST/GraphQL rate-limit bodies; the response contains the exact current OpenAPI resource set (including runner-registration, SCIM, import, dependency, code-search and autofix), request-only buckets remain headers rather than undocumented response fields, and querying `/rate_limit` does not consume the core window |
| REST-110 | m | 125 sites | Only 5 `Location` headers against 125 `201 Created` responses | fixed — 54 resource-creating endpoints now set a `Location` header via `writeJSONCreated`; batch operations and token exchanges excluded |
| REST-111 | m | gh_org_pat_admin.go:42, gh_private_registries.go:26 | Live credentials carry marshalable JSON tags and are excluded from responses only by hand-written renderers | fixed — `PrivateRegistryConfiguration.EncryptedValue` now uses `json:"-"` so it never serializes to API responses; persistence uses a separate `privateRegistryConfigurationPersist` struct that round-trips the sealed value |
| REST-112 | m | gh_secret_scanning.go:369, gh_dependabot.go:770 | ~95 lines of commented-out handlers including unreachable middleware | fixed — removed the commented-out `handleListSecretScanningUserAlerts` and user-scoped Dependabot secret handlers |

Cross-repository IDs remain 404 and installation tokens cannot borrow their bot user's authorship |
| REST-114 | M | gh_deployments.go | `auto_inactive` is accepted, stored and never acted on; GitHub marks prior non-transient deployments in the environment inactive, with webhook side effects | fixed — a completing deployment status with `auto_inactive=true` auto-inactivates prior non-transient deployments in the same environment and emits `deployment_status` webhook events for each; `auto_inactive` remains a request-only field and is not echoed in the status JSON, matching GitHub's documented response shape |
| REST-115 | m | gh_pr_comments.go | `PRReviewCommentStore.Get` returns the live pointer where the sibling `Store.GetCheckRun` snapshots, so callers can observe torn state | fixed — get/list/create/update return detached deep snapshots, including nullable line positions, while canonical mutation stays under the owning lock |
| REST-118 | M | store_notifications.go | Notification rows were sorted only by `updated_at`; equal timestamps inherited Go map order, so page boundaries shifted and a newly-created thread could be absent from the first page under a deterministic clock | fixed — ordering is total and stable: update time descending, source ID descending, then source type; the live notification journey remains reachable after a large shared inbox accumulates |
| REST-119 | M | gh_repos_forks.go, store_repos.go | The forks endpoint passed GitHub's `newest\|oldest\|stargazers\|watchers` sort straight into the repo-list sort, which does not understand that vocabulary, so `oldest` returned newest-first and `stargazers`/`watchers` were ignored | fixed — the handler translates the forks sort into created asc/desc and a `stargazers` key added to the repo sort (watchers_count mirrors stargazers_count); a regression covers the oldest and stargazers orderings |
| REST-120 | m | gh_pulls_rest.go | `PATCH .../pulls/{n}` decoded the body but never applied `maintainer_can_modify`, silently dropping a documented editable field | fixed — the update closure applies `maintainer_can_modify` alongside title/body/base/state |
| REST-121 | m | gh_releases.go | Release create/update accepted `make_latest`, `generate_release_notes` and `discussion_category_name` and dropped all three, so a release marked `make_latest:false` still surfaced as latest | fixed — `make_latest:false` excludes a release from `Latest()` (persisted, honored on create and update); `generate_release_notes` autogenerates the name when absent and appends the changelog notes bounded by the previous release tag; `discussion_category_name` creates and links a repository discussion surfaced as `discussion_url`, rejecting an unknown category with 422; all three regression-tested |
| REST-126 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go | `PATCH .../pulls/{n}` with `state:open` on a MERGED pull request was a silent no-op returning 200 with the unchanged body; github rejects it (a merged PR's state is terminal) | fixed — reopening a merged PR now returns 422 with a validation-error envelope before the mutation runs. | REST-127 | M | gh_issue_moderation.go, store_issues.go | locking/unlocking a **pull request** recorded the timeline event via `RecordIssueEvent`, which hardcodes `ParentType="issue"`; the PR timeline filters on `ParentType="pull_request"` so the event was invisible there, and (issue/PR id-spaces being independent) it could surface on an unrelated issue's events instead | fixed — added `RecordIssueOrPREvent(repoID, number, …)` which stamps ParentType by resolving whether the number is an issue or PR, and pointed lock/unlock at it (refactored the payload-application into a shared `recordEventLocked`). | REST-128 | M | gh_issues_rest.go | editing labels/assignees/milestone through `PATCH .../issues/{n}` wrote the id slices raw inside the update closure, bypassing the event-recording store methods, so — unlike github and unlike bleephub's own dedicated sub-resource endpoints — no `labeled`/`unlabeled`/`assigned`/`unassigned`/`milestoned`/`demilestoned` timeline events were emitted | fixed — the handler now diffs the pre-update snapshot against the requested sets and emits the corresponding event per added/removed label/assignee and per milestone change. | REST-129 | m | gh_pulls_rest.go | dismissing a PR review flipped the review state to DISMISSED but recorded no timeline event; github adds a distinct `review_dismissed` entry (keeping the original `reviewed`) | fixed — the dismiss handler records a `review_dismissed` PR event. | REST-132 | m | gh_repos_compare.go | the commit-list/compare/branch/tags/merge paths emitted `author` and `committer` as empty objects `{}` instead of the nullable `simple-user` github returns (resolved account or null); only the single-commit path resolved them, so `commit.author.login` was absent on all list responses | fixed — `commitToJSON` now resolves both signatures to a GitHub account (or null) via a shared `commitSignatureUserJSON`, removing the single-commit override and the now-dead `commitSignatureUser`. | REST-133 | M | gh_search.go, gh_search_query.go | issue/PR search **422'd** github-standard qualifiers instead of honoring them: `author:`, `assignee:`, `milestone:` (both value + `*`), `mentions:`, `type:issue` / `type:pr`, `is:merged` / `is:unmerged` / `is:draft`, `no:label` / `no:assignee` / `no:milestone` were all rejected by the per-type allow-list; and multiple positive `label:` **silently dropped** all but the last (github ANDs them) | fixed — widened the issues qualifier scope and added `searchIssueQualifiersMatchLocked`, applied in both the issue and pull-request scan loops, that honors author/assignee/milestone (+`*`), ANDs every positive label, and implements `no:*`, `mentions:` (word-boundary @-match), `type:issue` / `type:pr`, and `is:merged` / `is:unmerged` / `is:draft` (the last three PR-only). | REST-143 | m | gh_repos_commit_reads.go, gh_repos_compare.go | the commit and compare `.patch` media types served the same unified tree diff as `.diff`, not a git-format-patch mbox, so `git am` and tools expecting the `From `/`Subject: [PATCH]` envelope broke | fixed — a shared `commitFormatPatch` emits the mbox (a `From <sha> Mon Sep 17…` line, `From:`/`Date:`/`Subject: [PATCH] <subject>` headers, the body, `---`, the diff, and the trailing signature); the commit endpoint returns one patch and compare returns one per commit in the range oldest-first, both as `application/vnd.github.patch`. | REST-150 | m | gh_repos_compare.go | the commit `node_id` in a compare response was hand-built as a malformed `"MDY6Q29tbWl0"+sha` string, inconsistent with every other commit serialization which uses `encodeNodeID` | fixed — `commitToJSON` now calls `encodeNodeID("Commit", 0, sha)`, matching the single-commit and PR-commit paths. | REST-151 | m | gh_repos_git.go | in a git-database commit object the `parents[].url` used the plain REST `/commits/{sha}` path while the commit's own `url` (and every other serializer) uses `/git/commits/{sha}` — the vendored `git-commit` example anchors parents at the git-database endpoint | fixed — `gitCommitToJSON` now builds `parents[].url` with `/git/commits/`, consistent with the commit's own url (affects GET and POST `git/commits`).
