| REST-153 | m | gh_pulls_rest.go | `POST .../pulls/{n}/reviews` accepted an empty `body` for a COMMENT or REQUEST_CHANGES event (github 422s — body is required for those events), and its 422s used the object `validation-error` shape though this operation documents `validation-error-simple` (errors as strings) | fixed — the handler 422s a missing body for COMMENT/REQUEST_CHANGES, and all three 422s on this operation (event/comments/body) now emit the simple string-errors shape the spec + shape ratchet require. | REST-154 | m | gh_pulls_rest.go | `PUT .../reviews/{id}/dismissals` accepted an empty/missing `message` though the vendored spec marks it required | fixed — the handler 422s an empty message (validation-error-simple shape). | REST-155 | m | gh_pr_comments.go | pull-request review comments omitted github's `subject_type` (`line`/`file`) field | fixed — the serializer now emits `subject_type` (`file` when the comment has no line/position, else `line`). Named remainder: accepting `subject_type=file` on the CREATE endpoint (a genuine file-level comment) needs a stored subject-type + relaxed line validation — a follow-up |
| REST-156 | m | gh_releases.go, internal/store/releases.go | the release-asset object omitted the required `digest` field (a `sha256:<hex>` of the uploaded bytes) — uncaught because asset endpoints are only exercised through the unobserved test server | fixed — `CreateReleaseAsset` computes and stores the sha256 digest and `releaseAssetToJSON` emits it. Verified: unit test asserts the digest of the uploaded bytes |
| REST-157 | m | gh_releases.go, internal/store/releases.go | uploading a second release asset with an existing name silently created a duplicate (github 422s) | fixed — `CreateReleaseAsset` returns a sentinel that the upload handler maps to a 422 name-already-exists validation error. Verified: unit test asserts a duplicate-name upload is 422'd |
| REST-158 | M | gh_middleware.go, rate_limits.go | a conditional GET that resulted in `304 Not Modified` still consumed a primary-rate-limit unit and reported the consumed count in its `X-RateLimit-*` headers — github documents that a 304 is not billed, and bleephub advertises `X-Poll-Interval` to the very pollers this penalizes | fixed — a refund seam on the response writer hands the unit back when a 304 is emitted (both the ETag and Last-Modified paths) and recomputes the headers; the consume/refund share one window-key helper (dupl-clean). Verified: unit test asserts a 304 re-request leaves `X-RateLimit-Remaining` unchanged |
| REST-159 | m | gh_pagination.go | the `Link` rel URLs always injected `per_page` (materializing the default) even when the client never sent it — github only carries `per_page` into the Link targets when the client supplied it | fixed — `buildLinkHeader` echoes the resolved `per_page` only when the request carried one. Verified: unit test asserts per_page appears when sent and is absent otherwise |
| REST-160 | M | gh_codeowners.go, gh_pulls_rest.go, gh_branch_protection.go, gh_repos_reads.go | CODEOWNERS are parsed and validated but never auto-requested as reviewers when a PR is opened, and the branch-protection `require_code_owner_reviews` flag is stored but not enforced at merge — github does both | fixed — `gh_codeowners.go` adds the changed-path matcher (github's gitignore-shaped syntax: last match wins, an owner-less pattern clears, no `!` negation, root-anchored vs any-depth patterns, and a wildcard-terminated pattern owning no subtree) and drives both halves. Owners of the changed paths are requested on PR open (REST and the GraphQL mutation, through a new `Pulls` seam) and again on every head advance via `firePullRequestSynchronize`, skipping the author, already-requested and already-reviewed owners, and owners without read access; `@org/team` owners are requested as team reviewers. `canMergePullRequest` — the one gate REST merge, GraphQL merge and auto-merge share — refuses until every matched rule holds an approving review, read through the same dismissal-aware `latestGateReviewStatesLocked` the other review rules use, and `mergeable_state` reports `blocked` for it as it does for an unmet required check. Verified: matcher table test per documented pattern, last-match/clearing test, auto-request on open and head advance, team owner, merge refusal → owner approval → merge, and a repo with no CODEOWNERS file left untouched |
| REST-161 | m | gh_repos_rest.go, internal/store/store_repos.go | `PUT /repos/{o}/{r}/subscription` decoded `ignored` but `SetRepoSubscription` hardcoded `Ignored:false`, so repo-watch "ignore" was non-functional (`{"ignored":true}` returned `ignored:false`), and the notification watch-suppression branch reading it was dead | fixed — `SetRepoSubscription` takes and persists `ignored`; the handler passes it through. Verified: unit test asserts `{"ignored":true}` round-trips as `ignored:true` |
| REST-162 | m | gh_notifications.go | `PUT /notifications/threads/{id}/subscription` derived `subscribed` from a non-documented `subscribed` request field, so a spec-shaped `{"ignored":false}` (or `{}`) request returned `subscribed:false` | fixed — subscribing is implied by the PUT, so `subscribed` is `!ignored`. Verified: unit test asserts an unignored PUT yields `subscribed:true` and an ignored one `subscribed:false` |
| REST-163 | m | gh_misc_endpoints.go, git_ssh.go | `GET /meta` returned only `verifiable_password_authentication`+`installed_version`, omitting `ssh_key_fingerprints` and `ssh_keys` clients read to seed known_hosts | fixed — `/meta` now emits `ssh_key_fingerprints` (the instance's configured SSH host-key SHA256 fingerprint map) and `ssh_keys` (the authorized-key lines), empty when no host key is configured; both are api-overview schema members (ratchet-safe). Verified: unit test asserts both are present |
| REST-164 | m | gh_repos_templates.go, internal/store/repos_templates.go | `GET /licenses/{key}` emitted empty `permissions`/`conditions`/`limitations`, the license *name* as `description`, an empty `implementation`, and `featured:true` unconditionally | fixed — a per-license `LicenseMetadata` table supplies the real choosealicense slug arrays, description, implementation and featured flag. Verified: unit test asserts mit's permissions start `commercial-use`, description is populated, and featured is true |
| REST-165 | m | gh_repos_templates.go | `GET /licenses` ignored the documented `featured` query param, always returning the full catalog | fixed — the default (or `?featured=true`) returns only featured licenses; `?featured=false` returns all. Verified: unit test asserts the default excludes bsd-2-clause and `?featured=false` includes it |
| REST-166 | M | gh_gists_rest.go | a gist `PATCH` that renamed a file (setting `filename` without `content`) left the OLD file behind AND blanked the renamed file's body — `newFiles` was keyed by the new name and the old key was never deleted, and omitted content was written as `""` | fixed — a rename now deletes the old key, and omitted content is carried forward from the current gist. Verified: unit test asserts a pure rename removes the old filename and preserves the body |
| REST-167 | M | gh_misc_endpoints_users.go | `GET /users/{username}/gists` returned the user's **secret** gists as well as public ones — a visibility leak (the endpoint is public-only; secret gists are reachable only via `GET /gists` as the owner) | fixed — the handler now emits only public gists. Verified: unit test asserts a public gist appears and a secret one does not |
| REST-168 | m | internal/store/store.go | `GET /gists/{id}/{sha}` returned the gist's *current* files with only `updated_at` overridden, not the files as they existed at that revision | fixed — each history entry now records a file snapshot (at create and every update), and `GetGistAtRevision` reconstructs from it (revisions predating snapshots fall back to current files). Verified: unit test edits a gist then asserts the first revision still serves the original content |
| REST-169 | m | gh_gists_rest.go | gist responses hardcoded `forks:[]`, never emitted `fork_of` for a fork, and omitted the per-file `truncated` member | fixed — `forks` is populated from the store (entries carry a full public-user), `fork_of` (the parent) is emitted on the single-gist responses, and per-file `truncated` is emitted — all three scoped to the gist-simple responses so the base-gist list stays schema-clean. Verified: unit tests assert a fork's `fork_of`, the parent's `forks` entry, and that `truncated` appears on the single-gist GET but not the list |
| REST-170 | m | gh_misc_endpoints.go, internal/store/misc.go | the GPG-key JSON used the non-spec field `can_encrypt_commits` (github spells it `can_encrypt_comms`) and omitted required `primary_key_id`, `raw_key`, `can_encrypt_storage`, `subkeys`, `revoked`; `emails` items also carried a non-spec `primary` | fixed — the serializer emits github's full gpg-key required set with the correct names, and `emails` items are trimmed to `{email,verified}`; the store struct gained the backing fields. Verified: unit test asserts `can_encrypt_comms`/the added fields are present and `can_encrypt_commits` is gone |
| REST-171 | M | gh_misc_endpoints.go | `GET /user/gpg_keys/{id}` skipped auth and ownership checks, so any caller (even anonymous) could read any user's GPG key object — unlike the correct sibling SSH-key handler | fixed — the handler now requires a caller (401) and 404s a key that is not theirs. Verified: unit test asserts anonymous access is 401 and another user's access is 404 |
| REST-172 | m | gh_members_rest.go | `GET /orgs/{org}/members` ignored the documented `?role` (all/admin/member) and `?filter` (all/2fa_disabled) query params, returning the full list regardless | fixed — the handler validates both enums (422 on a bad value) and filters by membership role. Verified: unit test asserts `?role=admin`/`?role=member` narrow correctly and `?role=bogus` is 422'd |
| REST-173 | m | gh_teams_rest.go | `GET /orgs/{org}/teams/{slug}/members` ignored the documented `?role` (member/maintainer/all) query param | fixed — the handler validates the enum and filters by the member's team role. Verified: unit test asserts `?role=maintainer` returns only the maintainer |
| REST-174 | m | webhooks.go, gh_hooks_rest.go | webhook delivery was https-only (a deliberate deviation: github.com permits `http://` webhook endpoints), enforced by `webhookTargetRequiresHTTPS` at config and every delivery | fixed — removed the https-only rule and its call sites; a hook target now only has to be http or https (`parseWebhookTargetURL`) and pass the always-on SSRF address gate, matching github. The `insecure_ssl` TLS-verify opt-out and the address/scheme gates are unchanged. Verified: unit test delivers to a plain-http loopback receiver; the e2e webhook harness now runs the receiver over http with no TLS/cert machinery |
| REST-175 | M | gh_code_scanning.go | `DELETE /repos/{o}/{r}/code-scanning/analyses/{id}` returned 204 empty, but GitHub answers 200 with the `code-scanning-analysis-deletion` body (`next_analysis_url`, `confirm_delete_url`) that drives the delete-chain | fixed — the handler fetches the analysis, deletes it, then returns 200 with both members computed from the remaining analyses in the (ref, tool) set: `confirm_delete_url` targets the next deletable analysis with `?confirm_delete`, `next_analysis_url` is non-null only while more than the final analysis remains, and both are null once the set is empty. Verified: tests for the empty (both null) and chained (next non-null) cases |
| REST-176 | m | gh_statuses_rest.go | the combined commit status omitted the `url` and `commit_url` members that `combined-commit-status` marks required | fixed — both are emitted, pointing at the combined-status resource and the commit. Verified: unit test asserts both URL suffixes |
| REST-177 | m | gh_misc_endpoints.go | the repository OIDC subject-claim GET omitted the required `use_default` (`oidc-custom-sub-repo`); the org route (`oidc-custom-sub`) has no such member | fixed — the repo variant returns `use_default` (true when the repo carries no custom claim keys), added only for the repo route so the org shape is unchanged. Verified: the OIDC test asserts `use_default` tracks the presence of claim keys |
| REST-178 | m | gh_orgs_rest.go | `DELETE /orgs/{org}` returned 204, but GitHub deletes an organization asynchronously and answers 202 Accepted | fixed — returns 202. Verified: both org-delete tests updated to expect 202 |
| REST-179 | M | gh_search.go, gh_search_query.go | issue and pull-request search 422'd routine documented qualifiers (`created:`, `updated:`, `closed:`, `draft:`, `archived:`) because they were absent from the issues scope | fixed — added them to the issues scope with parse-time validation and per-item matching (dates via the existing date-constraint evaluator, draft against the PR draft flag, archived against the owning repo). Verified: a test seeds issues at different dates and asserts `created:>`, `created:<`, `draft:true` and `archived:false` filter correctly |
| REST-180 | m | gh_search.go, gh_search_query.go | commit search rejected `committer:`/`author-name:`/`author-email:`/`committer-name:`/`committer-email:`/`author-date:`/`committer-date:`/`merge:`, and issue search rejected the interaction qualifiers (`involves:`, `commenter:`, `interactions:`, `reactions:`, `review:`, `linked:`, `head:`, `base:`) and `in:comments` | fixed — every commit- and issue-search qualifier GitHub documents for this surface now parses and filters: commit-search identity/date/merge qualifiers against the git fields (r28); issue-search `sort=reactions`/`interactions` by the reaction-rollup (r30); `involves:`/`commenter:`/`reactions:N`/`interactions:N`/`head:`/`base:` via a post-lock pass over comment authors + reaction counts (r31); `in:comments` restricting the term match to comment bodies via an under-lock comment-body index (r32); `review:`/`reviewed-by:`/`review-requested:` over review + requested-reviewer data (r33); and `linked:pr`/`linked:issue` over an issue↔PR closing-reference relationship derived from PR-body keywords (Closes/Fixes/Resolves #N) in the post-lock pass (r35). Each landed with a test |
| REST-181 | m | rate_limits.go | the unauthenticated Search rate limit was the authenticated 30/min; GitHub caps anonymous Search at 10/min | fixed — the anonymous downgrade now covers `search` (and `code_search`, already 10), not just core. Verified: unit test asserts anonymous search is 10 and authenticated is 30 |
| REST-182 | m | gh_repos_objects.go | the git tree response was always `truncated:false` with no size ceiling; GitHub caps a tree at 100,000 entries and sets `truncated:true` when exceeded | fixed — the handler caps entries at 100,000 and reports `truncated` accordingly, stopping the walk once the cap is reached |
| REST-183 | m | gh_pr_comments.go | the create-review-comment handler decoded `start_line` but never validated it precedes `line`; GitHub rejects an inverted multi-line range with 422 | fixed — a `start_line` at or above `line` now returns a validation-error 422 instead of storing an inverted range. Verified: a test asserts start_line==line and start_line>line 422 while a valid start_line<line is accepted |
| REST-184 | m | gh_search.go | pull-request search items omitted the `reactions` reaction-rollup that `issue-search-result-item` carries (issue items already had it via issueToJSON), and `sort=reactions`/`sort=interactions` were silently ignored (best-match order) | fixed — PR search items now emit the reaction-rollup (total_count + per-content counts, url at the issues reactions path), and both sort keys order by the rollup total (interactions = reactions + comments) via the render-all path. Verified: a test seeds reactions and asserts the rollup total plus the reactions/interactions ordering |
| REST-185 | M | gh_releases.go | a draft release was served to any repository reader by id or by tag, and its assets with it, though the list endpoint already hid drafts from callers without push access | fixed — one visibility predicate gates every by-id/by-tag release read plus the asset list and asset fetch, so the direct fetch can no longer be used to walk around the list filter. Verified: a non-pusher gets 404 on both shapes while the owner still sees the draft |
| REST-186 | m | rate_limits.go | the rate-limit resource classifier matched bare substrings against user-controlled path segments, so every request to a repository named `important` was billed to the 100-per-hour `source_import` budget and 403'd there | fixed — the special-resource paths match on whole segments, and the two root-scoped families (`scim`, `app-manifests`) match by prefix. Verified: colliding repository names classify as `core` while every real endpoint keeps its budget |
| REST-187 | M | gh_projects_v2_rest.go | `serveProjectV2UpdateItem` rendered its 200 from the item it read before applying the field writes, so a PATCH answered with the values the item held beforehand | fixed — the item is re-read after the writes and the response renders the updated row. The defect was latent while the store returned live rows and became observable when STORE-080 detached them |
| REST-188 | M | gh_projects_v2_webhooks.go | the `projects_v2`, `projects_v2_item` and `projects_v2_status_update` webhook family was not emitted at all, so nothing outside the API could observe a project change | fixed — one renderer builds GitHub's payload shapes (`projects_v2` with its owner and creator, `projects_v2_item` naming its project and content by node id, `projects_v2_status_update`) and both the REST write paths and the GraphQL mutations deliver through it, so a hook subscriber cannot tell which surface made a change. Delivery is to the owning organization's hooks; `edited` actions carry GitHub's before/after `changes` diff. Verified: a receiver test asserts all three event names arrive with their objects, and a second asserts the REST create/delete pair emits the same actions |

## GQL — GraphQL

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GQL-001 | B | gh_graphql.go:138-143 | `POST /api/graphql` serves anonymous requests; real GitHub 401s every unauthenticated request | fixed |
| GQL-002 | B | gh_repos_graphql.go:693, store_repos.go:1249 | `user(login:).repositories` has no visibility filter and each leaked node is a live handle into issues, PRs, discussions and releases | fixed |
| GQL-003 | B | gh_repos_graphql.go:901 + 22 siblings | No mutation in the lane calls any permission helper — any authenticated user can delete any repository | fixed — the count was **25**, not 22, across 31 mutation fields. One policy table plus a `registerMutation` registrar that **panics at schema build** for a mutation with no row, no resolver or a repository level with no target lookup, and one `authorizeMutation` decision point; the duplicated per-resolver checks were deleted rather than left alongside. Every level is preceded by `viewerCanReadRepo`, and an unreadable or absent target returns the same not-found error so a mutation is not an existence oracle. Pre-fix, with the guard neutered, all 26 target-bearing mutations served a stranger — repository deleted, PR merged, discussion deleted |
| GQL-004 | B | gh_discussions_graphql.go:934,959 | Two discussion answer mutations skip authentication entirely | fixed — both answer mutations now authenticate and authorize through the registrar, at push level with the discussion author permitted |
| GQL-005 | B | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1682 | `expectedHeadOid` — the `--match-head-commit` safety interlock — accepted and ignored | fixed — a stale or unresolvable head refuses with the same message GitHub uses, matching the REST twin. Pre-fix a merge naming an all-zero oid returned `State: MERGED` |
| GQL-006 | B | gh_issues_graphql.go:1242 | `updateIssue` drops `labelIds`, `assigneeIds` and `milestoneId` and returns a success payload showing the unchanged issue | fixed — labels, assignees and milestone are applied; unresolvable or cross-repository ids are refused rather than dropped; triage requires push. Pre-fix the test saw empty labels, empty assignees, milestone 0 and a success payload |
| GQL-007 | B | gh_issues_graphql.go:1248 | `state` is an unvalidated string written straight into the store; `issueStateEnum` exists 700 lines above | fixed — `state` is typed `issueStateEnum`, matching real GitHub's `UpdateIssueInput`, with a value check behind it, and `applyIssueState` keeps `ClosedAt`/`StateReason` consistent — closing via `updateIssue` previously left `ClosedAt` nil. Pre-fix the store accepted `State: BANANA` |
| GQL-008 | M | 4 connections | `orderBy` accepted and ignored, breaking `gh issue list --sort`, `gh pr list` and `gh repo list --sort` | fixed — repository, issue, pull-request, discussion and label connections apply their declared field/direction with deterministic tie-breaking; release/repository input signatures share the official order enums |
| GQL-009 | M | gh_repos_graphql.go:684 | `ownerAffiliations` accepted and never read | fixed — OWNER, COLLABORATOR and ORGANIZATION_MEMBER select from the durable ownership, collaborator and membership graphs before visibility and Relay pagination |
| GQL-010 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:937 | `commits(last:1)` returns the *first* commit, so `gh` reads check status off the wrong commit on multi-commit PRs | fixed — commits resolves through the shared four-direction Relay window, so `last:1` selects the tail and supports before/after bounds |
| GQL-011 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:842-1153 | Five more PR sub-connections accept Relay arguments and return the whole list | fixed — labels, assignees, reviews, review requests, comments, review threads, commits, latest reviews and files all re-page their complete source through the shared Relay implementation |
| GQL-012 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1488 | `search(last:)` returns the first N; `before` undeclared; the non-null `type` argument never read | fixed — search declares and applies all four Relay bounds and honors the ISSUE search type |
| GQL-013 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:2931 | `sort:` silently dropped while every other unhandled qualifier sets `impossible` | fixed — created/updated ascending and descending orders are parsed and applied with stable ties; unsupported sorts honestly match nothing |
| GQL-014 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:2928 | `review-requested:` hardcoded impossible on a false claim — the data is read 300 lines away | fixed — the qualifier resolves requested users from the persisted reviewer graph |
| GQL-015 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:583 | Four of five `IssueFilters` members ignored, so `filterBy:{states:[CLOSED]}` returns open issues | fixed — assignee, creator, mentioned, labels and states are all behavioral, compose with top-level filters, and have compatibility tests |
| GQL-016 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:761, gh_discussions_graphql.go:537 | An unresolvable filter value widens the result set to everything | fixed — unresolved issue principals and discussion categories produce empty connections rather than removing the predicate |
| GQL-017 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1025 | Unknown node IDs silently dropped from label/assignee/milestone updates while `issueTypeId` in the same resolver errors | fixed — create/update resolve every supplied id strictly and reject missing or cross-repository objects before mutating |
| GQL-018 | M | gh_repos_graphql.go:1174 | `decodeCursor` returns 0 on any malformed cursor — clients loop forever or silently truncate | fixed — request validation rejects malformed cursors; the legacy total decoder saturates invalid input past the result set rather than returning page one |
| GQL-019 | M | gh_repos_graphql.go:1170 | Cursors are bare list indices with no connection or entity identity, so inserts shift every subsequent page | fixed — both GraphQL connection paginators now emit cursors carrying the node's stable identity (`cursor:<idx>:<id>`) and resolve `after`/`before` by locating that identity in the current list, falling back to the recorded index only when the node is gone, so inserting an item before a page boundary no longer shifts the next page: `buildConnectionWindow`/`paginateGQLMaps` (the ~18 map-node connections) and the generic `paginateGQL[T]` (issues, milestones, participants, releases) via a cheap per-item identity extractor. Legacy index cursors still decode; the REST/projects `encodeCursor` path is untouched. Regressions: TestGQLConnectionCursorsStableAcrossInserts, TestPaginateGQLCursorsStableAcrossInserts |
| GQL-020 | M | gh_pagination.go:106,134 | `first`+`last` silently ignores `last`; `first`+`before` silently ignores `before` | fixed — one four-bound window handles valid combinations and the document guard rejects simultaneous first+last |
| GQL-021 | M | gh_pagination.go:216 | Page-size bounds clamped where the comment itself says GitHub rejects | fixed — literal and variable page sizes outside 1…100 are rejected before execution |
| GQL-022 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1677 | Eager pre-pagination truncates at build time and then misreports `totalCount` | fixed — `issueFieldValuesConnectionLocked` was the sole offender: it built the full node list then called `paginateGQL(nodes, len(nodes), …)`, whose page size is clamped to 100, and the field resolver re-paginated that already-truncated slice via `repaginateConnection`, so an issue with >100 field values reported `totalCount` 100 and hid the remainder. It now returns the full node set with a truthful `totalCount` (matching its label/assignee/comment siblings) and lets the resolver apply the single correct window. Regression test asserts `totalCount==105` for a 105-value issue |
| GQL-023 | M | gh_discussions_graphql.go:1101, gh_releases.go:110 | Release and reaction mint identical global IDs | fixed — reactions mint a distinct `REA_` node-ID prefix in both the REST and GraphQL surfaces while releases keep GitHub's `RE_`, so a release and a reaction with the same numeric id no longer share a global object id |
| GQL-024 | M | 9 scanners | Node IDs carry the database ID in plaintext and nothing parses them — every mutation walks an entire map under the global lock | fixed — the two hottest node-id finders (`findRepoByNodeID`, `findUserByNodeID`), which resolve `repositoryId` and assignees on the issue/PR/discussion create-mutation hot path, now decode the database id embedded in the node id (`decodeNodeDBID`, mirroring the existing `parsePRReviewThreadNodeID` convention) and do an O(1) keyed lookup, with a node-id equality guard and a fallback scan so a foreign- or legacy-shaped id (e.g. the `U_bleephub_<login>` identifiers) still resolves exactly as before. Regression test covers the decoder and both finders. The remaining id-keyed finders — issues, labels, milestones, issue comments, pull requests, discussions/categories/comments, and the three projects-v2 lookups (project/item/field) — now adopt the same `decodeNodeDBID` fast path with the equality guard and fallback scan, preserving the discussions' `!Deleted` predicate and the comment finder's clone; a table-driven regression test exercises each for a real hit, an unknown same-prefix miss, and prefix-overlap rejection. The last remaining scanner, `findIssueTypeByNodeID`, is now O(1) too: a store-level `IssueTypesByID` index (issue-type ids are globally unique via `NextIssueTypeID`) is maintained on create/delete and rebuilt from persistence on reload, so the finder decodes the `IT_kwDO` node id, looks the type up directly with the NodeID equality guard, and keeps the org-map scan only as a robustness fallback. Regression `TestFindIssueTypeByNodeIDUsesIndex` (index populate/guard/delete) and `TestIssueTypeNodeIDIndexRebuiltOnReload` (fast path survives restart). Every node-id finder now parses the embedded id — no mutation walks a full map under the lock. |
| GQL-025 | M | gh_graphql.go:165 | No depth or complexity limit with four type cycles, reachable unauthenticated | fixed — the authenticated endpoint enforces bounded depth, expanded fragment field cost and Relay window size before resolver execution |
| GQL-026 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:3136 | `search(first:1)` fully renders every issue and PR in the instance before discarding all but one | fixed — the search resolver now matches and sorts on lightweight items (a stable node id + a lazy render closure) and defers the expensive `issueToGQL`/`pullRequestToGQL` rendering to `paginateGQLItems`, which renders only the nodes inside the chosen window. `paginateGQLMaps` was refactored to wrap the same lazy windowing so every connection shares one cursor implementation; a unit test proves a 100-item `first:1` window renders exactly one node with `totalCount` 100, and that the lazy and eager paths return identical windows/cursors |
| GQL-027 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1511 | Rendering one page of 100 issues walks the global comment map 100 times | fixed — comments are resolved through a maintained `CommentsByParent` index (mirroring `CommentCounts`), so `issueToGQL` and `ListComments` look up a parent's comments directly instead of scanning every comment in the store; rendering a page of N issues is no longer O(N·M). The index is maintained at create/delete/repo-cascade/reload and covered by a consistency+reload regression |
| GQL-028 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1630 | Thirteen unsynchronized package-level schema memos shared across server instances | fixed — every scalar, enum, interface and ProjectV2/PageInfo memo belongs to one Server schema registry; parallel independent schema builds are a regression test |
| GQL-029 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:2484 | A live `*Store` smuggled through the resolver graph as an untyped map entry | fixed — projectV2ToGQL no longer embeds a live *Store in the source map; the field/view/item resolvers reach the store through their *Server closure and projectV2SourceID extracts only the project id. Regression asserts the source carries no store |
| GQL-030 | M | gh_discussions_graphql.go:885 | Nil dereference when a comment outlives its soft-deleted discussion | fixed — `discussionToGQL` now returns nil for a nil discussion rather than dereferencing `d.RepoID`, so the several callers that pass a re-fetched `GetDiscussion` result (which is nil once the discussion is soft-deleted) can no longer panic; unit regression covers the nil input |
| GQL-031 | M | gh_graphql.go:95, gh_orgs_graphql.go:103 | Three root fields return bare null where GitHub returns a `NOT_FOUND` error, contradicting the file's own header comment | fixed — user, organization, repositoryOwner and repository entity misses return typed NOT_FOUND errors |
| GQL-032 | M | gh_graphql.go:80 | `viewer` resolves to null for anonymous callers; it is `User!` on GitHub | fixed — viewer is `User!` and the GraphQL endpoint rejects anonymous requests before execution |
| GQL-033 | M | gh_graphql.go:215 | Validation prepass discards its result, both branches return nil, and the document is parsed twice per request | fixed — parsing and validation produce one AST which the guarded execution path reuses |
| GQL-034 | M | whole lane | Zero interfaces declared; no `Query.node`, `nodes` or `rateLimit` — the canonical Relay refetch path fails outright | fixed — the canonical `Node` interface, stable global IDs, `Query.node`/`nodes` refetching, and `Query.rateLimit` now match the official schema. Node resolution covers users, organizations, repositories, issues, and pull requests while preserving private-resource visibility |
| GQL-035 | M | 11 types | Fabricated type names (`PRComment`, `IssueOrder2`, `PullRequestOrderDirection`) break fragments and generated clients | fixed — every fabricated object type is gone and the official-schema ratchet is down from 319 to exactly 9 enumerated principled entries. The check-suite, team-organization, label, user/issue-connection, latest-review, watcher, license, base-ref and ProjectV2 payload-item forks were renamed or merged into shared registry-memoized official types; a real ReactionContent enum replaced string contents and the three reaction forks collapsed into the shared Reaction/ReactionConnection/ReactionGroup shapes; PRComment/PRCommentConnection were deleted in favor of IssueComment(Connection); the RelatedIssue cluster became real Issue.parent/subIssues/repository fields with lazy resolvers; MinimizableComment/LockableSummary became the official Lockable and Minimizable interfaces over Issue/PullRequest/Discussion and IssueComment/DiscussionComment; and the flattened ProjectV2 field model became the official ProjectV2FieldConfiguration union + ProjectV2FieldCommon interface with GitHub-exact iteration configuration and inputs, resolving the ProjectV2Iteration object-vs-input kind collision. The 9 that remain are each deliberate: Mutation.deleteRepository plus its input/payload (bleephub's authz-audited admin deletion path, which GitHub exposes only via REST), Team.name kept nullable (graphql-go's strict SameResponseShape merge rule for gh CLI's User-or-Team fragments, documented at the definition), and the five built-in scalars introspection enumerates but GitHub's SDL never declares. Ratchet floors raised to 911 implemented / 910 exact-signature, gap ceiling lowered 79→9 |
| GQL-036 | M | 18 sites | `PageInfo` forked eighteen ways; two omit `hasPreviousPage`/`startCursor` though the data is computed | fixed — all implemented Relay connections expose the singleton official `PageInfo` type and complete four-field values |
| GQL-037 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:477 | `RequestedReviewer` union declares Bot and Team and unconditionally returns User | fixed — the RequestedReviewer union's ResolveType now discriminates on the __typename the producers set (User/Bot/Team) instead of unconditionally returning User; requestedReviewer sources are tagged accordingly. Unit regression covers the mapping |
| GQL-038 | M | 12 payloads | `clientMutationId` absent, so a client that sends and selects it gets a validation error | fixed — the mutation registrar guarantees the official input/payload echo for every registered mutation |
| GQL-039 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1191 | `AddCommentPayload.subject` typed as Issue and nulled for PRs | fixed — subject is the Node interface and returns the concrete Issue or PullRequest |
| GQL-040 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1877, gh_pulls_graphql.go:2517 | `authorAssociation` hardcoded OWNER | fixed — OWNER/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR/NONE derive from repository ownership, organization membership and collaborator grants |
| GQL-041 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1872 | Comment `url` is the empty string where GitHub returns `URI!` | fixed — comments expose a non-empty typed URI path |
| GQL-042 | M | 9 sites | Only `Repository.url` honours the external URL; every other entity returns a relative path | fixed — a shared externalURL(path) helper prefixes BLEEPHUB_EXTERNAL_URL onto every GraphQL `url` field (repository, user, org, release, issue, pull request, discussion, comment), matching what Repository.url already did; `resourcePath` fields stay relative. Unit regression covers the helper and the broad GraphQL suite passes unchanged |
| GQL-043 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:2529 | `reviewDecision` derived from all reviews rather than the latest per author, so an approval never clears CHANGES_REQUESTED | fixed — only each author's latest decisive review participates |
| GQL-044 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:935 | `assignableUsers` returns every account on the instance on a false claim that the membership graph is unmodelled | fixed — results are the repository owner, owning-organization members and repository collaborators only |
| GQL-045 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1034 | `autoMergeRequest`, `mergeCommit`, `resolvedBy` and `isPinned` are unconditional nulls or falses contradicting stored state | fixed — `mergeCommit` resolves the persisted merge SHA + git object, and the review-thread `resolvedBy` is now populated: a `ResolvedByID` was added to the review-comment root and its persistence record, `ResolveThread` captures the resolving actor (cleared on unresolve), and the connection renders it via the same `userToGraphQL` lookup it already uses for authors. Regression tests assert `resolvedBy{login}` after resolve, null after unresolve, and that the resolver survives a persistence reload. `autoMergeRequest` and `isPinned` are NOT defects — they are faithful nulls: there is no per-PR auto-merge state and no issue/PR pin store (GitHub itself returns null when the feature isn't enabled), so a resolver defect does not exist for them. Enabling those two is feature work, tracked separately |
| GQL-046 | m | gh_repos_graphql.go:104 | `hasIssuesEnabled` registered twice; `AddFieldConfig` overwrites silently | fixed — the field has one typed registration |
| GQL-047 | m | gh_repos_graphql.go:1008 | Input decode panics rather than returning an error with a path | fixed — optional boolean inputs are comma-ok decoded and malformed values are rejected by GraphQL coercion |
| GQL-048 | m | gh_pagination.go:163 | Two incompatible conventions for reading integer arguments across 14 sites | fixed — the remaining 13 sites reading GraphQL Int arguments via a bare `.(int)` assertion (across the discussions/issues/pulls/repos connection resolvers and `paginateGQLMaps`) were unified onto the `intArg` helper. A literal Int arrives as `int`, but a variable-supplied one arrives as `float64` (or `int64`) after JSON decoding, so `.(int)` silently dropped it and the connection fell back to its default page or a zero `number`. `intArg` accepts all three numeric shapes. Regression test proves `first` supplied as `float64` is honored |
| GQL-049 | m | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go:402 | Three unlock paths with no `defer` — a panic leaks the store read lock | fixed — all project-owner resolution locks defer their unlock |
| GQL-050 | m | gh_discussions_graphql.go:1071 | `bodyHTML` is escaped plaintext in `<p>` tags; goldmark is already a dependency | fixed — discussion Markdown renders through Goldmark |
| GQL-051 | m | gh_graphql.go:174 | Full query text logged on every errored request | fixed — error logs record only operation, query SHA-256, byte length and formatted errors |
| GQL-052 | B | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1736 | The GraphQL merge path bypassed branch protection entirely, while the REST twin enforced it — the merge gate was reachable around | fixed — calls `canMergePullRequest` like REST |
| GQL-053 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1750, :1946 | `authorEmail` was accepted and never recorded, and `updatePullRequest` declared `milestoneId` and dropped it — two more instances of the GQL-006 class in the same file | fixed — both applied, label and assignee ids resolved strictly |
| GQL-054 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go:1020 | `createIssue` silently dropped unresolvable label, assignee and milestone ids | fixed — strict |
| GQL-055 | M | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | Four mutations authenticate without authorizing: `createProjectV2`, `addProjectV2ItemById`, `createProjectV2Field`, `updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue` | fixed — the policy table was repository-shaped, so it is now one rule implementation per resource class (`viewerOnly`/`repoRule`/`projectRule`), and the schema has 31 mutations and 31 rows, asserted by equality. Projects reuse the owner-scoped pair the REST surface already enforces rather than a reinvented ladder. Pre-fix, with the rule neutered, all four served a stranger **and changed state** |
| GQL-056 | B | gh_pulls_graphql.go:1729 | Adding `canMergePullRequest` to the GraphQL merge was not sufficient: it returns true for a repository admin whenever `enforce_admins` is off and only evaluates required status checks **after** that bypass, whereas the REST handler runs the required-checks gate unconditionally **before** it. GraphQL could therefore merge a red pull request that REST refuses — for exactly the class of caller most likely to try | fixed — the unconditional gate runs first, mirroring REST; both callers of `completePullRequestMerge` are now gated identically |
| GQL-057 | B | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go:110 | Write access to a project was never read access to what went into it, so any project owner could pull a stranger's private issue in by node id and read its title back through the project | fixed — `viewerCanReadProjectContent` gates the content as a second resource |
| REST-116 | B | gh_projects_v2_rest.go:911 | `serveProjectV2AddItem` is the REST twin of the hole above: it validates that the content resolves but never that the caller may read it | fixed — the same `viewerCanReadProjectContent` the GraphQL twin uses. Verified exploitable pre-fix: both the database-id and the owner/repo/number addressing modes returned 201 and the victim's private issue was indexed against the stranger's project |
| REST-117 | M | gh_apps_perms.go, gh_teams_rest.go, gh_members_rest.go, gh_orgs_people_rest.go | Organization and team handlers re-ran human owner/member/maintainer checks after `requirePerm` had authorized a GitHub App installation token, so the synthetic `<slug>[bot]` user made valid Members, Administration, Webhooks, and organization-Administration grants fail | fixed — `requirePerm` now carries an unforgeable typed grant into downstream organization resolvers; team, membership, invitation, organization-administration and webhook journeys honor installation permissions and target isolation without persisting the bot as a human member. Team-repository writes explicitly require both Members:read and repository Administration:write, preventing the parity fix from admitting under-scoped Apps |
| ACT-098 | m | webhooks.go:1298 | `createStartupFailureRun` published the terminal run into `s.store.Workflows` under `store.mu`, released the lock, then called `queueActionsEvent` — whose `cloneWorkflowEventSnapshot` copies `*wf` while holding only `snapshotMu`. Once the run is store-visible another goroutine can resolve and mutate it under `store.mu`, so reading its fields lock-free is a data race (the lone one of ~24 `queueActionsEvent` sites not holding `store.mu`); low severity only because the run is already completed so a concurrent writer is unlikely | fixed — both `queueActionsEvent` calls moved inside the same `store.mu` critical section as the publish/persist, matching every other call site (lock order store.mu→snapshotMu is unchanged, no deadlock). Verified under `-race` |
| STORE-078 | m | store_enterprises.go:116, gh_api_insights.go:33, store_issues.go:31 | Three more stringly-typed enums whose valid sets lived only in comments: enterprise Dependabot `dependabot_default_level` (public/internal, "" = unset), API-insights `actor_type` (5 values) + `subject_type` (2 values), and milestone `state` (open/closed) | fixed — typed `DependabotDefaultLevel`, `APIInsightsActorType`/`APIInsightsSubjectType`, and `MilestoneState` with constant sets; struct fields carry the types, internal producers use the constants, and conversion happens only at request/filter boundaries (the milestone list `all` filter value stays a plain string). JSON byte-identical; behaviour-preserving |
| STORE-079 | M | releases.go | the release store was a hole in the closed getter-detachment invariant: `Get`, `GetByTag`, `Latest`, `List`, `GetReleaseAsset` and `ListReleaseAssets` all handed back the live rows, so any reader could rewrite a persisted release or asset by touching the value it was given | fixed — every release and asset getter returns a snapshot (`Assets` and `PublishedAt` are the only reference fields), matching the pattern the other stores already hold. Verified by reversion: restoring the live return fails the new detachment pin |
| STORE-080 | M | store_projects_v2.go | the Projects v2 store was a hole in the closed getter-detachment invariant: `GetProject`, `GetItem`, `GetField`, `GetView`, every `Lookup*ByNodeID` and every `List*`/`*ForProject` handed back the live rows, so a reader could rewrite a persisted project, item, field or view by touching the value it was given | fixed — every getter and list returns a snapshot, with per-type clones because each row carries slices or maps (`Collaborators`, `FieldValues`, `Options`, `Iterations`, `VisibleFields`, `SortBy`). Two call sites were relying on the aliasing to observe their own writes and now re-read explicitly (GQL-073, REST-187) |
| STORE-081 | M | store_projects_v2.go | a new project was created empty, so `gh project field-list` returned nothing and a board had no Status column until somebody built one by hand; github.com seeds every project with its built-in fields, a Status option set, a table view and the default workflows | fixed — `SeedProjectDefaults` installs the seven built-in fields plus Status (Todo/In Progress/Done with GitHub's colours), a "View 1" table view and the three default workflows. `ProjectV2SeededFieldNames` exists so tests count against the seed rather than a literal |
| REST-124 | B | gh_reactions.go:275 | `resolveReactionParent` re-scoped only the `issue` parent to the repository in the URL; for `issue_comment`, `commit_comment`, `pull_request_review_comment` and `release` it returned the raw path id unchecked. Since the store looks each up by global id alone, a caller with write on any repo could `POST /repos/{owned}/{repo}/issues/comments/{victimCommentID}/reactions` to inject a reaction onto — and via the list endpoint read the reactions and reacting users of — a comment or release in another (private) repository. It also never verified the parent existed | fixed — every parent type now resolves its object and requires the object's repo == the path repo (404 otherwise), mirroring the sibling handlers' `requireRepoOwns`/`RepoID != repo.ID` checks. Regression test reacts/lists via a foreign repo path for all four non-issue types and asserts 404 with no reaction landing on the victim's parent; the existing all-parent-types test now seeds real parents |
| REST-125 | B | gh_projects_classic.go:544, store_projects_classic.go:388 | `handleMoveProjectCard` authorized `projects:write` only on the card's own project/repo, then passed the request-body `column_id` to `MoveProjectCard` after only an existence check — the destination column's project/repo was never authorized. A caller with projects-write on their own repo could `POST /projects/columns/cards/{ownCardID}/moves` with a `column_id` belonging to another repo's private classic project and reassign their card into it (cross-tenant board injection; same "trusted raw body id" shape as the reaction/PR-review IDORs) | fixed — `MoveProjectCard` now requires the target column and the card's source column to share a `ProjectID`, returning an error the handler maps to 422 (GitHub's response for a cross-project move). Regression test moves a card into a foreign project's column (422, card unmoved, victim column uninjected) and confirms same-project moves still 201 |
| STORE-077 | m | store_code_scanning.go:1059, gh_environment_policies.go:31, store_dependabot.go:23 | Three more stringly-typed enums whose valid sets lived only in comments: CodeQL variant-analysis `analysis_status`, deployment-branch-policy `type` ("branch"/"tag"), and Dependabot alert `state` | fixed — typed `CodeScanningAnalysisStatus`, `DeploymentBranchPolicyType`, and `DependabotAlertState` with constant sets; struct fields carry the types, validators/filters convert only at the REST boundary. JSON byte-identical; behaviour-preserving. Completes the alert-state-machine trio with code-scanning + secret-scanning |
| STORE-076 | m | gh_pull_merge_async.go:21, gh_deployments.go:62, gh_statuses_rest.go:25 | Async-merge `status`, deployment `state`, and commit-status `state` were free `string` fields whose value sets lived only in comments; commit-status even had a normalizer (`normalizeStatusState`) that canonicalized input but threw the guarantee away by returning `string` | fixed — typed enums `MergeAsyncStatus`, `DeploymentStatusState`, and `CommitStatusState` with constant sets; `normalizeStatusState` now returns the typed value (parse-don't-validate), struct fields carry the types, and conversion happens only at the request boundary. JSON byte-identical; behaviour-preserving |
| STORE-075 | m | store_code_scanning.go:23, store_secret_scanning.go:42, store_issues.go:55 | Alert lifecycle states and reasons (code-scanning `state`/`dismissed_reason`, secret-scanning `state`/`resolution`, issue/PR `active_lock_reason`) were free `string` fields whose valid sets lived only in comments and scattered literals — a typo or drifted value was representable | fixed — introduced typed enums `CodeScanningState`/`CodeScanningDismissedReason`, `SecretScanningState`/`SecretScanningResolution`, and `LockReason` with the documented constant sets; the struct fields carry the types, validators/serialisers convert only at the REST boundary, and JSON output is byte-identical (typed strings marshal as strings). Behaviour-preserving; scanning + lock/moderation suites green |
| REST-122 | B | gh_pulls_rest.go:826 | `handleUpdatePRReview` wrote the review body by global id (`UpdatePullRequestReview` looks reviews up by id alone) *before* scoping it to the PR in the path and never checked authorship — a caller who owned any repo/PR could `PUT /repos/{owned}/{repo}/pulls/{n}/reviews/{victimReviewID}` and rewrite a review living on another repository, and the late 404 hid that the write had already landed; even naming the correct repo, a non-author could edit another user's review | fixed — resolve the review and require `review.PRID == pr.ID` (404) and `review.AuthorID == user.ID` (403, author-only per GitHub) before mutating; unauthenticated is 401. Regression test proves cross-repo 404 + non-author 403 both leave the body unchanged, and the author edit still succeeds |
| REST-123 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go:443 | `handleMergePullRequest` gates only on `user != nil` (and branch protection, which passes when none is configured); a read-only authenticated user can merge an open PR on a public repo with no protection. GitHub requires write access | fixed — `handleMergePullRequest` now requires push access via `viewerCanPushRepo` before merging, returning `403 "Must have write access to the repository."`; a read-only authenticated user (or a non-collaborator on a public repo with no protection) is refused while owners/collaborators with push still merge cleanly. This is not the "uniform gap" the earlier note feared: GitHub deliberately gates the PR verbs differently — opening a PR uses the fork model (read + head-branch access, no base push), updating is author-or-push, and merge alone requires base-repo push — so gating merge on push is exact parity, not an inconsistency. Regression `TestMergePullRequestRequiresPushAccess` (403 for a reader, PR unmerged; 200 for the owner) |
| GQL-058 | m | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | The project lane's NOT_FOUND wording differs in capitalisation from the repository lane's `gqlMissingNode` | fixed — projects-v2 NOT_FOUND errors now return the typed ghNotFoundError with gqlMissingNode's 'Could not resolve ... .' wording, so the project and repository lanes match (capitalisation + trailing period) |
| GQL-059 | M | gh_orgs_graphql.go | `User.organizations` sorted only by `createdAt`; equal timestamps retained map order, making cursor pages unstable and omitting newly-created organizations from bounded connections under a deterministic clock | fixed — equal creation times are ordered by descending organization ID, giving a total newest-first order and stable Relay page boundaries |
| GQL-060 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go | The GraphQL `createIssue`/`closeIssue`/`reopenIssue` mutations mutated the store but never emitted the `issues` webhook or recorded the timeline event the REST paths do, so `on: issues` workflows never fired for gh-CLI-driven issue changes | fixed — the resolvers deliver the opened/closed/reopened webhook and record the close/reopen timeline event through a shared helper guarded on the actual state transition; a regression asserts opened+closed deliveries originate from GraphQL |
| GQL-061 | m | gh_issues_graphql.go | `createIssue` declares `projectIds` and `issueTemplate` inputs the resolver never reads, so a non-null `projectIds` is accepted and silently dropped | fixed — a supplied `projectIds` adds the new issue to each named ProjectV2 board (resolved via `LookupProjectByNodeID`, an unknown id erroring like GitHub); `issueTemplate` is a client hint with no server-side state and is accepted for coercion; regression covers a non-null `projectIds` |
| GQL-062 | m | gh_discussions_graphql.go, gh_issues_graphql.go, gh_moderation_graphql.go, gh_pulls_graphql.go, gh_reactions_graphql.go | ~40 mutation resolvers returned a bare `fmt.Errorf("could not resolve to a …")` for a missing node, so — unlike the query paths, which use `*ghNotFoundError`/`gqlMissingNode` — the wire error carried no `type: NOT_FOUND` and used lowercase/period-less casing, leaving a client unable to distinguish "no such object" from a transport failure | fixed — every such site now returns `gqlMissingNode(type, id)` (github's canonical `"Could not resolve to a X with the global id of 'Y'."`) or, where the id was already consumed, `gqlMissingNodeType(type)`, both `*ghNotFoundError` so the envelope stamps `NOT_FOUND`. Verified: unit test asserts a mutation on a missing node returns `errors[].type == NOT_FOUND`; the existing query-path NotFound test still passes |
| GQL-063 | M | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go, gh_repos_graphql.go | the `deleteProjectV2Item` mutation was absent, so `gh project item-delete` (and any client removing an item from a Projects v2 board) had no server support — the schema exposed add/update but not delete | fixed — added `deleteProjectV2Item(input{projectId,itemId})` returning `deletedItemId`; it resolves the project and item, refuses an item that belongs to another project, deletes through the store, and is gated by the same owner-scoped `projectRule` as the sibling project mutations (with a policy-table row and refusal case, so the coverage tests stay green). Verified: unit tests assert the owner deletes their own item and receives its node id back, a stranger is refused, and an item from another project is not found; the introspection snapshot and official-schema ratchet were regenerated |
| GQL-064 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go, gh_repos_graphql.go | the GraphQL schema exposed `addPullRequestReview` and thread resolve/unresolve but not the review-lifecycle mutations `submitPullRequestReview` and `dismissPullRequestReview`, though the store fully backs both — a client could create a review but neither submit a pending one nor dismiss one over GraphQL | fixed — registered both (signature-exact to the official schema: submit takes nullable pullRequestId/pullRequestReviewId + required event, dismiss takes required pullRequestReviewId + message), gated by a new owner-scoped `mutationTargetReview` authz target (read-level submit with authorMayAct, push-level dismiss), each with a policy-table row + refusal/entitled coverage case; introspection snapshot + official-schema ratchet regenerated. Verified: authz refusal/entitled cases plus a behavioral test that submits a pending review (→ APPROVED) then dismisses it (→ DISMISSED). Named remainders: `requestReviews`, `addPullRequestReviewThread`/`addPullRequestReviewComment` (need a team-node resolver + diff-position plumbing), the pending-by-default lifecycle for `addPullRequestReview`, and computing `PullRequestReviewThread.isOutdated` against the head diff — each a scoped follow-up |
| GQL-065 | M | gh_reactions_graphql.go, gh_repos_graphql.go | `addReaction`/`removeReaction` resolved only Discussion and DiscussionComment subjects and hard-coded the discussions scope, so a client could read an issue's reactionGroups but not add a reaction over GraphQL | fixed — subject resolution now covers Issue, PullRequest and PullRequestReview (mirroring the REST parent-type strings the store already keys), and a subject-typed scope hook gates each reaction on the subject's own grant (issues, pull_requests or discussions) instead of one blanket scope. Verified: a test adds and removes reactions on an issue, a PR and a review over GraphQL and asserts the store rows |
| GQL-066 | m | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | the `ProjectV2CustomFieldType` enum omitted `MULTI_SELECT`, so `createProjectV2Field(dataType: MULTI_SELECT)` failed enum coercion though the store backs it | fixed — added the value; introspection snapshot and official-schema ratchet regenerated |
| GQL-067 | m | gh_graphql.go | GraphQL permission denials carried no `type` member, unlike the NOT_FOUND channel, so clients could not distinguish a forbidden write from an untyped error | fixed — a `ghForbiddenError` analog is returned by the authorize permission branches and the serializer stamps `type: FORBIDDEN`, leaving the NOT_FOUND masking for the unreadable-resource path. Verified: a test asserts an under-privileged push-level mutation returns `type: FORBIDDEN` and leaves the resource untouched |
| GQL-068 | m | gh_reactions_graphql.go | `AddReactionPayload`/`RemoveReactionPayload` exposed only `clientMutationId`; GitHub also exposes `reaction`, `reactionGroups` and `subject` | fixed — all three fields now resolve. `reactionGroups: [ReactionGroup!]` off the mutated subject (r29); `reaction: Reaction` returns the created/removed reaction with a `REA_` node id via the shared Reaction type (r34); and `subject: Reactable` (r36) returns the fully-populated concrete subject through a real Reactable interface (id/databaseId/reactionGroups/reactions/viewerCanReact) with ResolveType across all nine official concrete types — Issue, PullRequest, PullRequestReview, Discussion, DiscussionComment, IssueComment, CommitComment (a new GraphQL type), PullRequestReviewComment and Release, each declared to implement it — and addReaction/removeReaction now resolve every one of the nine subjects (four new node-id finders added). Introspection snapshot + official-schema ratchet regenerated with no new compatibility gaps; verified by a test selecting `subject{__typename ...on Issue{title} viewerCanReact reactionGroups{content}}` from addReaction |
| GQL-069 | B | gh_projects_v2_types_graphql.go | Projects v2 had no query entry point: `Organization.projectV2/projectsV2`, `User.projectV2/projectsV2` and the `Repository` pair were all absent, so a project was reachable only from an issue that already sat on one — every `gh project` subcommand starts from the owner and had nothing to query | fixed — all six fields resolve, gated by the same owner-scoped read predicate the REST surface uses, with `orderBy` (ProjectV2Order) and `query` filtering. A private project is dropped from the connection and answers null by number for a caller who may not see it, so the listing cannot become an enumeration oracle. Verified: entry-point, user-owner and stranger-visibility tests, plus `gh project list/view` in the CLI harness |
| GQL-070 | B | gh_graphql.go:598 | The page-size guard rejected `first: 0` as malformed, but zero is a legal Relay window asking for a connection's metadata without nodes — `gh project create` selects the shared project fragment with `items(first: 0)` and `fields(first: 0)`, so the command was refused outright with "first must be between 1 and 100" | fixed — the bound is 0..100, and `paginateGQLItems` now honours an explicit zero as an empty window instead of folding it into "unspecified" and serving the 30-item default. Verified by the CLI harness (`gh project create` was the failing verb) and by tests asserting totalCount survives while nodes is empty, and that -1 and 101 are still refused |
| GQL-071 | M | gh_graphql.go:183, gh_orgs_graphql.go:16 | `ProjectV2Owner` was minted while the Projects v2 surface was assembled, long after the `User` and `Organization` objects were built; graphql-go reads an object's interface list once and memoizes it, so neither type could ever become a possible type and gh's `... on Organization` spread failed validation with "objects of type ProjectV2Owner can never be of type Organization" | fixed — the interface is constructed before both objects and declared in their `Interfaces` lists, with its own fields behind a thunk so it may name the ProjectV2 object that does not exist that early |
| GQL-072 | M | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | `createProjectV2Field` read only `name` out of each single-select option, silently discarding the colour and description GitHub requires, and `ProjectV2SingleSelectFieldOptionInput` declared only `name` where GitHub declares `name`, `color`, `description` and an optional `id` | fixed — one memoized option input matching GitHub's shape, shared with `updateProjectV2Field` and mirrored by `ProjectV2MultiSelectFieldOptionInput`; the resolver reads all four members. Verified: a test asserts a created option keeps its RED colour and description |
| GQL-073 | M | gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | `updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue` rendered its payload from the item snapshot read before the write, so the mutation answered with the value the item had beforehand | fixed — the item is re-read after the store write and the payload renders the updated row; the same defect in the REST twin is REST-187 |
| GQL-074 | M | gh_projects_v2_mutations_graphql.go | Twenty-seven of GitHub's thirty-two ProjectV2 mutations were absent — project metadata, delete, copy, templates, repository/team links, collaborators, field and view editing, item archival, reordering, draft editing and conversion, status updates and workflows | fixed — all twenty-seven added, each through `registerMutation` with a `graphqlMutationAuthz` row plus refusal and entitled test cases. Subjects named by a bare field, view, status-update or workflow id authorize over the project that subject belongs to; `copyProjectV2` demands read on the source and write on the destination account through its own two-sided rule |
| GQL-075 | B | gh_resource_graphql.go | `Query.resource` — GitHub's URL-to-node lookup — did not exist, so `gh project item-add --url` could not turn a pasted issue or pull-request URL into the node id `addProjectV2ItemById` takes, and failed before reaching the mutation | fixed — `resource(url: URI!): UniformResourceLocatable` resolves a repository, issue or pull-request URL, with the `UniformResourceLocatable` interface declared on all three types (and `resourcePath` added to each, which GitHub exposes and none of them had). A repository the caller cannot read answers the same null a missing one does, so a pasted URL is not an existence oracle. Verified: tests resolve an issue and a repository URL, reject four non-resource URLs, and confirm a stranger gets null for a private repository's issue |
| GQL-076 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go | the `ProjectV2ItemFieldValue` union had five of GitHub's twelve members and no `ProjectV2ItemFieldValueCommon` interface, so `gh project item-list`'s single fragment — which names every member — failed validation, taking every `gh project` subcommand down with it, not only the ones reading those columns | fixed — all twelve members exist: the six stored ones (text, number, date, single select, multi select, iteration) implement Node and ProjectV2ItemFieldValueCommon, and the six content-derived ones (labels, milestone, repository, users, reviewers, linked pull requests) read their value off the issue or pull request the item points at. The union and both interfaces are built in one pass, because graphql-go fixes a union's members and an object's interfaces at construction |
| GQL-077 | M | gh_pulls_graphql.go | `PullRequest.repository` was absent — only `headRepository` existed — and gh's shared project item fragment selects it | fixed — the field resolves the repository the pull request was opened against, as distinct from the fork its head came from |
| GQL-078 | M | gh_issues_graphql.go | `ProjectV2FieldType` omitted seven of GitHub's values including `MULTI_SELECT`, and there was no `ProjectV2MultiSelectField` configuration type, so a multi-select field's `dataType` returned null for a non-nullable field and the whole selection errored | fixed — the enum carries all twenty-one values, `ProjectV2MultiSelectField` joins the ProjectV2FieldConfiguration union with its `multiSelectOptions(names:)` lookup, and the store persists options for both option-bearing data types rather than single-select only. `createProjectV2Field`/`updateProjectV2Field` accept `multiSelectOptions`, and `updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue` accepts `multiSelectOptionIds`. Verified: a round-trip test writes a two-option selection and reads back the options plus GitHub's comma-joined `value`, and an unknown option id is refused |
| GQL-079 | B | gh_labels_graphql.go, gh_repos_graphql.go | GitHub's three Labelable mutations were absent — `addLabelsToLabelable`, `removeLabelsFromLabelable`, `clearLabelsFromLabelable` — and so was the `Labelable` interface their payloads return; `gh issue edit --add-label` speaks only these and has no REST fallback, so the command died on `Unknown type "AddLabelsToLabelableInput"` | fixed — all three registered signature-exact to the official schema (`labelableId: ID!` plus `labelIds: [ID!]` for add and `[ID!]!` for remove), returning `labelable: Labelable` behind a real interface Issue and PullRequest declare. They share one resolver that writes through the store's replace-the-set primitive, so the labeled/unlabeled timeline events and the webhook label delta are the same ones the REST routes produce, and a label id belonging to another repository is refused rather than attached. Each has a `graphqlMutationAuthz` row (issues scope, push level, no author exemption — labeling is triage of the repository, not of your own content) with refusal and entitled coverage cases; introspection snapshot and official-schema ratchet regenerated with no new gaps |
| GQL-080 | B | gh_pulls_graphql.go:2955 | `gitActorSourceLocked` wrote its user lookup into the `GitActor` source unconditionally, and a nil `map[string]interface{}` placed in an interface-typed member is not a nil interface — so a commit signed by an address no account owns produced a non-null `User` shell with no node id and the whole query failed with `Cannot return null for non-nullable field User.id`, taking every field of `gh pr view --json commits` down with it | fixed — the member is present only when the signature's email belongs to an account, which is where GitHub puts the null; `PullRequest.headRepository`/`headRepositoryOwner` carried the same typed-nil defect and are now interface-typed too. Verified by reversion: the regression reproduces the exact error and path before the fix and passes after, with a companion case proving a known address still resolves its account |
| GQL-081 | M | gh_checks_required_graphql.go, gh_pulls_graphql.go:122 | `StatusContext.isRequired` and `CheckRun.isRequired` did not exist, so `gh pr checks` — which selects the field on every rollup context — failed document validation and the subcommand was unusable | fixed — both fields plus the `RequirableByPullRequest` interface GitHub declares them on, with GitHub's `pullRequestId`/`pullRequestNumber` argument pair. The answer comes from the base branch's protection through a new `RequiredStatusCheckContexts` seam over the server's `requiredCheckContexts`, the same set the merge gate blocks on, so the table cannot mark a check required that the merge would let through; a pull request in another repository is not allowed to answer for this commit. Verified: a test asserting one required and one optional context of each kind, both argument forms, and a pull request onto an unprotected branch requiring nothing |
| GQL-082 | M | gh_gists_graphql.go | `User.gists` and the whole gist object graph — `GistPrivacy`, `GistOrder`, `GistOrderField`, `Gist`, `GistFile`, `GistEdge`, `GistConnection` — were absent though the REST `/gists` surface is fully backed, so `gh gist list` failed validation on `Unknown type "GistPrivacy"` | fixed — the field resolves with GitHub's `privacy`, `orderBy` and all four Relay window arguments, rendering gists from the one store the REST surface reads. A secret gist is listed for its owner alone, so the connection cannot become a way around the REST gate, and equal timestamps break their tie on the node id so a page boundary does not move between identical requests. Verified: the gist-list document gh sends, privacy filtering both ways, cursor pagination, and a stranger seeing only the public gist |
## ACT — Actions engine, runners, webhooks

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ACT-001 | B | artifacts.go:890 | `Content-Range` end is unbounded — a 1-byte body can allocate a terabyte | fixed — a `Content-Range` end beyond the cache limit is rejected, the body read is bounded by the declared range via `MaxBytesReader`, and chunks buffer as segments rather than into a buffer sized from the header. Verified: the old preallocation thrashes for 11.8s where the fix answers in 0.6s |
| ACT-002 | B | artifacts.go:305,632, timeline.go:38 | Artifact and log endpoints unauthenticated with sequential IDs; omitting the run ID returns every artifact across every repository | fixed — artifact and blob routes take a job runtime token; a missing `workflow_run_backend_id` is 400 and an unknown one 404, never a wildcard. Timeline and log routes are bound per-plan, so one job cannot write another's |
| ACT-003 | B | actions.go:52,180 | Unauthenticated action-tarball endpoint returns a gzip of any private repository at HEAD | fixed — the tarball route takes a job token and resolves the action's repository **before the cache hit is served**, 404ing when it is not hosted and requiring scope coverage for a private one |
| ACT-004 | B | jobs.go:353-369 | Matrix expansion shares one env map across combinations, so every job gets the last combination's values — but only when the job declares `env:`, which the existing test does not | fixed |
| ACT-005 | B | jobs.go:341,374 | The `needs` rewrite depends on map iteration order and fails nondeterministically about half the time | fixed |
| ACT-006 | B | jobs.go:342 | `include:`-only matrices are silently dropped; `ExpandMatrix` handles them and is never reached | fixed |
| ACT-007 | B | webhooks.go:265 | Fork PRs never trigger any workflow; the whole fork-approval machinery is unreachable | fixed — fork pull requests trigger, gated by the approval flow that already existed |
| ACT-008 | B | webhooks.go:260,465 | Workflow files are read from repository HEAD, not the triggering ref | fixed — the definition comes from the triggering ref for a push and from the **base** repository for a fork pull request, so a fork cannot rewrite the workflow to read the base's secrets. Every failure path returns an error rather than falling back. Verified by reversion: pointing the fork case at its own ref fails the test |
| ACT-009 | B | workflow_triggers.go:308 | A package-global regex cache written with no mutex from concurrent trigger evaluation — `fatal error: concurrent map writes` | fixed — `sync.Map`. Verified: the plain map produces 5 data races under `-race` |
| ACT-010 | B | workflows.go:706, broker.go:154 | `LockedUntil` is written three times and never read; the pending message is removed before the response is flushed, so a dropped connection loses the job permanently | fixed — the lease is real and an expired one redelivers; the broker half requeues on a failed write rather than dropping the job |
| ACT-011 | B | webhooks.go:80,183,204 | Webhook delivery is a full SSRF: user-controlled URL, no IP validation, redirects followed, unbounded goroutines, no per-hook ordering | fixed — `webhookAddrBlocked` covers loopback, link-local incl. the metadata endpoint, RFC1918, fc00::/7, CGNAT, multicast, unspecified, broadcast and the IPv4-mapped/NAT64 spellings; checked at configuration time and again on `net.Dialer.Control` against the address actually dialed, so DNS rebinding between the two cannot win; redirects become the recorded outcome rather than being followed; a 16-worker dispatcher with a per-hook FIFO bounds fan-out and preserves ordering. `BLEEPHUB_ALLOW_PRIVATE_OUTBOUND_TARGETS` (renamed; see AUTH-064) is deny-by-default and never relaxes the scheme rule |
| ACT-012 | M | workflows.go:402 | Concurrency admission is a TOCTOU across two lock acquisitions and ignores non-running holders | fixed — cross-replica admission now serializes on the shared database: on a clustered deployment (persistence attached and not exclusively owned) submit-side admission, pending-group promotion and job-group admission each take a TTL'd named lock (`actions/concurrency/…` via Persistence.AcquireLock, the same primitive git object storage already uses in production) and force a staleness refresh inside the lock so the group scan sees peer-admitted holders before deciding; the decision is committed via persistWorkflowRecord before release. Single-writer deployments skip it entirely (OwnedExclusively), zero cost. On lock error or deadline it degrades to process-local admission with a logged warning rather than losing a run. Accepted residual: lock TTL expiry under a stalled replica can double-admit a group for the ~5s TTL window — the same bounded exposure the git-object lock accepts; fencing tokens are deliberately out of scope. Covered by TestConcurrencyAdmissionSerializesAcrossReplicas |
| ACT-013 | M | workflows.go:417,1166 | `cancel-in-progress` does not cancel pending runs, and the queue releases oldest-first so a loaded group replays a stale backlog | fixed — a submission supersedes every older pending run, `cancel-in-progress` cancels active and pending holders, and recovery promotes only the newest pending run |
| ACT-014 | M | workflow.go:137-161 | `permissions:`, `defaults:`, `run-name:` and job-level `concurrency:` are accepted by the parser and discarded; GITHUB_TOKEN scope is fixed regardless | fixed — declarations are strongly parsed and validated; run defaults and expression-backed run names execute; job concurrency is now evaluated, persisted, case-insensitive, enforced across runs, replaces stale pending jobs, and delivers cancellation to an active holder. And least-privilege GITHUB_TOKEN API scopes are now enforced: `resolveJobTokenPermissions` computes the effective permission map (GitHub semantics — a job block replaces a workflow block replaces the repo default; `read-all`/`write-all` and `none` handled; an empty block yields metadata:read only), which is embedded in the per-job token by `makeJobJWT` (new `repo`/`perms` claims). The REST gate recognizes the token as a `jobTokenPrincipal`, so `requirePerm` forbids any scope/level the workflow was not granted and `credentialMayAccessTarget` binds it to its own repository alone. The token authenticates as `github-actions[bot]` for handler attribution, matching the installation-token precedent. Regressions `TestResolveJobTokenPermissions` (semantics) and `TestGithubTokenIsRepoScopedLeastPrivilege` (granted scope passes, ungranted 403, cross-repo denied) |
| ACT-015 | M | workflow.go:103-112 | `steps.*.env` and `steps.*.shell` are parsed with zero read sites; `working-directory`, `continue-on-error` and `timeout-minutes` are not even fields | fixed — all five fields are represented and encoded in the runner's step message |
| ACT-016 | M | jobs.go:368 | Matrix values round-trip through `map[string]string`, so `matrix.version == 3` is false where GitHub says true | fixed — combinations stay typed through `JobDef`, `WorkflowJob`, and PipelineContextData |
| ACT-017 | M | workflows_msg.go:127 | Internal `__matrix_*` keys leak into every matrix job's shell environment | fixed — the environment is copied independently and matrix data has a dedicated typed field |
| ACT-018 | M | matrix.go:20 | `include` applied before `exclude`, reversing GitHub's documented order | fixed |
| ACT-019 | M | workflows.go:315 | Matrix group membership reverse-engineered from the job key's textual shape, so unrelated jobs named `build_1`/`build_2` share fail-fast | fixed — expansion records explicit group identity |
| ACT-020 | M | workflows.go:80,323 | `max-parallel` stored per run rather than per matrix group, last writer wins | fixed — limits are retained and enforced per matrix group |
| ACT-021 | M | workflows.go:834,1037 | `continue-on-error` does not prevent the run from failing — its entire documented purpose | fixed — including the fail-fast interaction |
| ACT-022 | M | workflows.go:1230 | A completion arriving with no result is silently recorded as successful | fixed — a missing runner result is a failure |
| ACT-023 | M | workflow_call.go:374,461 | Broken `with:`, output and secret templates are logged and skipped, so a called workflow runs with empty inputs or unauthenticated | fixed — input/secret template errors fail closed before runner dispatch and an invalid output fails the collector |
| ACT-024 | M | workflow_call.go:398 | `"yes"` coerces to false for a boolean input and `"12abc"` to 12 for a number; `choice` options unvalidated | fixed — strict full-value boolean/number parsing and choice membership validation |
| ACT-025 | M | workflow_call.go:197 | A reusable workflow's own top-level `env:` is dropped | fixed — called-workflow environment is merged into each child with job-level precedence |
| ACT-026 | M | webhooks.go:249 | ~40 `on:` events parse and never fire; only push, pull_request, repository_dispatch, release and schedule are ever dispatched | fixed — webhook emission is the single event-production choke point and every implemented mutation event automatically feeds Actions. Issue-comment create/edit/delete and pull-request-review submit/dismiss now join push, PR, issue, review-comment, release, deployment, status/check and workflow lifecycle producers; families with no real mutation producer remain open, though the set keeps shrinking — label create/edit/delete now produces the `label` event (regression `TestWebhookLabelEvent`), and milestone create/close/reopen/edit/delete now produces the `milestone` event with GitHub's action semantics (regression `TestWebhookMilestoneEvent`); branch/tag creation and deletion now produce the `create` and `delete` events with `ref`/`ref_type` (regression `TestWebhookCreateDeleteRefEvent`); commit-comment creation produces the `commit_comment` event repository forks produce the `fork` event on the source repo (regression `TestWebhookCommitCommentAndForkEvents`, which also surfaced and fixed a pre-existing commit-comment response-shape gap — `path`/`position`/`line` are now emitted as required-nullable), starring a repo produces the `watch` event with action `started` (regression `TestWebhookWatchEvent`), switching a repo from private to public produces the `public` event (regression `TestWebhookPublicEvent`), creating a classic project produces the `project` event (regression `TestWebhookProjectEvent`), establishing/updating/removing a branch-protection rule produces the `branch_protection_rule` event (regression `TestWebhookBranchProtectionRuleEvent`);
