accepting a repo invitation produces the `member` event, creating a discussion/comment produces the `discussion`/`discussion_comment` events, finishing a Pages build produces the `page_build` event, and classic project column/card create and card move produce the `project_column`/`project_card` events (regressions `TestWebhookMemberEvent`, `TestWebhookDiscussionEvents`, `TestWebhookPageBuildEvent`, `TestWebhookProjectCardColumnEvents`) Every GitHub-Actions `on:` trigger event that has a producer in bleephub now fires: the pre-existing set plus the families wired here (create, delete, milestone, commit_comment, fork, watch, public, project, project_column, project_card, branch_protection_rule, member, discussion, discussion_comment, page_build, registry_package). The only Actions trigger events that still do not fire are the ones with no producer in this surface — `merge_group` (no merge queue; `gollum` now has a producer, see the wiki-over-git entry) — and the non-webhook mechanisms `workflow_dispatch`/`workflow_call`; `pull_request_target` shares the `pull_request` producer. |
| ACT-027 | M | webhooks.go:290 | `pull_request` runs report the head branch ref and SHA instead of the merge ref | fixed — runs now report `refs/pull/<number>/merge` and prefer `merge_commit_sha`; until Bleephub materializes synthetic merge commits, an event without that field falls back to the head SHA And the missing piece is done: an open pull request's test-merge commit is now materialized (`computeMergeCommitHash` merges head into base without advancing the base ref, extracted from `performMergeCommit`) on PR open and on synchronize, stored as `PullRequest.PotentialMergeCommitSHA`, and reported as the event's `merge_commit_sha` — so a `pull_request` run runs against the merge ref/SHA, not the head. A conflict or missing git storage clears the field, keeping the head-SHA fallback; `MergeCommitSHA` stays merged-only so GraphQL `mergeCommit` is unaffected. Regression `TestPullRequestRunReportsMergeSHA`. |
| ACT-028 | M | workflows_msg.go:601 | Step conditions omit GitHub's implicit `success() &&` guard, so steps run after an earlier failure | fixed — conditions without an explicit status function receive the implicit guard |
| ACT-029 | M | expressions.go:93 | Status-function detection is a lowercased substring scan — `contains(msg,'always()')` is misread as a gate | fixed — lexer tokens identify actual function calls without inspecting string contents |
| ACT-030 | M | workflows.go:643,1276 | The timeout clock starts at queue time, and a timed-out job concludes `cancelled` where GitHub says `failure` | fixed — queue and execution timestamps are separate, only running jobs time out, and the conclusion is failure |
| ACT-031 | M | broker.go:318, actions_events.go:62 | Cancellations and lifecycle webhooks are dropped when a buffer is full — a cancelled job keeps running while the API reports otherwise | fixed — lifecycle admission is lossless and non-blocking under the store lock; the unbounded condition queue cannot deadlock on its consumer or silently discard a transition |
| ACT-032 | M | actions_events.go:27,100 | Event payloads rendered at drain time from live pointers: a `queued` event can carry `completed` state, and the reads race the engine | fixed — every transition deep-snapshots workflow inputs/event payloads and job outputs/matrix state at admission; webhook rendering uses that immutable snapshot while check mutations retain the live identity |
| ACT-033 | M | webhooks_payloads.go:120 | `forced: false` asserted though it was computed one line earlier; `commits`, `head_commit`, `compare` and `pusher` empty, so `[skip ci]` silently misbehaves | fixed — object-graph force detection, bounded commit vectors, head commit, compare and pusher payloads; all documented skip directives suppress Actions |
| ACT-034 | M | workflows.go:264 | `run_number` is the global run ID, so the first run of a new workflow reports a five-digit number | fixed — each workflow file uses an atomically allocated durable sequence and reruns retain the original number |
| ACT-035 | M | broker.go:148,289 | A session with no agent object skips the busy check, satisfies hosted-runner labels, drains the queue and produces uncancellable jobs | fixed — session creation and polling require an authenticated registered agent, and the broker independently refuses nil/zero agents |
| ACT-036 | M | expressions.go:427 | Object and array filters (`.*`, `[*]`) are standard GitHub syntax and fail the job | fixed — both filter spellings project arrays and objects through chained properties, with declaration order for arrays and deterministic key order for objects |
| ACT-037 | m | webhooks_store.go:26 | `insecure_ssl` accepted, persisted, echoed and never applied | fixed — the dispatcher selects a shared TLS transport with verification disabled only for hooks explicitly configured with `insecure_ssl=1`; address and redirect controls remain enforced |
| ACT-038 | m | webhooks.go:79 | Hook fields read off a shared pointer outside the lock; the same race was recognised and fixed for one field only | fixed — delivery queues receive a deep hook snapshot, including events and last response, captured under the store lock |
| ACT-039 | m | actions.go:65 | A malformed body produces a successful empty response, so the runner fails later with an opaque error | fixed — malformed JSON and incomplete action entries return 400 at the download-info boundary |
| ACT-040 | m | actions.go:20,147 | Action tarball cache keyed by mutable ref, never invalidated, unbounded, holding full tarballs in memory | fixed — cache identity is the resolved immutable commit SHA, returned bytes are detached, and insertion is bounded to 128 archives |
| ACT-041 | m | timeline.go:307 | Timeline attachments read and discarded — `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` content never appears anywhere | fixed — bounded summary attachments persist with the job and render as safe Markdown in run detail |
| ACT-042 | m | timeline.go:202, artifacts.go:548, secret_scanning_ingest.go:57 | Unbounded `io.ReadAll` on log, artifact and scanning paths; the 4 MiB log cap is applied after the full read | fixed — the remaining unbounded reads (secret-scanning blob scans in secret_scanning_ingest.go) now read through io.LimitReader capped at 5 MiB; the log/artifact/step-summary paths were already bounded by MaxBytesReader/LimitReader |
| ACT-043 | m | workflows.go:1248 | `cancelTimeout` written without the store lock; watcher goroutines leak for runs that never resolve | fixed — a dedicated mutex serializes watcher replacement/stop and replacing a watcher cancels the previous goroutine |
| ACT-044 | m | workflows.go:746, broker.go:169, artifacts.go:1028 | Linear scans over every job and workflow on hot paths, never garbage collected — completed job messages holding plaintext secrets are retained indefinitely | fixed — the hot paths are indexed and the secret payloads are freed. Unexported store indexes (jobsByPlanID/jobsByRequestID, planScopes, workflowsByRunID, per-concurrency-group maps) make runner long-poll job matching, plan/request lookups, artifact scope resolution and group admission O(1)-per-key instead of O(all jobs and workflows ever); job-token auth (repoForJobScope, requirePlanJob) reads a small planScopes entry recorded at dispatch and no longer JSON-parses the secret-bearing message on every request. GC: at run finalization every job's Message (GITHUB_TOKEN plus all secret values) is cleared; a 10-minute janitor retires job stubs, plan scopes, LogMasks and in-memory log claims once CompletedAt + the 6h runner-token TTL passes (late teardown calls still authenticate via planScopes); run and repo deletion tear down eagerly. The used-ephemeral-runner disqualification that previously depended on completed jobs staying in memory now rides an EverAssigned flag, proven by TestUsedEphemeralAgentStaysDisqualifiedAfterJobGC; TestLateFinishJobAfterMessageGCAuthenticates and TestActionsJanitorSweepsRetiredJobState cover the GC contract |
| ACT-045 | m | workflows_msg.go:173 | Secret values inserted into a regex mask unescaped, so a secret with metacharacters is rejected and left unmasked | fixed — every literal token and secret is `regexp.QuoteMeta` encoded before entering the regex mask list |
| ACT-046 | m | matrix.go:135 | Matrix display names use sorted keys rather than declaration order, breaking required-status-check contexts | fixed — YAML mapping order is retained and drives Cartesian expansion and display names |
| ACT-047 | m | matrix.go:110 | Include/exclude matching compares values via `fmt.Sprintf`, so `1` and `"1"` are equal | fixed — normalized values are compared with type-preserving equality |
| ACT-048 | m | actions_events.go:151,389 | Startup-failure runs emit no check suite and an empty conclusion; the merge gate's `startup_failure` branch is dead code | fixed — jobless startup failures enqueue requested/completed lifecycle events, persist their suite id, and conclude the suite `startup_failure` |
| ACT-049 | m | workflow_schedule.go:35 | The dispatcher re-reads and re-parses every workflow file of every repository every minute; parse errors swallowed | fixed — parse errors are now structured warnings with repository, file, cron, and timezone context; and the dispatcher no longer re-reads and re-parses every default-branch workflow every minute: a `scheduleIndex` cache holds each repo's parsed, cron-validated schedule keyed by the default-branch tip commit (`resolveRefSha`), rebuilt only when that tip moves (`buildScheduleIndex`), so a steady-state tick reduces to cron-matching against pre-parsed entries. The cache is pruned to live repos each tick (`retain`). Regression `TestScheduleIndexRebuildsOnlyOnTipChange` asserts one rebuild across two unchanged-tip ticks and a second rebuild after the tip advances |
| ACT-050 | m | dependabot_ingest.go:148 | Any non-numeric version segment is silently treated as not vulnerable | fixed — package-style prerelease/build identifiers participate in comparison and an unknown dialect fails safe by retaining the advisory rather than suppressing it |
| ACT-051 | m | workflows_msg.go:280 | `runner.os`, `arch` and `name` are constants; the github context omits `workflow_ref` and `job_workflow_sha`, which are load-bearing for OIDC claims | fixed — workflow/job workflow ref and SHA fields now reach the runner context, including reusable-workflow identity; and runner OS/architecture/name are now late-bound to the agent that actually leases the queued message: the job message is built runner-agnostically (`runnerContextData(nil)`) and the broker rewrites `contextData.runner` (os/arch/name plus OS-appropriate tool_cache/temp) to the leasing agent at delivery in `pullPendingMessage` (`rebindRunnerContext`, idempotent so a requeue rebinds afresh). OS/arch derive from the agent's self-reported labels, falling back to its OS description. Regressions `TestRunnerContextDerivedFromAgent` and `TestLeasedJobRebindsRunnerContext` |
| ACT-052 | m | artifacts.go:966 | The cache download token — documented as the sole access control — is compared with `!=`; no eviction, and `LastAccessedAt` is faked | fixed — constant-time token comparison and persisted restore-time `LastAccessedAt` landed earlier; finalizing a cache now enforces a per-repository budget (`maxRepoCacheBytes`, GitHub's 10 GiB default; a per-instance field so tests drive it small) via `evictRepoCacheOverLimit`, which deletes least-recently-used finalized entries (oldest `LastAccessedAt`, id breaking ties) until the repo is back under budget, byte-deletes outside the lock. LRU-order regression test |
| ACT-091 | B | actions.go | `ActionDownloadInfo` returned `"token": "x-access-token"` — the literal *username* half of the Basic credential, mistaken for the token — beside a hardcoded `"expiresAt": "2099-01-01T00:00:00Z"`, the same far-future placeholder another test in this package explicitly bans. The runner dutifully sent `Basic base64("x-access-token:x-access-token")`. The tarball route also read only Bearer, so a real token in the shape the runner sends would have been refused too | fixed — the download info mints a job token for the plan the caller already authenticated as, with its real expiry, and the route accepts the Basic download credential as well as a Bearer job token. ACT-003's private-repository scoping is unchanged and now covered through the Basic form |
| ACT-092 | m | auth.go | `authenticateRunner` reports `missing runner bearer token` identically for an absent Authorization header and for one carrying a scheme it does not read, which is what made the tarball failure look like an anonymous request for two CI cycles | fixed — absent, malformed and unsupported-scheme credentials are distinct failures with regression coverage |
| ACT-093 | B | auth.go, Dockerfile.runner | Actions Runner 2.336 signs its client assertion with PS256 while the server accepted only RS256, so the current official runner could register but not establish a session. Its bundled .NET 8.0.28 runtime also carries five fixed HIGH vulnerabilities, and replacing that runtime beneath the precompiled assemblies is binary-incompatible | fixed — the assertion verifier accepts and independently verifies both PS256/PSS and legacy RS256/PKCS#1 signatures. Both runner images rebuild the immutable 2.336.0 source commit against the digest-pinned .NET 8.0.423 SDK and patched 8.0.29 runtime, with a fully content-hashed NuGet graph, then retain only the checksum-verified official scripts and Node runtimes around that rebuilt binary tree. The real runner harness pins registration, sessions, jobs, matrices, outputs, artifacts, cancellation, service containers and dispatcher-spawned ephemeral execution |
| ACT-094 | M | workflow_schedule.go | The schedule dispatcher ran the per-minute scan inline on the ticker goroutine and recomputed the next boundary afterwards, so a scan overrunning a minute silently skipped every intervening minute's schedules | fixed — the dispatcher threads a minute cursor and `fireSchedulesThrough` replays every whole minute in `(lastFired, now]`, bounded by a ten-minute catch-up and deduped by the per-minute firing claim; a regression drives a nine-minute overrun and asserts the skipped due minutes fire exactly once |
| ACT-095 | M | workflow_schedule.go | A timezone'd schedule deduped its firing on the UTC minute, so a DST fall-back — the same wall-clock minute at two UTC instants — fired the workflow twice | fixed — the claim is keyed on the reconstructed civil minute in the schedule's zone, collapsing both instants to one claim; a regression fires the two 01:30-local instants and asserts a single run |
| ACT-096 | M | workflow_schedule.go, persistence.go | The firing claim was written before the run was created and never released, so a transient submit failure consumed the occurrence with no run and no retry | fixed — `fireScheduledWorkflow` distinguishes transient failures and the caller releases the claim via `ReleaseScheduleFiring` so another replica or attempt can retry, while permanent conditions keep the claim; a regression re-claims a released firing |
| ACT-097 | m | workflow_schedule.go | `fireScheduledWorkflow` re-fetched git storage with no nil guard and passed it to `resolveRefSha`, which dereferences a nil storer if a concurrent refresh dropped the entry | fixed — a nil storer is logged and skipped before use |
| ACT-102 | M | webhooks_payloads.go | every commit in a `push` webhook hard-coded empty `added`/`removed`/`modified`, so consumers that branch on changed files (CI path filters, deploy bots) saw nothing | fixed — each commit is diffed against its first parent to fill the three arrays (a root commit reports all files as added). Verified: the push webhook test asserts the pushed file appears in `added` |
| ACT-103 | m | webhooks_payloads.go, gh_hooks_rest.go | the `ping` webhook payload omitted `sender`, which GitHub includes on every event | fixed — `buildPingPayload` takes the acting user and emits `sender`; both the create-hook auto-ping and the manual ping pass it. Verified: the ping test asserts `sender.login` |
| ACT-104 | m | webhooks.go | automatic delivery retries reused one `X-GitHub-Delivery` GUID across all three attempts, so a deliveries list showed multiple rows under one GUID, impossible on real GitHub | fixed — each attempt mints a fresh GUID (retries stay flagged as redeliveries), matching GitHub's one-GUID-per-delivery model |
| ACT-105 | B | workflows_msg.go | a `pull_request` run whose head is a fork received every repository, organization and environment secret, though the fork contributor authors the workflow file that run executes | fixed — a fork-originated `pull_request` run is handed no secrets beyond `GITHUB_TOKEN`, matching GitHub's unconditional rule; `pull_request_target` is deliberately unaffected because it runs the base repository's definition in the base context. Verified: the fork case loses the repository secret while the same-repo and `pull_request_target` cases keep it |
| ACT-106 | B | workflow_call.go, workflows_msg.go | secrets re-widened at the second level of reusable-workflow nesting: a caller that passed an explicit `secrets:` map to workflow B, which then called C with `secrets: inherit`, gave C's jobs the repository's FULL secret set rather than the one secret B holds | fixed — call bindings carry a parent link and secret resolution walks the chain outside-in, so each call can only narrow what the enclosing call already granted. Verified: the leaf job of a two-level chain sees only the mapped name, never the caller's unmapped secret |
| ACT-107 | M | matrix.go | `include:` merging deviated from GitHub's documented algorithm two ways — an include could not overwrite a key an earlier include contributed, and combinations appended by an earlier include were themselves matched by later ones — so GitHub's own documentation example produced 5 jobs with wrong values instead of 6 | fixed — merge candidates are the Cartesian combinations only, and the overwrite rule is scoped to the ORIGINAL matrix dimensions; `ExpandMatrix(nil)` also no longer dereferences its nil argument. Verified: the documented fruit/animal/color example now reproduces byte-for-byte |
| ACT-108 | M | workflow_triggers.go, webhooks.go | `on.workflow_run.workflows` was parsed nowhere and filtered nothing, so a listener workflow started on EVERY run in the repository — including its own completion | fixed — the filter is parsed, the source run's name rides the trigger event, and a listener only starts for the workflows it names. Verified through the real event path: a `Release` completion no longer starts a `CI`-scoped listener, and the listener no longer restarts itself |
| ACT-109 | M | workflows.go | the implicit `success()` a job carries was only dropped for `always()` and `failure()`, so an `if: cancelled()` job was skipped by the dependency roll-up for the very cancellation that made its condition true — the case `CancelWorkflow` deliberately keeps such a job pending for | fixed — any of `success()`/`always()`/`cancelled()`/`failure()` replaces the default status check, the same rule the step path already applied, and the cancellation gate is now token-accurate instead of a substring search that a string literal could satisfy. Verified: an `if: cancelled()` job with a cancelled dependency dispatches, while a status-function-free `if:` still skips behind a failed dependency |

## STORE — persistence, storage, dqlite, S3

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STORE-001 | B | persistence.go:71 | `PutBatch` is used at 4 call sites against 398 `MustPut` and 206 `MustDelete`; `DeleteRepo` alone issues 54 independent transactions | fixed — a batch executor commits a whole write set in one transaction. `DeleteRepo` stages its whole cascade in that transaction; the repository re-key on `RenameRepo`/`TransferRepo` now stages the repos row and every one of its ~40 subresource buckets (secrets, collaborators, teams, hooks, checks, workflows, commit statuses, notification read state, scanning alerts, packages, env secrets/variables, pages, …) into one `persistBatch` via a batch-aware `moveRepoKeyLocked`, so a crash can no longer split a repository across its old and new names; and `StarRepo`/`UnstarRepo`, `CreateOrg`, `RemoveMembership`, `DeleteLabel`/`DeleteMilestone`, `CreateOrgInvitation`, `AddRepoCollaborator`/`RemoveRepoCollaborator`, `CreateRepoDeployKey`/`DeleteRepoDeployKey`, `DeleteTeam` (with child re-parenting), `RemoveOutsideCollaborator`, `CancelOrgInvitation`, `DeleteEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfig` and `CreateSecretScanningPushProtectionBypass` each commit their whole multi-bucket mutation atomically. Org-invitation acceptance (`consumeOrgInvitationLocked` — every invited-team join plus the invitation removal) and expiry (`reconcileOrgInvitationsLocked` — the abandoned pending-membership drop plus the invitation-failed write) each now commit in one transaction, and `RenameBranch` re-keys the branch protection and writes the repo's default-branch change together. The classic and v2 project deletions commit their whole subtree in one transaction — `DeleteProject` (v2) with its fields/items/views, and `DeleteProjectClassic`/`DeleteProjectColumn` with their columns and cards via batch-aware `deleteProjectCard/ColumnBatchLocked` helpers — so a crash can no longer orphan a child pointing at a deleted parent. The pull-request reviewer and label mutations that emit one issue-event per element (`RequestReviewers`, `RemoveRequestedReviewers`, `AddPullRequestLabels`, `SetPullRequestLabels`, `ClearPullRequestLabels`, `RemovePullRequestLabel`) and the matching issue label and assignee mutations (`SetIssueLabels`, `ClearIssueLabels`, `AddIssueLabels`, `RemoveIssueLabel`, `AddIssueAssignees`, `RemoveIssueAssignees`) now commit every event with the pull-request/issue row in a single `persistBatch` via batch-aware `recordPullRequestEvent/LabelEventBatchLocked` and `recordIssueEventWithIDsBatchLocked`, so the timeline can no longer diverge from the reviewer/label/assignee state across a crash. A further sweep converts the credential and multi-bucket cascade mutations: user-to-server/refresh token create, revoke, rotate and grant-revoke; `RevokeCredentials` across all four token buckets; `DeleteOAuthApp` with all its issued tokens; classroom and classroom-assignment deletion with their assignments/acceptances; and the org-scoped delete cascades (`DeleteCodeSecurityConfiguration`, `DeleteCustomProperty`, `DeleteNetworkConfiguration`, `DeleteIssueField`, `handleDeleteOrganizationRole`) plus repo-invitation acceptance — each now a single `persistBatch`. Also converted: `CreateCheckRun` (inline suite + run), `PruneEnvironmentPolicies` (branch policies + protection rules), `persistTeamExternalGroupsLocked` (org groups + team binding), `replaceOrganizationSCIMUser` (backing account + SCIM record, unlocking before any panic), `runPagesBuild`'s completion phase (the finished build record and the site's new status commit together so a crash can't mark the build built while the site row still says building), the standalone `DeleteDeployment` (which previously dropped the deployment row and each of its statuses as independent transactions — the shared `deleteDeploymentBatchLocked` now runs under a committed batch on that path too, matching the already-batched cascade caller), and `RecordAPIRequest` (the new api-insights record and its FIFO evictions now commit together so a crash can't leave the durable ring over its cap or missing the just-served request). Release deletion is atomic on both paths: the single-release `Delete` and the repo-scoped `DeleteAllForRepo` each stage the release row and every one of its release-asset rows into one `persistBatch` via batch-aware `deleteReleaseBatchLocked`/`deleteAssetBatchLocked`, so a crash can no longer delete a release while orphaning its assets or drop assets while the release row survives. The GHES org rename `renameGHESOrganization` is inherently multi-phase (the per-repo `TransferRepo` cascade runs unlocked between two locked phases, so it cannot be one transaction), but its final re-key phase — every membership key move, the installation target-login updates, the org row and enterprise settings — now commits as a single `persistBatch` (unlocking before any panic) instead of ~2N independent membership transactions, so a crash can no longer split the org across its old and new login. The final sweep converted every remaining kv-only multi-bucket mutation: the DeleteRepo cascade's per-comment reaction deletes now stage into the cascade batch (closing a hole in the flagship atomic delete); SARIF uploads commit analysis + every alert + the upload row in one transaction; package-version create/delete/restore batch their file/version/package rows; createRepoLocked accepts a caller batch and stages its five default discussion categories with the repo row, which also makes CreateTemporaryFork (advisory + fork repo + categories) and PublishCodespace (repo + codespace, closing the permanently-unpublishable crash window) single-transaction; issue create/lock/unlock/comment and comment deletion commit with their timeline events and reaction cleanup; Copilot seat grant/cancel batch the expiry prune with the seat writes; discussion answer re-marking is atomic; RecordIssueEvent builds fully and puts once; SeedDefaultUser commits user + token together; and the commit-comment, PR-review-comment and release deletion stores stage their reaction deletes with the row deletes. What remains non-batch is so by design, not omission: the S3 purge/rename phases (tombstone-and-resume with compensation), CreateCodespace's unlocked container start (reconciler-covered), object-store byte interleavings with explicit rollback, per-invitation reconciliation, and SetMembership (self-healing via the invitation reconciler) |
| STORE-002 | B | persistence.go:400 | Mutators change memory before persisting and `log.Fatalf` on failure — `os.Exit(1)` from a handler with the lock held and the mutation half-applied | fixed — `MustPut`/`MustDelete` panic with a typed failure rather than exiting, and recovery now reloads a revision-stable durable snapshot so an uncommitted memory mutation cannot survive the 500. If rollback cannot reach persistence, the store is poisoned and every later request retries recovery or fails 503 rather than serving half-applied memory. Several such multi-step mutations are now single `persistBatch` commits (repo re-key on rename/transfer, star/unstar, org creation and member removal, label/milestone deletion, org invitation create/cancel, repo collaborator add/remove, deploy key create/delete, team deletion with child re-parenting, outside-collaborator removal, enterprise code-security config deletion, secret-scanning bypass creation, org-invitation acceptance and expiry, branch rename's protection re-key, classic/v2 project deletion with their full column/card/field/item/view subtree, and pull-request reviewer/label mutations that batch their per-element issue-events with the PR row); every remaining kv-only independent-transaction mutation has now been converted (see STORE-001 for the final sweep's enumeration), so the recovery contract holds everywhere: a mutation either commits whole or the revision-stable reload reflects none of it, and what stays multi-phase does so with explicit tombstone/compensation designs rather than bare sequential writes |
| STORE-003 | B | s3fs.go:584 | `s3File.Lock()` returns nil; go-git relies on it for ref compare-and-set and `packed-refs` rewrites | fixed — a real per-object-key exclusion in process (reference-counted, a bounded wait that errors rather than deadlocking) plus a durable TTL lock table when shared state exists. With persistence disabled there is no shared durable state and so no second replica, which makes the in-process lock the whole lock — stated as the rule, not a fallback. If the durable store is unreachable `Lock` errors rather than proceeding |
| STORE-004 | B | s3fs.go:356,146 | `Chroot` shares one active-file map across every repository keyed by chroot-relative paths — repo A's read returns repo B's bytes | fixed — the staging map is keyed by the bucket-absolute object key rather than the chroot-relative path, so identical relative names in different repositories no longer collide. Pre-fix the test reads repository B's bytes through repository A |
| STORE-005 | B | store_repos.go:336 | Rename re-registers the same path-bound storer under the new key, so all later git I/O targets the vanished prefix | fixed — a path-bound storer is reopened against the new prefix after the bytes move; an in-memory storer holds its objects and is only re-keyed, because reopening it would silently empty the repository. The reopen precedes any map mutation so a failure aborts cleanly |
| STORE-006 | B | store_repos.go:310 | The rename aborts only on `EEXIST`, which POSIX `rename(2)` never returns; every real failure is ignored | fixed — rename and ownership transfer share one validated move path; any `os.Rename` failure aborts, the destination parent is created first, and path-backed storers are rebound so later refs address the moved bytes |
| STORE-007 | B | store_orgs.go:227, store.go:1147 | A partial cascade writes a permanent poison pill: the server refuses to boot forever after the next restart, with no repair path | fixed — the cascade now reaches the organization's repositories, which is where the orphan rows came from, under a durable deletion intent committed in one transaction. On boot an org-owned repo with no organization row is tolerated **only** when a matching intent exists, and is then finished; without one it still fails loudly, because then it really is corruption |
| STORE-008 | B | store.go:2752 | ~70 ID counters rebuilt as `max(surviving)+1`; for attestations and package files that ID is the S3 object key, so a new entity inherits deleted bytes | fixed for 52 of ~70 counters — every `Put` records a durable per-bucket high-water mark **before** the row, so an interruption can only skip identifiers, never reuse one; load takes `max(recomputed, durable)`. Covers the object-key counters the finding names. Not covered: buckets whose key is not the entity identifier — those need a different key scheme, not a different counter. Pre-existing databases keep the old behaviour until the first write per bucket |
| STORE-009 | B | store.go:930 | The in-memory graph is the source of truth with no read-through, invalidation or leader election, while the code assumes multiple replicas | fixed — every metadata transaction advances a monotonic shared revision; before serving a request a dqlite replica detects peer revisions and reloads until the revision is stable across the complete durable-state inventory. Store reconciliation preserves explicit process-local runner/session/log state and locally owned active workflows; the separate Actions artifact/cache index now follows the same revision and preserves in-flight uploads. Refresh failures return 503. Independent database connections prove organizations, Classrooms and Actions metadata propagate while local runtime state survives |
| STORE-010 | B | store.go:1554 | Boot performs a write pass that force-cancels every non-completed run — against a shared database one replica starting cancels another's live runs | fixed — the cancel-and-rewrite pass runs only when the persistence layer is exclusively owned (a local SQLite file). Against a dqlite quorum the runs belong to peers and are left untouched. When it does run it is one batched transaction rather than one write per workflow |
| STORE-011 | B | dqlite-node/main.go:108 | No auth, no TLS, hijack error discarded, and an unbuffered send from a handler started before the node exists | fixed — the upgrade is authenticated with a constant-time shared-secret comparison; that independently generated cluster secret also derives a deterministic Ed25519 TLS identity, so transport is TLS 1.3 and a client rejects another cluster. Rotation replaces both credentials. Hijack errors are logged, delivery is shutdown/timeout bounded, signal context precedes the server, shutdown is graceful, the port is configurable, and release CI compiles the real dqlite build |
| STORE-012 | B | persistence.go:39 | No schema version, no migration runner; rows are raw marshalled structs so a rename decodes to zero and a downgrade drops fields | fixed — a `schema_meta` table, an ordered migration list, and a hard refusal to start on a version this build does not understand |
| STORE-013 | B | store_codespaces.go:182, timeline.go:210, store_repos.go:249 | The global store lock is held across `docker pull` (180s), S3 prefix operations (120s) and every Raft round-trip | fixed — Codespace creation, workspace export, runtime deletion, repository storage creation, fork copying and repository deletion perform external work outside the global lock behind identity/name reservations, with blocking-backend regressions proving unrelated reads continue. Rename/transfer commit their whole re-key in a single transaction instead of ~40 independent persistence rounds while the lock is held, and their filesystem move is a constant-time `os.Rename`. The last slow case — the S3 object-prefix rename — now runs outside the lock too: `renameRepoS3` reserves the target name and records a crash-recoverable `pending_renames` intent under the lock, copies the object graph outside the lock (both prefixes coexist so old-name readers keep working), swaps the metadata under the lock, then purges the old prefix; `finishInterruptedRenames` finishes either side of a crash at startup. A blocking-backend regression proves an unrelated read proceeds while the copy blocks, and a reload regression proves the recovery path. No global-lock round-trip now spans a `docker pull`, an S3 prefix operation or a Raft round-trip |
| STORE-014 | B | store_repos.go:368 | `DeleteRepo` destroys bytes before metadata with an early return at each of six steps and no compensation | fixed — the durable intent now contains every external cleanup target, including Codespaces, package/CodeQL/attestation/Pages/release bytes, Actions artifacts/caches/logs and git storage. Repository, workflow/timeline and all cascade sub-store metadata commit in one transaction, then external data is removed outside the lock. Any partial cleanup is idempotently replayed before the intent clears |
| STORE-015 | B | store_codespaces.go:270,686 | A workspace guard makes codespaces permanently undeletable, and `DeleteRepo` propagates the error — the repository can never be deleted | fixed — a scratch export this server created is removed, while the repository's own git directory that the codespace merely borrows is left alone; deleting it would have destroyed the repository. Only a mount that is neither still errors |
| STORE-016 | M | all store files | No optimistic concurrency anywhere; the pattern exists on one type and was not generalized | fixed — Git ref-derived mutations now have storage-level optimistic concurrency across REST and Git transports, with stale update/delete conflicts and exclusive creation. And generic metadata resources now get optimistic concurrency on writes too: every GET already returned a strong content-derived ETag (writeJSON `"sha256(body)"` + If-None-Match/304), and a reusable `checkIfMatch` helper now enforces `If-Match` on mutations — the current resource's ETag (the same representation a GET returns) must match or the write is rejected 412, so a stale editor cannot clobber a concurrent update; an absent If-Match stays unconditional as on GitHub. Applied to the primary generic resources — issues, issue comments, pull requests, repositories, labels and milestones — and any other update handler adopts it in one line. Regression `TestIssueUpdateOptimisticConcurrency` (matching ETag succeeds, stale/post-update ETag 412, wildcard and absent unconditional) |
| STORE-017 | M | gh_repos_git.go:688 | Zero `CheckAndSetReference` calls against 20+ `SetReference` sites — concurrent pushes silently lose one another | fixed — content writes, ref updates, merge, merge-commit, squash and rebase all compare against the exact ref they read; create requires atomic absence and delete compares the old value. Every repository storer serializes ref operations in-process and through the shared dqlite lease manager, covering HTTP/SSH pushes as well as REST. An AST ratchet forbids unconditional writes in derived-ref functions and deterministic races prove a competing ref is never overwritten or deleted |
| STORE-018 | M | s3fs.go:211,369 | Rename is copy-then-delete with no rollback; a failed delete reports failure after the copy succeeded | fixed — failure to delete the source now compensates by deleting the copied destination, and reports a combined error if compensation itself fails |
| STORE-019 | M | object_bytes.go:18 | The byte-store interface is `[]byte`-only in both directions, and no upload route has a size limit | fixed — every request-body read is now bounded: the shared JSON decode path (`decodeJSONBody`/`decodeJSONBodyOptional` and the label decoder) caps at 25 MiB, and the binary-upload routes (release assets, container blobs/manifests, CodeQL databases, SARIF, markdown, branch protection, enterprise/user endpoints) wrap `r.Body` in `http.MaxBytesReader`, so no route buffers an unbounded body and an oversized one gets 413; and the byte-store interface is no longer `[]byte`-only: `actionsByteStore` now offers `PutStream(io.Reader)` and `GetStream() io.ReadCloser` alongside the whole-value forms. The S3 backend implements them — `PutStream` stages the reader to a temp file (never the heap) while hashing it, then uploads with the SHA-256 in metadata; `GetStream` returns the object body as a stream. The object-store download path streams end-to-end (`servePackageFile` now `GetStream`+`io.Copy`, Content-Length from the recorded size, so a large package download no longer allocates its whole size in RAM). Uploads whose object key or checksum derives from the full body (container blob digests, CodeQL DB keys) necessarily still read it, but bounded by MaxBytesReader. Round-trip regression `TestByteStoreStreamingRoundTrip` |
| STORE-020 | M | object_bytes.go:56, persistence.go:255 | No checksum on any S3 put or get; `synchronous(NORMAL)` means acknowledged writes can be lost with no stated contract | fixed — new S3 objects carry a SHA-256 metadata checksum and reads verify it before returning bytes (legacy objects stay readable for migration); and the single-node SQLite store now runs journal_mode(WAL) with synchronous(FULL), fsyncing the WAL at every commit so an acknowledged write survives an OS crash or power loss, not only a process crash, with the contract documented at the connection (dqlite deployments get durability from Raft replication). Regression asserts the pragmas |
| STORE-021 | M | store_copilot_spaces.go:93 + 9 more | Getters return live pointers that writers mutate in place; the `Stargazers` map case is a hard panic, and blind writes resurrect deleted rows | fixed — Gist and Codespace reads now return deep snapshots, their mutations persist a clone before publishing it, and repository updates clone nested maps/slices before persistence. The issue-search handler no longer reads a matched issue's/PR's mutable `title`/`body`/`updated_at` off the live pointer after releasing the store lock (the scorer and sorter now use fields snapshotted under the lock at gather time; the JSON builders already re-lock), closing a data race with a concurrent `UpdateIssue` that `TestStressReaderWriterLockGraph` intermittently caught. The secret-scanning and security-advisory families now do the same: `GetSecretScanningAlert`, `GetSecurityAdvisoryByGHSA` and `ListSecurityAdvisories` return detached clones (`cloneSecretScanningAlert`/`cloneSecurityAdvisory` copy the slice and time-pointer fields), so a reader can no longer race a concurrent update or mutate the stored row; the writers were re-pointed at the live row (`UpdateSecretScanningAlert` now re-fetches by `RepoKey`/`Number` and hands back a fresh snapshot, and the advisory PATCH handler re-reads after the keyed `UpdateSecurityAdvisory`), with new detachment tests pinning both. `GetOrgInvitation` (cloning `TeamIDs`) and `GetOrgInteractionLimit` (all-value shallow copy) in the teams/people family are likewise detached, again pinned by a test. The code-scanning family follows the secret-scanning shape exactly: `GetCodeScanningAlert` returns a `cloneCodeScanningAlert` snapshot and `UpdateCodeScanningAlert` re-fetches the live row by `RepoKey`/`Number` and hands back a fresh snapshot, pinned by a test. The dependabot alert family is the same again: `GetDependabotAlert` returns a `cloneDependabotAlert` snapshot and `UpdateDependabotAlert` re-fetches the live row by key, covering both its update handlers (the alerts endpoint and the security-review dismissal). So all three code-security alert families plus advisories, invitations and interaction limits are now read-detached; `GetAttestation` (cloning its `Bundle` and `SubjectDigests`) is too, as are `GetPackageVersion` (cloning its `Metadata` map), `GetPackageFile` and `GetDiscussionCategory` (all-value shallow copies) — all with read-only callers and no mutate-through writer. The issues family's `GetLabel`/`GetLabelByName` and `GetIssueEvent` (all-value), `GetMilestone`/`GetMilestoneByNumber` (a `cloneMilestone` copying `DueOn`/`ClosedAt`), and `GetDiscussionComment` (copying `LastEditedAt`) are detached too; their writers are already keyed (e.g. `UpdateLabel(id, fn)`). `GetDiscussion`/`GetDiscussionByNumber` (a `cloneDiscussion` copying `LastEditedAt`/`PublishedAt`) join them, their eight callers all render-only. The whole classic-projects family is done: `GetProjectClassic`/`GetProjectColumn`/`GetProjectCard` return copies, and every mutate-through writer (`UpdateProjectClassic`/`UpdateProjectColumn`/`MoveProjectColumn`/`UpdateProjectCard`/`MoveProjectCard`/`ConvertProjectCardToIssue`) re-fetches the live row by id — reassigning the parameter for the stateful moves and returning a fresh snapshot for the value-returning ones. `GetMembership` (all-value shallow copy; its 24 callers only read `State`/`Role` and writes go through the keyed `SetMembership`) is detached too, as are `GetTeam`/`GetTeamByID` (a `cloneTeam` deep-copying `MemberIDs`/`MaintainerIDs`/`RepoNames`/`RepoPermissions`) — verified that all 31 team-getter callers only read identifier fields while every team write goes through the keyed `UpdateTeam` or the live `st.Teams` row. `GetOrg`/`GetOrgByID` (a `cloneOrg` copying the `MembersCanCreateRepositories` bool pointer) are detached too — all 144 org-getter callers read only, and org writes go through the keyed `UpdateOrg` or the live `st.Orgs` row; a test that had reached through `GetMembership` to set `m.Public = true` was fixed to call `SetMembershipPublic`. `GetPullRequestReview` (a `cloneReview` copying `SubmittedAt`/`DismissedAt`) is detached too — its nine callers read only and review writes go through the keyed Update/Submit/Dismiss methods. `GetIssue`/`GetIssueByNumber` (a `cloneIssue` deep-copying `AssigneeIDs`/`LabelIDs` + `ClosedAt`) are detached too — all 34 issue-getter callers read only, and issue writes go through the keyed `UpdateIssue(id, fn)`; the unexported `issueByRepo*` under-lock helpers keep returning the live row for internal mutation. `GetPullRequest`/`GetPullRequestByNumber` (a `clonePullRequest` deep-copying the four ID slices + `ClosedAt`/`MergedAt`) are detached too — all 21 callers read only, writes go through the keyed `UpdatePullRequest(id, fn)`, and the unexported `findPRByRepoNumberLocked` under-lock helper keeps returning the live row. The architecture ratchet prevents backsliding. The enterprise families are done too: `GetEnterpriseTeam` returns a `cloneEnterpriseTeam` snapshot (deep-copying `GroupID`/`MemberIDs`/`SelectedOrgLogins`) and all eight `*EnterpriseTeam` writers re-fetch the live team by id — `UpdateEnterpriseTeam` mutates the live row and syncs a fresh snapshot back into the caller's pointer for rendering, the member/org mutators reassign to the live row, and the read-only `Is`/`List` methods read live state; `GetEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfig` returns a `cloneEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfig` snapshot and its mutate-through writers (`TouchEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfig`, `SetEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfigDefault`) commit the caller's edits onto the live row, both pinned by new detachment tests. The repository family is done too: `GetRepo`/`GetRepoByFullName`/`GetRepoByID` now return a `cloneRepo` snapshot (deep-copying `ArchivedAt`/`InteractionLimitExpiry`/`HasDiscussions`, the `Topics` slice and the `Stargazers` map; the shared `Owner *User` cross-reference stays shallow), so a reader can no longer race `StarRepo`/`UnstarRepo`'s in-place `Stargazers` map write — the acute fatal concurrent-map-access case — nor leak a write back into the store. The copy-on-write `UpdateRepo(owner,name,fn)` already swapped in a fresh row rather than mutating in place, so the update path was safe; the one handler that mutated a getter result directly (`setRepoLFS`) was rerouted through `UpdateRepo`, and an audit confirmed no other through-getter repo mutations, no repo-pointer identity comparisons, and that every `*Repo`-parameter store method reads only immutable identifier/scalar fields; a `TestRepoGetIsDetached` pins the star-map/topics/scalar detachment. Those smaller singletons are done too: `GetPackage`/`GetDeletedPackage` (a `clonePackage` copying `DeletedAt`; the live rows are mutated in place by `recomputeVersionCountLocked`/`DeletePackage`/`RestorePackage`), `GetDependabotUserSecret` (a value copy; `UpsertDependabotUserSecret` mutates an existing secret in place), and `GetCodeScanningDefaultSetup` (copying the `Languages` slice; `SetCodeScanningDefaultSetup` stamps `UpdatedAt` in place) now return detached snapshots, and `GetCodeScanningAutofix`/`GetRepoSubscription` return defensive value copies even though their writers already swap in a fresh row — all pinned by `TestSmallStoreGetsAreDetached`/`TestRepoGetIsDetached`. Every exported single-object store getter now returns a detached snapshot except two documented deliberate exceptions: `GetCodeQLDatabase` (write-once row that `UpsertCodeQLDatabase` replaces by pointer-swap, holding a potentially large `Content` blob that cloning on every metadata read would needlessly duplicate) and `GetGitStorage` (a `gitStorage.Storer` backend handle, not a mutable data row). A follow-up sweep caught the single-object getters that return a live element from a `for … range` loop rather than a direct map index (the earlier sweep only matched `return st.X[key]`): `GetCopilotSpace`/`ListCopilotSpaces` now return a `cloneCopilotSpace` deep snapshot (copying the Collaborators/Resources slices and each resource's Metadata map) and `SaveCopilotSpace` publishes the caller's mutated snapshot back onto the live row (copy-on-write), closing the mutate-through path its handlers use; `GetCodeSecurityConfiguration`/`GetCodeSecurityConfigurationByName` return a `cloneCodeSecurityConfiguration` snapshot (four optional-scalar pointers) with the keyed `UpdateCodeSecurityConfiguration` and its handler already rendering the writer's fresh return; `GetDiscussionCategoryByName` (the missed sibling of the already-detached `GetDiscussionCategory`), `GetEnvBranchPolicy` and `GetEnvProtectionRule` return value-copy snapshots. `GetAppDelivery` is documented as a deliberate live-pointer exception (deliveries are write-once — appended, never mutated; a redelivery appends a new record — and each carries full request/response payloads cloning would needlessly copy). The `List*` methods that return slices of live element pointers are now being detached at the source too, starting with the acute pointer/slice/map-bearing element types whose writers mutate in place: every `[]*Repo` list (`ListForks`, `ListReposByOwner`/`ForOrg`/`ForUser`/`ForAuthUser`, `ListPublicRepos`, `ListTeamRepos`, `ListRepoSubscriptionsForUser`, `ListCodeSecurityConfigurationRepos`, `ListEnterpriseCodeSecurityConfigRepos`, `ListOrgReposForProperties`, `ListOrgImmutableReleasesRepos`) now returns `snapshotRepos(...)` clones — closing the fatal `Stargazers` concurrent-map race on the list path — and `ListIssues`, `ListPullRequests`, the `[]*Comment` lists (`ListIssueComments`/`ListRepoIssueComments`/`ListComments`/`ListCommentsFor`), `ListMilestones`, the `[]*Org` lists (`ListOrgsByUser`/`ListPublicOrgsByUser`/`ListOrgsAll`/`ListEnterpriseTeamOrgs`) and the `[]*Team` lists (`ListTeamsByUser`/`ListChildTeams`/`ListTeams`/`ListTeamsForRepo`/`ListTeamsWithOrgRole`) return `snapshot*` clones via their existing element clone helpers (all under the read lock). The security-alert, discussion, package and enterprise families followed: `ListCodeScanningAlerts`/`ByOrg`, `ListDependabotAlerts`/`ByOrg`, `ListSecretScanningAlerts`/`ByOrg`/`ByUser`, `ListDiscussions`, `ListPackages`, `ListPackageVersions`, `ListAttestations`, `ListCodeSecurityConfigurations`, `ListEnterpriseTeams` and the `[]*OrgInvitation` lists (`ListPendingOrgInvitations`/`ListFailedOrgInvitations`/`ListPendingOrgInvitationsForTeam`) each now return `snapshot*` clones via their existing element clone helper. The value-only-element `List*` methods were then swept too — a generic `snapshotSlice[T]` shallow-copies each element (a full snapshot for a struct with no slices/maps/pointers), applied to the check-suite, code-quality, env-policy, repo-invitation, autolink, artifact-storage, analysis, copilot-seat, dependabot-user-secret, discussion-category, issue-event, label, membership, package-file, classic-project, repo-activity, deploy-key, workflow-file, gist-comment and remaining user/webhook/gist-history lists — and the `[]*User` families got a `cloneUser` deep-copying `StarredRepos`/`Emails`/`ExternalIdentities` (StarRepo mutates the map in place). Fixing `handleSetWorkflowState` to route through the keyed `SetWorkflowFileState` rather than mutate a resolved snapshot closed the one caller that relied on the leak. Every exported single-object getter and every `List*` method now returns a detached snapshot, with four documented deliberate live-pointer exceptions where the row is genuinely write-once and cloning would copy large payloads needlessly: `GetCodeQLDatabase`/`ListCodeQLDatabases`, `GetGitStorage`, and the append-only webhook-delivery reads (`GetAppDelivery`/`ListAppDeliveries`/`ListMarketplaceDeliveries`/`ListDeliveries`). The `TestReliabilityDebtOnlyShrinks`-style architecture ratchet and the per-family detachment tests guard against backsliding |
| STORE-022 | M | store_projects_classic.go:34, store_rulesets.go:28 | `json:"-"` silently means "not durable": board ordering and ruleset version history are lost on reload | fixed — ruleset history and its next-version counter are durable and reload-tested, and classic project board ordering is now durable too: `ProjectColumn.Position`/`ProjectCard.Position` carry json tags, are persisted on create and on every column/card move, and are restored and sorted on reload. `TestPersistenceReload_ProjectBoardOrder` pins that a reorder whose position order differs from id order survives reload |
| STORE-023 | M | store_notifications.go:554, store_repo_reads.go:86 | Whole unbounded collections stored as one row and rewritten on every mutation — including on every `git clone` | fixed — both collections that auto-grew without bound (and were re-serialised in full on every mutation, so the per-mutation cost itself was unbounded) are now capped, making that cost O(cap). The clone-traffic actor set (`store_repo_reads.go`) is capped at `maxTrafficActors` so anonymous clones — the `git clone` path called out here — cannot grow it without limit. The per-user notification read-marker set `ReadThreadIDs`, which auto-grows on every thread a user reads and is the map re-serialised on every `MarkThreadRead`, is now capped at `maxReadThreadIDs` and prunes the oldest markers by read-at time once it grows a slack beyond the cap (batched so the prune amortises to O(log n) per mark; GitHub ages notifications out, so dropping the oldest is faithful) — `TestMarkThreadReadBoundsReadMarkers` pins that the oldest are dropped and the newest retained. The other per-user maps (`DismissedThreadIDs`, `Subscriptions`, `RepoLastReadAt`) grow only with explicit user actions or distinct-repo count, not automatically, so they are human-bounded rather than an unbounded/attacker-driven vector |
| STORE-024 | M | persistence.go:436, gh_api_insights.go:271 | `List` is the only read primitive and materializes whole buckets; one durable row per API request ever served | fixed — the request log's FIFO eviction was in-memory only: `RecordAPIRequest` capped `APIRequestRecords` but never deleted the evicted rows' durable `api_insights_requests` entries, so the bucket grew by one row per request forever (and the load path re-trimmed only in memory). Eviction now `MustDelete`s the evicted durable rows, and the load path `Delete`s any excess a pre-fix instance leaked, so the bucket converges to the cap. The cap is a per-Store field (`apiRequestRecordCap`, default `maxAPIRequestRecords`=10000) so a reload+runtime test drives it small and asserts the durable row count stays bounded to the newest-N. (`List` materialising whole buckets is the persistence design; bounding this bucket removes the growth this row is about.) |
| STORE-025 | M | store.go:958, persistence.go:211 | Sessions are never reaped and every logout scans and decodes every historical session row inside a write transaction | fixed — the scheduler now reaps expired login sessions hourly through a deterministic-clock, transactional deletion pass that survives restart and retries after persistence failure. And per-user logout no longer lists and decodes the whole durable session bucket: a durable secondary index (`login_sessions_by_user`, one row per session keyed `<userID>:<sessionStorageKey>`) is written in the same `persistBatch` as each session, so `DeleteLoginSessionsForUser` resolves a user's sessions with a bounded prefix scan (new `Persistence.ListPrefix`, an indexed `[prefix, prefix++)` range query). Because it reads durable state it revokes sessions this replica never cached, including ones created on other replicas — the property the old full scan provided and an in-memory index would have lost. The index is a superset of live sessions (Put adds atomically, so revocation never misses one; the bespoke OIDC back-channel transaction may leave a harmless stale row reclaimed at the next per-user purge), and `SetPersistence` backfills it once at startup so upgrades and drift self-heal. Reap/DeleteLoginSession/OIDC-delete drop index rows alongside their sessions. Cross-replica + backfill + disambiguation regressions in store_login_session_index_test.go. (The in-memory cache eviction still iterates the bounded in-memory map, which does no I/O.) |
| STORE-026 | M | store_packages.go:304, store_code_scanning.go:883 | Blob writes have no compensation and reuse IDs, silently aliasing orphaned objects | fixed — the CodeQL-database path already reclaims the superseded archive and compensates on metadata-persist failure (`UpsertCodeQLDatabase` deletes `previous` when its content-addressed path changes, with rollback), and package files use monotonic ids (no reuse). The remaining gap was `CreatePackageVersion`: a multi-file version wrote each file's bytes before committing any metadata, so a mid-loop write failure orphaned the bytes already written. It now tracks written blobs and deletes them (object store or local dir) on any write/mkdir failure before returning. Regression test with a byte store that fails the 2nd write asserts zero orphaned blobs and no committed metadata |
| STORE-027 | M | store_secret_scanning.go:228 + 3 | Bulk mutations validate inside the loop and commit a map-iteration-order subset before returning 422 | fixed — the remaining store-level bulk alert update validates and builds detached copies first, commits every durable row in one transaction, then publishes the result to memory |
| STORE-028 | M | store_orgs.go:247 + 4 | Delete paths enumerate secondary indexes, so soft-deleted packages orphan their S3 bytes and cancelled subscriptions leave access live | fixed — the package secondary-index half is fixed: `deleteRepoLocked` and `repoDeletionIntentLocked` iterated `PackagesByOwnerKey`, which a soft-delete prunes while keeping the row in the authoritative `st.Packages`, so a soft-deleted package's rows and file bytes were orphaned on repo delete. Both sites now iterate `st.Packages` filtered by owner (soft-deleted included). Regression test soft-deletes a package then deletes its repo and asserts the row is reclaimed. Those larger cascades are now done too: `deleteOrgMetadata` cascades org-directly-owned packages (rows, versions, files and their object bytes via the deletion intent) and cancels the org's Marketplace purchases; and admin user deletion (`handleAdminDeleteUser` via the new `deleteUserOwnedResourcesLocked`) cascades the user's repositories, directly-owned packages (+bytes), Marketplace purchases and org memberships, mirroring the org path, so a deleted account leaves no orphaned rows or object bytes. Regressions `TestDeleteOrgCascadesPackagesAndMarketplace` and `TestDeleteUserCascadesOwnedResources` |
| STORE-029 | M | store_workflow_files.go:51 | The primary key hashes the mutable repo name; rename re-persists under the old ID and the next registration duplicates the row | fixed — the repo-rename cascade (`moveRepoKeyLocked`) only rewrote `WorkflowFile.RepoFullName` while leaving the row keyed by `stableWorkflowFileID(oldName, path)`, so the next registration under the new name derived a different hash and inserted a duplicate. The cascade now re-keys each affected file to `stableWorkflowFileID(newName, path)` in both the map and persistence (snapshotting keys first to avoid mutating a map under iteration). Regression test renames a repo with a workflow file and asserts no stale-hash residue and no duplicate on re-registration |
| STORE-030 | M | store_workflow_files.go:180 | A discarded read error writes empty YAML over a good row, which the type's own comment says makes dispatch 422 forever | fixed — `DiscoverWorkflowFilesFromGit` now checks the error from `f.Contents()` and skips the file on failure instead of re-registering it with an empty body; a transient content read (e.g. a lazily-fetched S3-backed blob) no longer overwrites the good stored YAML. The next discovery re-reads it |
| STORE-031 | M | store_codespaces.go:372 | The encrypted codespace secret value is never referenced and the endpoint returns 201 | fixed — CodespaceSecret carries a Value field and CreateCodespaceSecret stores the decrypted plaintext on both the create and update paths; it is persisted only in the encrypted codespace_secrets bucket and never surfaced in an API response |
| STORE-032 | M | store_rulesets.go:269 | Three ruleset-suite endpoints discard their arguments and return nil | fixed — ref writes evaluate active/evaluate rulesets, persist official-shaped suites, and serve filtered/paginated list and detail endpoints with reload coverage |
| STORE-033 | M | store_secret_scanning.go:253 | `validateSecretScanningTransition` ends in an unconditional nil return, making its own state machine dead code | fixed — only same-state, open-to-resolved, resolved-to-resolved, and resolved-to-open transitions pass; every other state pair returns a validation error |
| STORE-034 | M | store_teams_people.go:208, store_copilot.go:42 | GET endpoints take the exclusive lock and perform durable deletes; a persist failure during a read kills the server | fixed — both halves resolved. The copilot-seat reads were made pure earlier; now the org-invitation reads (`ListPendingOrgInvitations`/`ListFailedOrgInvitations`/`GetOrgInvitation`) take a read lock and no longer run the reconcile state machine (durable deletes + `MustPut`) on a GET. The state machine — expire/consume/cancel with the durable membership side effects — now runs on the background schedule dispatcher (`ReconcileAllOrgInvitations`, recover-guarded since a background goroutine has no recover middleware), alongside the existing login-session reap. Consume already ran event-driven on membership activation, so only TTL-expiry timing shifts (≤ one tick). Regression tests: a read leaves an expired invitation un-failed and its pending membership intact, while the reconciler applies both durably. |
| STORE-035 | M | git_storage.go:27 | A transient S3 client failure is memoized for the process lifetime with no reset | fixed — initialization becomes sticky only after success (or when object storage is intentionally disabled), so transient discovery/credential failures retry |
| STORE-036 | M | persistence.go:386 | Unbounded retry treats every error as "waiting for quorum", including a malformed address map that can never succeed | fixed — `MustNewPersistence` retried every `NewPersistence` error forever (1s intervals) whenever dqlite was configured, so a misconfiguration (malformed `BLEEPHUB_DQLITE_ADDRESS_MAP`, missing transport secret, empty server set, bad encryption key) or a schema written by a newer build looped endlessly behind a "waiting for dqlite quorum" log. The unrecoverable config/schema/encryption paths now return a `permanentPersistenceError`, and `MustNewPersistence` fails fast on it (`log.Fatalf`) while still retrying the genuinely transient dial/ping/query failures that occur while a quorum forms. Regression tests cover the classifier and a malformed address map |
| STORE-037 | M | s3fs.go:93 vs :190 | `Open` misses the `NotFound` shape, and go-git discards the read error — a transient S3 failure reads as "ref does not exist" and a live branch is overwritten | fixed — verified the s3fs layer already maps only the definite-404 shapes (`GetObject`→`NoSuchKey`, `HeadObject`→`NoSuchKey`/`NotFound`) to `os.ErrNotExist` and wraps every other failure, and the atomic-ref wrapper propagates any non-`ErrNotExist` read error (`RemoveReferenceCAS` returns it; `CheckAndSetReference` delegates to go-git, which propagates non-NotExist errors), so a transient failure fails the CAS safely rather than overwriting a live branch. Locked the data-loss invariant in with a regression test that injects a retryable HTTP 500 via SDK middleware and asserts `Open`/`Stat` return a non-`ErrNotExist` error |
| STORE-038 | M | s3fs.go:181,261 | The active buffer is invisible to `Stat` and `ReadDir`; a failed `Close` leaves a phantom file and leaks memory | fixed — `Stat` reports staged size, `ReadDir` merges and deduplicates the shared staging namespace with S3, and `Close` removes the staging entry whether its flush succeeds or fails |
| STORE-039 | M | store_issues.go:126 | Every PR event is written first under the issue parent type, then rewritten — a crash in the window files it against an unrelated issue | fixed — issue events are built with their final ParentType (and extra fields) already set and persisted exactly once via buildIssueEventLocked + persistIssueEventLocked, so a pull-request event is never first written under the issue parent and rewritten. Reload regression asserts the durable parent type |
| STORE-040 | M | store_orgs.go:552 | `UpdateTeam` has no slug collision check, orphaning the shadowed team from every index-driven operation | fixed — the store validates a detached team copy before re-keying, so a rejected collision cannot corrupt either the primary row or slug index |
| STORE-041 | M | store_codespaces.go:175 | Container and workspace created before any durable record exists, with no startup reconciler | fixed — both halves closed. `reserveCodespace` already commits the durable "Provisioning" record before `CreateCodespace` starts the container (record precedes container, with a `cleanup()` on every failure path), and `loadFromPersistence` now reconciles the remaining gap: a codespace persisted as "Provisioning" is an interrupted-creation orphan whose container never survives a restart, so on boot it is transitioned to the resumable "Shutdown" state (whose start path re-provisions) and the heal is committed in one transaction. Settled codespaces are untouched. Regression `TestInterruptedCodespaceProvisioningReconcilesOnLoad` (orphan → Shutdown, durable across a second reload; an Available codespace undisturbed) |
| STORE-042 | M | store_packages.go:275 | Missing the "persistence implies object store" guard its sibling has, so metadata replicates while bytes stay on one node | fixed — `CreatePackageVersion` now enforces the same `st.persist != nil && st.ObjectByteStore == nil` guard its attestation and code-scanning siblings already carry: with persistence enabled, package bytes must go to the shared object store rather than a single node's local package directory (where replicas that see the replicated metadata could not read them). Regression test asserts the error fires with persistence enabled and no object store, using a non-empty PackageDataDir so it exercises this guard rather than the pre-existing "not configured" one |
| STORE-043 | M | store_projects_v2.go:782 | Field update re-mints every option ID, dangling every item's stored value | fixed — UpdateField now preserves the ID of any single-select option kept by name (the mutation input carries only names), minting fresh IDs only for genuinely new options, so an item's stored OptionID no longer dangles after a field edit. Regression asserts a kept option's ID is stable |
| STORE-044 | M | store_issues.go:718 + 3 | Caller slices and maps adopted by reference across the store boundary | fixed — the setters now clone rather than adopt caller-owned collections: `SetIssueLabels`/`SetPullRequestLabels` (LabelIDs), `UpsertDependabotOrgSecret`/`CreateCodespaceSecret` (SelectedRepoIDs, cloned once so both create and update branches are safe), `SetCopilotCodingAgentSelectedRepos` (SelectedRepositoryIDs) and `SetCopilotContentExclusion` (the rules map and its nested slices). Regressions mutate the caller's slice/map after the call and assert stored state is unaffected |
| STORE-045 | M | gh_actions_permissions.go:343 + branch_protection | In-place `s[:0]` filtering rewrites the backing array readers already hold | fixed — every in-place `[:0]` filter that operates on a *stored* slice (a concurrent reader could hold its backing array) now uses the full three-index reslice `[:0:0]`, forcing `append` to allocate a fresh array instead of overwriting the original: `s.store.PendingMessages` (×3: run-control + workflows), `OrgOIDCPropertyInclusions`, `Misc.keysByUser`, enterprise `team.MemberIDs`, hosted-runner `img.Versions`, codespace `access.SelectedUsernames`, runner-group `a.Labels` (×2), org-actions `p.SelfHostedRunnersSelectedRepoIDs`.
