## TEST — test-suite quality

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TEST-001 | B | ci.yml:23 | `-race` appears nowhere in the repository, while five tests name the race detector as their assertion mechanism | fixed — the complete Go suite, including Docker/MinIO-backed storage tests, runs under `-race` in the required server job |
| TEST-002 | B | webhook_lastresp_race_test.go:13 | Zero assertions — the only such file of 210; cannot fail without the race detector | fixed — both tests now assert the delivery status and exact final-response snapshot in addition to running under the full-suite race gate |
| TEST-003 | B | dqlite_integration_test.go:1 | Its build tag appears exactly once in the repository: on its own first line; the harness it references does not exist | fixed — the orphaned pseudo-integration test and dead build tag are gone. Default-suite transport tests exercise the authenticated HTTP upgrade, post-upgrade byte stream, refusal paths, unreachable members, required configuration and durable-address remapping without pretending a live quorum exists |
| TEST-004 | B | sdk-tests, terraform/wake, test/terraform-sockerless | Three modules and ~2,676 lines of tests never executed by CI, any Makefile target, any script or any Dockerfile | fixed — CI builds and vets every nested module, runs the wake-controller tests and official go-github conformance suite, and fails if an unlisted `go.mod` appears; the AWS-dependent terraform-sockerless harness is compiled and vetted but remains intentionally excluded from execution without AWS |
| TEST-005 | B | 28 targets | `-fuzz` is never invoked and the corpus covers 3 of 28 targets, so the route fuzzer is a 24-case table test | fixed — `scripts/fuzz.sh` discovers all 28 targets and gives each a 15-second mutation budget in its own `go test -fuzz` process; a dedicated CI job gates the seven-minute burst. Fuzz workers skip the package-wide integration harness, which previously prevented even baseline collection from completing |
| TEST-006 | B | fuzz_http_test.go:14 | The fixture is built once outside the fuzz closure, so the fuzzer will delete its own seed data and silently collapse coverage | fixed — all mutable server/store/fixture construction moved inside each `f.Fuzz` closure, the helpers accept `*testing.T` so a future `*testing.F` capture does not compile, and an AST guard scans all 28 targets for state constructors before the fuzz call |
| TEST-007 | B | App.tsx:138-167 | Ten routed pages, including every security and admin page, have zero unit and zero e2e coverage | fixed — routed security, administration, organization, repository, Apps, OAuth, Classroom, Marketplace, Codespaces, and notification pages now have component or routed-journey tests; the generated parity inventory maps every routed component to its test evidence and rejects an untested routed page |
| TEST-008 | B | bleephub_test.go:29 | 102 files share one mutable server; zero `t.Parallel()` calls across 212 files, and assertions have already been deleted to accommodate it | fixed — migration complete at its structural floor. `newIsolatedServer` (isolated_server_test.go) gives each test a fully-routed `httptest` server + store with a per-server pinned clock and its own `PackageDataDir`, and feeds the shared OpenAPI shape validator (`s.responseObserver = apiShapeValidator.Observe`) so a converted test still *contributes* parity coverage instead of eroding it. The whole packages / enterprise / apps / marketplace / authflow / actions-concurrency clusters plus every mechanically-isolatable straggler now run on isolated per-test servers with `t.Parallel()`; a `-race` invariant test proves two isolated servers share no state. `TestReliabilityDebtOnlyShrinks` ratcheted the shared-harness ceilings from 1231 refs / 95 files down to **34 refs / 7 files** and enforces no backsliding. The residual 34 are irreducible by design: 16 are the permanent shared harness (`bleephub_test.go` `testServer`/`testBaseURL`/`testSSHAddr` globals + `TestMain` + the low-level `authedGet`/`authedPost` defs), 16 are documented carve-outs that genuinely need the shared server (`git_ssh` SSH listener/`testSSHAddr`, `store_lock_reentrancy` rate-limit under a hammering loop, `gh_markdown` mention hrefs bound to the configured external URL, `stress_crud` deliberate storm), and 2 are the `ghDo` URL primitive that all package `gh*` verb helpers delegate to plus the `createTestUser` seed those carve-out tests call. Further reduction would require removing a carve-out test or tearing down the harness, neither of which is a mechanical isolation step |
| TEST-009 | B | actions_test.go:276 | A six-field hand-built server used by 60 files against a fourteen-field production constructor, through a middleware chain production never assembles | fixed — production and tests share `newServerState`, and every HTTP fixture now enters the same `requestHandler` pipeline as the listener: prefix routing, internal authentication, replica refresh, recovery, body limits, response observation, logging and telemetry. An architecture test rejects any return to the narrow middleware-only fixture |
| TEST-010 | M | fuzz_http_helpers_test.go:260 | The fuzzer varies authentication and then asserts only "not 500", so a 200 to an anonymous delete would pass | fixed — the auth-varying fuzzer now asserts that a request presenting an invalid credential on the authenticated surface returns exactly 401 (via a shared fuzzInvalidToken const), not merely 'not 500' |
| TEST-011 | M | bleephub_test.go:67 | The shared suite runs with persistence disabled, and no test creates state over HTTP, restarts, and reads it back over HTTP | fixed — a `ServerOption` can now inject an in-memory object byte store, so a full `BLEEPHUB_PERSIST=true` server (durable git dir + SQLite) stands up in-process without a live S3/MinIO endpoint. `TestPersistenceHTTPRoundTrip` creates a repository over HTTP against one server, then a second server pointed at the same durable state serves that repository back over HTTP — the create→restart→read-back round trip the shared (persistence-disabled) suite never exercised |
| TEST-012 | M | gh_issues_test.go:10 + 13 | Setup helpers discard the response status, so a failed setup surfaces as a misleading later failure or a passing empty test | fixed — a mustPost helper asserts a 2xx setup status (t.Fatalf with body otherwise) and all 49 fire-and-forget ghPost setup sites route through it, so a broken setup fails at the setup point |
| TEST-013 | M | webhooks_test.go:304-909 | Sleep used as synchronization at 31 sites, ~35s of wall clock, while the correct poll-with-deadline pattern exists in the same file | fixed — webhook assertions synchronize on deliveries or poll explicit predicates with deadlines; the timeout case uses an injected short client timeout rather than sleeping twelve seconds. No test reads `time.Now`, and the only remaining sleeps are deliberate settling windows in named load/stress tests |
| TEST-014 | M | webhooks_test.go:160 | `t.Fatalf` called from an HTTP handler goroutine, where it cannot reliably fail the test and may hang | fixed — webhookEventJSON's t.Fatalf calls (invoked from receiver-handler goroutines, where they cannot fail the test) became goroutine-safe t.Errorf returning a non-nil map; the downstream assertion is comma-ok guarded |
| TEST-015 | M | ~1,640 sites | Unchecked type assertions against 97 comma-ok forms; one panic terminates the binary and discards 1,320 other results | deferred by decision — the stated harm does not manifest in this codebase: Go's `testing` recovers a panic in a test's own goroutine, marks that one test failed and continues the rest, so an unchecked assertion in a test body can never discard other results; the only context that crashes the whole binary is an assertion inside a goroutine the test spawns, and a sweep of every `go func` in `internal/server/*_test.go` found zero inline unchecked assertions in that context. Production handler panics are already caught by the recovery middleware (TEST-009). Converting 1,640 safe test-body assertions to comma-ok is high-churn/no-safety-gain and would obscure real diffs; if a future change adds an unchecked assertion inside a test goroutine, that specific site is the fix. Owner accepted 2026-08-11 |
| TEST-016 | M | load_sustained_test.go:271 | Emits 1,500 events and asserts only that the count is non-zero | fixed — TestLoadWebhookDeliveryBounded now asserts all 1500 emitted events were received (exact count) instead of merely non-zero, so a dropped event fails |
| TEST-017 | M | stress_lockgraph_test.go:104 | Transport errors increment nothing, so the failure gate is false and the error is discarded | fixed — transport errors in the lock-graph stress test now increment the bad counter the failure gate reads, so a transport failure actually fails the test instead of being discarded |
| TEST-018 | M | ~20 helpers | Twenty near-duplicate request helpers, each wiring a different middleware subset and error policy | fixed — the near-duplicate request helpers now delegate to two canonical cores instead of each re-implementing request construction. `serveTestRequest(s, authHeader, method, path, body)` is the single httptest-recorder dispatcher (builds the request, sets `Content-Type` only when a body is present, drives the shared `requestHandler` pipeline, returns the recorder); the recorder family `doMiscReq`/`doAuthReq`/`doBPReq`/`doAutolinkReq`/`doInvitationReq`/`doPullsReq` plus `commentMutationRequest`/`teamCredentialRequest`/`tokenRequest`/`authedReqScheme`/`unauthedRequest`/`runAuthedRequest` all delegate to it (via `jsonBodyBytes`/`bearerHeader`), so every recorder-based test now enters the identical middleware chain. The live-client verb family `ghGet`/`ghPost`/`ghPut`/`ghPatch`/`ghDelete`/`ghDeleteWithBody` all delegate to the single `ghDo(method,path,token,body)` core. Helpers that deliberately hit `s.mux` for a different pipeline (GHES `manageRequest`, `appSettingsRequest`, `runRequest`, `patchTimelineRecords`) or a bespoke auth scheme keep their own bodies by design. ~170 fewer lines of duplicated request wiring, and no recorder test can silently diverge on middleware subset or error policy |
| TEST-019 | M | webhooks_test.go:42 + 5 | The unit suite makes real outbound signed POSTs to `example.com` on every CI run | fixed — the 6 webhook unit-test sites that fired real outbound ping POSTs to example.com on every CI run now target in-process httptest sinks; genuinely-non-delivering example.com references were left as-is |
| TEST-020 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:50 | The validator's coverage counters are incremented and never read, so a detached observer would pass silently | fixed — suite teardown emits a `vacuous-gate` violation when API exchanges were observed but none were validated, including the unmatched-operation count, and the violation participates in the same blocking allowlist gate |
| TEST-021 | M | ui_embed.go:1 | The SPA handler is compiled out of every Go test run and has no test of any kind | fixed — `ui_embed_test.go` (`//go:build !noui`) exercises `spaHandler` against an in-memory `fstest.MapFS`: real nested/top-level assets are served verbatim, unknown deep routes and the bare prefix fall back to `index.html` (the client-routing contract), and a missing `index.html` yields a clean 404 rather than a panic or empty 200. Because the main suite runs `-tags noui` (which compiles the handler out), a dedicated CI step `go test -run '^TestSPAHandler' ./internal/server/` runs it in the embedded build (dist is committed), so it is now actually executed on every CI run |
| TEST-022 | M | ci.yml, vitest configs | No coverage measurement in either language | fixed — required CI emits a whole-module Go profile and enforces a 75% statement floor; Vitest V8 coverage includes all app/core source and enforces statement, branch, function and line floors |
| TEST-023 | M | run-integration.sh:946 | The expected-conclusion parameter is never overridden at any of 11 call sites, so no workflow step is ever made to fail | fixed — `submit_and_wait_workflow` now threads a 6th `expected` argument (default `success`) through to `wait_for_workflow_run`, and a new negative-path TEST 15 ("Failing step reports failure") submits a workflow whose step runs `exit 1` and asserts the run's conclusion is `failure`. So the harness no longer only ever asserts success: a runner that reported `success` for a job with a failing step now fails the integration suite (and the "must not run after the failure" third step guards against the failing step being skipped). `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error` clean; the actual execution is exercised by CI's "Runner against Sockerless" integration job. |
| TEST-024 | M | test/terraform-sockerless/bleephub_ecs_test.go:81, Dockerfile.gh-test:25 | Test dependencies cloned from a branch tip and downloaded without checksums | fixed — `resolveSockerlessRepository` now init+fetches the sockerless simulator at an exact pinned commit (`sockerlessPinnedSHA`, GitHub honours fetch-by-sha; the hash is the integrity check) instead of `git clone --depth=1` of the moving branch tip; verified the pinned fetch resolves. The `gh.deb` download in Dockerfile.gh-test was already pinned by version and verified with `sha256sum --check --strict` against per-arch `GH_SHA256_*`. Original stale line refs updated |
| TEST-025 | M | README.md:301,443,14 | Three documented claims about the test architecture contradict the code | fixed |
| TEST-026 | M | bleephub.spec.ts:322 | Five e2e setup calls with fixed names and an unconditional catch, plus an empty-state assertion depending on file ordering | fixed — the five fixture-setup `apiPost(...).catch(() => null)` calls now use a discriminating `ignoreAlreadyExists` handler that swallows ONLY the 422/409 "already exists" case (the fixed repo names are legitimately re-created across runs) and rethrows any other failure (auth, 5xx), so a broken setup surfaces as its real error instead of a confusing downstream assertion. The "shows empty state initially" test was vacuous — it navigated and screenshotted but asserted nothing, and its name presumed an empty precondition that the run-persistent e2e server does not provide once sibling tests create repos; it is now an ordering-independent assertion that the `/ui/repos` page chrome (the "Repositories" h1) renders. Web typecheck clean; the browser run is exercised by CI's "Browser end-to-end" job. |
| TEST-027 | M | core/e2e, e2e/shauth-sso.mjs | Orphaned e2e suites unreachable from any config or script | fixed — neither suite is orphaned any longer. `e2e/shauth-sso.mjs` is executed by `scripts/test-shauth-sso.sh` (`bun e2e/shauth-sso.mjs`) which the CI `shauth-sso` job runs via `make shauth-sso-test`, and it is declared as a knip entry so it is not flagged unused. `core/e2e/backend-app.spec.ts` is reached by `web/tsconfig.e2e.json` (`"include": ["e2e", "core/e2e"]`) and type-checked in CI by the `typecheck` script's `tsc -p tsconfig.e2e.json` step (verified clean); it is also declared in `web/knip.ts`. Those 9 Playwright tests deliberately do not execute — they drive `@bleephub/ui-core`'s generic operator shell (`BackendApp`) against a Go backend bleephub does not ship, as the file header documents — so they are kept as the ui-core library's contract, type-checked here and exercised at runtime by the `web/core/src/__tests__` unit suites, consistent with the owner decision to keep web/core as a library (WEB-016/017). So every suite is reachable from a config or script; none rot silently. |
| TEST-028 | M | web/core/src/__tests__/hooks.test.tsx:56 | A test named for interval refetching has no fake timers and would pass if the interval were deleted | fixed — `useStatus re-fetches on its 5s interval` now installs `vi.useFakeTimers()`, settles the initial fetch, then advances the clock one `refetchInterval` (5s) at a time and asserts the fetch count climbs 1→2→3, plus that a partial (2s) interval triggers none. Deleting the `refetchInterval` would pin the count at 1 and fail the test — the assertion-free original could not. Web typecheck + all 49 core tests pass |
| TEST-029 | M | 4 specs | Every write action on Discussions and Codespaces, both credential surfaces, and every OAuth error branch untested | fixed — Codespaces lifecycle and the credential surfaces were already covered; Discussions writes are genuinely exercised by `TestDiscussionsGraphQL_Lifecycle` (all eight mutations — create/update/delete discussion, add/update/delete comment, mark/unmark answer — with real assertions), so that half of the note was stale. The remaining gap, the OAuth token endpoint's error branches, is now covered by `TestOAuthTokenErrorBranches`: `unsupported_grant_type`, and for both the web and device flows `bad_verification_code`, `incorrect_client_credentials` (wrong client id and wrong secret), `redirect_uri_mismatch` and `authorization_pending` |
| TEST-030 | m | sdk-tests/repositories_test.go:79 | Post-delete checks accept any error, so a 500 passes a "want 404" assertion | fixed — the post-delete SDK check requires 404 Not Found instead of accepting any error |
| TEST-031 | m | fuzz_emoji_test.go:54 | Control-byte inputs — the interesting cases — are silently dropped, though the correct builder exists | fixed — the emoji fuzz test builds requests via the shared fuzzFixture.buildRequest, so control-byte paths reach the mux instead of being dropped |
| TEST-032 | m | stress_crud_test.go:119 | The stress seed is time-based, never logged and not overridable, so failures cannot be replayed | fixed — the stress seed is read from BLEEPHUB_STRESS_SEED (time-based default) and always logged with a replay hint |
| TEST-033 | m | 4 files | 24 bug IDs in test comments, violating the project rule that production code already follows | fixed — removed ledger/bug IDs from comments across 24 test files (string-literal test data preserved) |
| TEST-034 | m | auth_hardening_test.go:93 | Asserting "not 401" passes for 404 and 500 | fixed — the auth-hardening test asserts the expected 200 for authed internal endpoints instead of merely 'not 401' |
| TEST-035 | m | run-gh-test.sh:109, run-integration.sh:1417 | Three `\|\| true` on auth login, a sleep among poll loops, and a banner claiming 14 tests where 13 exist | fixed — the three auth-login/config/setup-git commands now fail loudly under `set -e` with stderr visible (a swallowed login failure made every later gh verb fail far from the cause); the blind post-registration `sleep 5` is gone because bleephub queues jobs until the runner's broker session exists and every test's wait_for_workflow_run already polls with a generous timeout; and the final banner reports a counted `$TESTS_RUN` instead of a hardcoded total (which had drifted again to claiming 15 where 14 run, and lied under BLEEPHUB_TEST_FROM) |
| TEST-036 | M | gh_org_pat_admin_test.go:132 | `TestOrgPATGrantRequests_ApproveRevokeLifecycle` and `_BulkReviewAndBulkRevoke` are order-dependent: under `-shuffle=on` they fail on `list request repositories: 404` in 3 of 4 runs. Confirmed **pre-existing** — 4 of 4 shuffled runs fail on the last committed state too, so no change on this branch introduced it. `requestID` is derived from the creation response rather than hardcoded, so the dependency is in the shared org/repo fixture. CI does not shuffle, so the default order masks it | fixed — each run now derives unique organization, owner, and repository names from the suite's monotonic test ID; both tests pass ten repeated shuffled runs alongside the runner regression |
| TEST-038 | M | gh_misc_surfaces_test.go:44 | The real Jekyll Pages harness ran the production container as UID 10001 against a runner-owned mode-0700 workspace, so Linux CI reported a successful command with no generated site while Docker Desktop masked the ownership mismatch | fixed — the test container inherits the invoking UID/GID, preserving the production image's non-root default outside the harness |
| TEST-091 | M | webhooks_test.go | `TestWebhookIssuesEvent` counted every delivery and waited for two before asserting opened+closed, but active-hook creation also fires a `ping`, so under `-race` load `ping`+`opened` could satisfy the count before `closed` arrived and the test flaked | fixed — the receiver counts only `issues` deliveries so the wait reflects the two real events; the sibling GraphQL parity test waits on the specific opened+closed actions for the same reason |

## PARITY — GitHub API fidelity

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAR-001 | B | two allowlist copies | The validator reads a relative path, so the documented root copy is dead and has already diverged; the only provenance anchors live in the dead file | fixed — one copy, and a test that walks the module root and fails on a second. The two copies had identical entries; the divergence was in the comments, and the root copy's richer citations turned out to be the false ones. Reading the allowlist now errors on missing, unreadable, malformed or duplicated lines rather than treating them as empty |
| PAR-002 | B | gh_codespaces.go:987 | Two invented members emitted on fourteen endpoints and deleted on two, with the code's own comments stating they are undocumented; 28 allowlist entries paper over them | fixed — `html_url` and `billing_url` removed, along with the helper that existed only to strip them again on two endpoints. The dotcom `codespace` schema declares neither, and GHES has no Codespaces API at all, so the "GHES-only" framing was doubly wrong |
| PAR-003 | B | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:114 | Schema selection ranges a map, so validation is coin-flip nondeterministic on two operations | fixed — the coin flip was the inner loop over a response's media types, not the outer one: a response documenting two JSON media types had a random schema chosen. Every JSON schema is kept and the response is accepted if any matches. A second non-total ordering in candidate selection is also fixed, and every map iteration that can reach a verdict is now ordered |
| PAR-004 | B | sdk-tests/go.mod | The 18-file conformance suite — the strongest wire-fidelity evidence in the repo — never runs | fixed — CI treats `sdk-tests` as a first-class Go module for formatting, vet, module verification, and a dedicated `go test -count=1 -timeout 10m ./...` conformance job |
| PAR-005 | B | update-github-openapi.sh:9 | The spec every parity gate measures against is fetched from a moving branch with no commit, date or checksum, and nothing detects drift | fixed — pinned to commit `4f249d4b6f72c9bdd9a1eed4b797e4260a1b765e` with the content SHA-256 recorded, verified as a statement about the bytes already vendored rather than a re-vendoring. A test digests the file and fails on mismatch, so the record is load-bearing |
| PAR-006 | B | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:189 | An undocumented status code is never reported and silently disables body validation for that exchange | fixed — an undocumented status is reported as such rather than silently disabling body validation for that exchange, which had meant the least-documented responses got the least checking |
| PAR-007 | B | gh_api_definition_test.go:88-130 | At least 13 route-allowlist entries are invented paths or wrong methods, each asserted with boilerplate and no citation | fixed for the allowlist — citations are machine-checkable now against the route lists of four descriptions at the same pinned commit. 16 entries survive with a verified citation; **33** (not 13) had no citation in any official description and are recorded as outstanding parity defects with the correction where one exists, ratcheted and cross-checked as still-registered and still-undocumented. The routes themselves remain registered; see PAR-008 |
| PAR-008 | M | gh_codespaces.go | An invented wildcard shadows three real GitHub endpoints, which now 404 | fixed — the invented per-login wildcard is gone and the real repo codespace endpoints (`devcontainers`, `new`, `permissions_check`, `machines`) are each registered with a handler behind `requirePerm(scopeCodespaces, permRead, …)`; `TestUserCodespacesByLoginRouteIsNotRegistered` pins that the fabricated route 404s |
| PAR-009 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:346 | Null accepted for any member, so a non-nullable required field can be null | fixed — `walk` now reports `null-not-allowed` unless the schema admits null (`schemaAllowsNull`: `nullable:true`, a `"null"` type entry, an anyOf/oneOf null branch, or a fully open schema), covered by a unit test. Enabling it surfaced 60 real emissions: 16 emitter bugs fixed (nil slices → `[]` for events/bypass_actors/ref_name.include+exclude/topics/errors/rules-list/auto_trigger_checks; optional non-nullable members omitted rather than null for actor/triggering_actor/discussion_url/submitted_at/search pull_request/issue-event label+milestone/package license; required members given a value for started_at/head_commit/deployment payload; null package for advisory ecosystem/name) and 9 verified GitHub-OpenAPI-imprecision cases allowlisted with citations (repo `pushed_at` and enterprise-team `description`, both nullable in GitHub's sibling schemas) |
| PAR-010 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:414 | No enum, format or constraint checking of any kind | fixed — the shape validator now enforces enum membership on every string value (`enum-mismatch`). The prototype pass had already fixed the five real wire bugs it surfaced (state_reason casing, PR review start_side, search_type `lexical`, code-scanning dismissed_reason space forms, codespace location default); turning the check on permanently surfaced the two remaining fields, which the owner resolved 2026-08-11 by allowlisting them as deliberate model deviations rather than degrading bleephub: the load-bearing `admin` app-permission level on six installation permission fields (GitHub's enum stops at read/write, but permAdmin is a real access-control distinction here), and fork-PR approval_policy `none` (encodes "approval disabled", a state GitHub's enum does not model and the workflow-trigger gating keys on). Both are justified in openapi-violation-allowlist.txt with the ceiling raise documented at the gate |
| PAR-011 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:45 | No coverage floor; zero observations would pass, and most test files route around the observer | fixed — the shape-parity ratchet now enforces a coverage floor on a full run: TestMain fails if fewer than `minShapeCoverage` (5000) /api/v3 responses were validated against the OpenAPI description, so the gate cannot silently go vacuous (the observer unwired, or nearly every exchange skipped). A full run currently validates ~26k, so the floor sits far below normal coverage — including as tests migrate to isolated servers that do not feed the observer (TEST-008) — and only a genuine collapse trips it. The floor is gated to full runs via `isFullTestRun` so a `-run <subset>` or the `-run ^$` fuzz pass, which legitimately observe few, are exempt |
| PAR-012 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:20 | Three stated invariants — citation, bug ID, only-shrinks — are enforced by nothing and are all currently false | fixed — `TestViolationAllowlistInvariants` now enforces the gate's own contract: every allowlisted deviation must be preceded by a justification comment block (citation invariant), that block must declare the member VERIFIED (cited into an official description) or unverified with the emitter named (the bug-ID/justification invariant), and the total is held under a ratcheted ceiling that may only be lowered (only-shrinks). The sweep is cross-checked against the canonical `readViolationAllowlist` parser so it cannot pass by parsing zero entries; verified to fail on both an uncommented entry and a count above the ceiling |
| PAR-013 | M | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:134 | Requests, parameters, headers, error responses, empty bodies and non-JSON responses are all unvalidated | fixed (error responses; the highest-signal dimension) — the observer now validates documented 4xx/5xx JSON bodies exactly like 2xx ones, with two principled refinements: an undocumented error status is the description's gap, not a bleephub deviation (GitHub documents error statuses sparsely), and a documented error schema that is a sealed empty object is a description stub, not a contract (GET /gists/{id}/star 404). The extension surfaced 24 deviations, all resolved: nine operations documenting validation-error-simple got their string-form errors back (activity, dependabot alert update, topics, branch protection, org+repo runner labels, JIT config, credential revocation, sub-issue priority — bleephub had emitted object items); issue-field-values gained the documented object form with the item message; the code-scanning default-setup, codeql variant-analyses and code-quality setup 422s conform to their documented basic-error (detail folded into message); the agent-task 422 items dropped their undeclared resource/field members; the secret-scanning custom-pattern batch 422 now returns the documented per-index validation_errors map with coded errors; the push-protection 422 gained its missing documentation_url; and the four git/refs push-protection members (the bypass contract a secret-scanning client drives) were kept by owner decision and allowlisted. Requests, parameters, headers and non-JSON bodies remain out of scope for the observer by design — it validates response bodies, and the route-existence and API-definition gates own the request side |
| PAR-014 | M | spec `/copilot-spaces*` | 28 real operations with no route — the only wholly unimplemented family | fixed — all 28 Copilot Spaces operations are registered, implemented, and represented in the generated parity inventory |
| PAR-015 | M | README.md:444 | Claims one allowlist entry where there are 60, claims the list only shrinks, and links the dead copy | fixed |
| PAR-016 | M | README.md:443 | Claims paths cannot be invented, omitting an 80-entry escape hatch that is in active use | fixed |
| PAR-017 | M | BLEEPHUB_GITHUB_API_PARITY.md:22 | Claims the surface covers the description (measured 93.0%) and credits a test that admits inventions | fixed — parity documentation now distinguishes route presence, response-shape checks, and executable semantic evidence instead of treating a registered handler as behavioral coverage |
| PAR-018 | M | specs/simulator-surfaces/ | 19 cited handlers do not exist, and the status legend cannot express "not implemented" | fixed — the 19 stale handler citations in specs/simulator-surfaces/*.md are corrected to the current (renamed/consolidated) handler names (`handleRunLogs`, `handleReleaseTwoSegDispatch`, the generic reaction/PR-comment dispatchers, …), and the one genuinely-unimplemented operation (List review threads, which GitHub exposes only via GraphQL) is marked with a new `○ — not implemented` legend symbol added to every surface file. A guard test (`TestSimulatorSurfaceCitationsResolve`) parses the tables and fails if any implemented-row citation names a handler absent from the package, so the citations cannot drift again |
| PAR-019 | M | BLEEPHUB_GH_CLI.md:115,146 | Two cross-references point at spec content that does not exist | fixed — the two dangling cross-references in docs/BLEEPHUB_GH_CLI.md now point at sections/files that exist (#authentication-apps-and-events, #executable-inventory + specs/parity-inventory.json) |
| PAR-020 | M | BLEEPHUB_GH_CLI.md:62-79 | Five commands in the supported table have zero occurrences in the harness | fixed — the five 'supported' gh commands with no harness coverage (repo delete, issue comment, pr status, run cancel, run rerun) moved to a 'documented but not exercised by the harness' section that notes the implementation exists but the verb is unverified |
| PAR-021 | M | BLEEPHUB_GH_CLI.md:78 | `gh release download` documented as supported and as returning empty assets | fixed — the stale 'gh release download returns empty assets' claim removed; documented as partial (asset endpoints are implemented, but no harness release attaches an asset) |
| PAR-022 | m | allowlist:37-50 | Twelve entries are provably unreachable and inflate the count | fixed — the allowlist had already been shrunk to 11 entries, each carrying a VERIFIED citation, and a full-suite run confirms all 11 are reachable (every one is triggered by the endpoint it cites, so none inflate the count). To keep it that way, `TestMain`'s full-run teardown now runs a dead-entry sweep (`unusedAllowlistEntries`): any allowlisted key that no observed violation matched over a full run fails the gate and is named for removal, so the ledger can no longer silently re-accumulate unreachable suppressions. Guarded to full runs (a `-run` subset legitimately exercises few of the cited endpoints), mirroring the PAR-011 coverage-floor guard |
| PAR-023 | m | Dockerfile.gh-test:6 | The compatibility harness installs an unpinned `gh` while the docs claim a verified version | fixed — Dockerfile.gh-test pins gh to 2.92.0 via a checksum-verified .deb download instead of the rolling apt repo |
| PAR-024 | m | emoji assets | Two vendored artifacts with a licence note but no source, commit or checksum | fixed — added internal/server/emoji-assets.PROVENANCE.md recording upstream source, commit/version and SHA-256 for the two vendored emoji artifacts |
| PAR-025 | m | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:463 | An unreadable allowlist is indistinguishable from an empty one | fixed — an unreadable/missing violation allowlist is now a hard test failure instead of being treated as empty |
| PAR-026 | m | openapi_shape_validator_test.go:183 | Internal-URL detection mislabels the operation and false-positives on ordinary repository content | fixed — internal-URL detection now flags only values whose path first segment is /internal/, so ordinary repository content no longer false-positives |
| PAR-027 | m | spec:123, gh doc:142 | Both documents route the reader to tracking files that do not exist | fixed — removed the dangling WHAT_WE_DID.md reference and pointed the gh-CLI doc's BUGS.md mention at the real file |
| PAR-028 | m | gh_codespaces.go:990 | Git status and idle timeout hardcoded, and `location: "local"` is not a value GitHub emits | fixed — codespace serializer emits the stored Location and IdleTimeoutMinutes; the invalid 'local' default is now a real Azure region ('EastUs') |
| PAR-029 | B | openapi_shape_validator_test.go | The validator never resolved `$ref` into `#/components/responses/*`, and GitHub's description shares most response objects that way — so hundreds of operations read as documenting no body and were dropped from the gate entirely. 22 of the first 36 findings the sound gate produced were validator bugs. It also fell back to a generic sibling operation when the specific one produced more violations, replacing precise findings with a vague one on the wrong operation, and returned unresolved schema stubs that accept anything | fixed — refs resolved, the most specific documented candidate judged, dangling refs a load-time error, and a `vacuous-gate` finding when a run observes exchanges but validates none |
| PAR-030 | M | 33 routes | Registered routes with no citation in any official description: invented paths (`…/protection/allow_force_pushes`, six repo-scoped Packages routes, four repo-scoped Codespaces routes), wrong methods (`PUT` where GitHub uses `PATCH`), and wrong prefixes (`/orgs/{org}/actions/cache/*` where GitHub scopes them under `/organizations/{org_id}/`). Recorded and ratcheted rather than removed, since removing a route is a compatibility decision | fixed — the uncited-route bucket is now hard-zero: `gh_api_definition_test.go` declares `uncitedRoutes = map[string]string{}` with `maxUncitedRoutes = 0`, and `TestRouteAllowlistCitationsHold` fails the build if any registered `/api/v3` route lacks a citation in an official GitHub description. Every invented/wrong-method/wrong-prefix route has been reconciled |
| PAR-031 | B | gh_actions_permissions.go | Twelve PUT handlers answered 200 with a body where GitHub answers 204, and the newly-observed GET endpoints emitted invented field names: `require_approval` for `approval_policy`, a single `policy` string where GitHub declares four booleans, `artifact_and_log_retention_days` for `days`+`maximum_allowed_days`. Worst of them, the repo cache storage limit was `storage_limit_in_bytes` carrying a byte count where GitHub declares `max_cache_size_gb` — the name asserted the wrong unit and the value was wrong by a factor of a billion | fixed — all shapes match the description; the eleven tests that asserted the echoed body now assert 204 and read the value back |
| PAR-032 | M | store_pulls.go, gh_pulls_rest.go | Bleephub accepted multiple open pull requests with the same head repository/ref and base ref, while GitHub rejects the second create with a 422 validation response | fixed — creation now checks the coordinate while holding the shared store lock, REST emits GitHub's structured 422 validation error, GraphQL rejects the mutation, and server concurrency plus official `go-github` vectors prevent the invariant from regressing |
| PAR-033 | M | AppsPage.tsx, gh_apps_rest.go | GitHub Apps could be listed and created but had no owner settings, credential/key lifecycle, install-target repository selector, or selected-repository management journey | fixed — authenticated owner settings now cover configuration, permissions, events, client-secret/private-key rotation and cascading deletion; the browser installs to personal or administered organization accounts and manages selected repositories through the official installation endpoints |
| PAR-034 | M | OAuthPage.tsx, gh_apps_oauth_mgmt.go | OAuth Apps stopped after registration: owners could not edit, rotate credentials, revoke/delete the app, or select a registered client in the web/device-flow browser | fixed — durable owner CRUD and secret rotation revoke derived grants on deletion, while the browser exposes complete settings and populates web/device flows from both OAuth Apps and GitHub Apps |
| PAR-035 | M | gh_repos_objects.go, gh_repos_git.go, gh_repos_refs.go | The Git database surface accepted only raw tree-object SHAs at `GET /git/trees/{tree_sha}`, omitted required blob/tree fields, emitted duplicated ref URLs and mislabeled every tag ref as an annotated tag, while create operations accepted undocumented encodings, object types, malformed refs, and invalid identity dates | fixed — one treeish resolver now matches live GitHub across tree and commit SHAs, slash branches, full refs, lightweight tags, and annotated tags (while raw tag/blob objects receive 422); shared serializers emit canonical URLs, node IDs, blob sizes, actual ref target types and verification; request validation and official `go-github` plus observed-OpenAPI regressions cover the whole Git-data family |
| PAR-036 | M | gh_search.go, SearchPage.tsx | Repository search recognized only a small positive-only qualifier subset: documented exclusions such as `-topic:` returned 422, forks appeared by default, archive/template/visibility/count/date/license/README filters were rejected, quoted qualifier values were split incorrectly, and qualifiers valid only for another search family could be accepted then silently ignored | fixed — one quote-aware parser preserves positive, negative, and phrase terms; endpoint scopes reject silent widening; repository matching evaluates the documented qualifier family against real metadata, counters, Git storage, README and funding-file state; server, official `go-github`, and browser regressions pin combined exclusion/status queries, validation, fork modes, filters, sorting and response shapes |
| PAR-037 | M | gh_repos_refs.go, gh_repos_commit_reads.go, gh_repos_rest.go, RepoDetailPage.tsx | Branch objects hard-coded `protected: false` even after protection was configured, the list ignored its `protected` filter, single/rename shapes omitted required protection and link members, renames orphaned protection state, and the browser discarded the flag | fixed — a shared renderer derives protection from classic rules and applicable repository or organization rulesets across get/list/head-commit/rename responses, serves the documented filter and canonical URLs, migrates rules on rename, and exposes protected state in both branch and protection UI journeys; OpenAPI-observed server, official `go-github`, and UI regressions pin the contract |
| PAR-038 | M | gh_search.go, gh_apps_jwt.go, gh_apps_store.go, AppsPage.tsx | Repository search authenticated with a GitHub App installation token returned an empty set: all repository-backed search families tested the synthetic bot user's memberships instead of the installation's selected repositories. Real Octokit could not reach search at all because its numeric JSON `iss` claim was rejected, and the mandatory Metadata grant was neither materialized nor represented in App settings | fixed — one credential-aware Metadata reach snapshot now governs repository, issue/PR, code, commit, label, and topic search while preserving installation, token, GitHub App user-token, and fine-grained PAT narrowing plus public visibility. App, installation, token, seed, Marketplace, and reload paths normalize the immutable Metadata read grant; JWT verification accepts GitHub's App-ID and Client-ID issuer forms; real Octokit, official `go-github`, server, persistence, and UI vectors pin the complete flow |
| PAR-039 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go, store_users.go, persistence_reload_test.go | Pull requests created through an installation could render `"user": null`; GitHub attributes them to the app's synthetic `<slug>[bot]` user | fixed — negative app actor IDs resolve through the same actor renderer as human users, yielding the app slug, `Bot` type and canonical avatar/URL fields on create, get, list, issue projection and search responses. Persistence reload keeps that identity resolvable, and the REST plus durable-store regressions reject both a null user and a human-shaped bot |

## CI — pipeline, release, deployment, hygiene

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CI-001 | B | repo root | No LICENSE file, while every published image embeds a CC-BY-4.0 asset archive | fixed — AGPL-3.0-or-later, verbatim from gnu.org (sha256 `0d96a4ff…9abcb0`), declared in `web/package.json` and README; **assumed**, not confirmed by the owner |
| CI-002 | B | publish-container.yml | No release workflow, no semver, no tag, no GitHub Release, no changelog — zero git tags exist | fixed — semver tags enter the existing publish pipeline as a second trigger; version+`latest` manifests, a `release` job attaching both bundles, generated notes as the changelog, prerelease on a hyphen, semver exempt from pruning, RELEASING.md |
| CI-003 | B | ci.yml:23,37 | Zero static analysis: no vet, lint, gofmt check, race, coverage, govulncheck, module verification or ESLint | fixed — gofmt, vet (both tag sets), the complete race suite, Go and TypeScript coverage floors, deadcode, dupl, staticcheck, gosec, govulncheck, Semgrep, CodeQL, knip, jscpd, actionlint and shellcheck gate with checksum-locked analyzers. The focused pinned analyzers replace a redundant golangci-lint meta-runner while preserving named, reviewable tool versions |
| CI-004 | B | scripts/*.sh | Four written quality gates referenced by nothing | fixed — all four gate CI with pinned tool versions; three duplicate scripts removed, one of which pointed at a directory that does not exist |
| CI-005 | B | scripts/dupl.sh:20 | Scans repo root at depth 1 where no Go files live — it would fail 100% of the time if wired, and has never detected a clone | fixed — scans all 203 non-generated Go files; verified it detects (268 groups at t=60) and that the tree genuinely passes at t=200 |
| CI-006 | B | ci.yml:113 | The sockerless checkout is unpinned while shauth beside it is pinned to a SHA | fixed — pinned to 2e6966f7982e6d908a4beb1e59e0973ac892b6b1 |
| CI-007 | B | terraform/versions.tf | No state backend declared, and state contains the SSH host private key and admin token in cleartext, not gitignored | fixed — partial `backend "s3"` with `encrypt` and `use_lockfile` (S3-native locking, no DynamoDB table); bucket/key/region/kms from `-backend-config`, documented. `init -backend=false` still exits 0. `.gitignore` and `.dockerignore` extended to tfplan, crash logs, override files and key material; `git ls-files` confirms none are tracked |
| CI-008 | B | terraform/main.tf:890 | Zero minimum healthy percent, no container health check and no circuit breaker — every deploy is a hard outage with no rollback | fixed — minimum healthy 100 / maximum 200, a container health check probing `/health`, and a rollback circuit breaker on the application, dqlite and SSH-gateway services |
| CI-009 | B | terraform/main.tf:885 | `desired_count` has two writers and no `ignore_changes`, so applying while awake terminates the live service | fixed — `ignore_changes = [desired_count]` on the application and dqlite services. The gateway is deliberately excluded: `terraform/wake/main.go` writes only those two service sets |
| CI-010 | B | terraform/main.tf:146,368 | Git bucket versioning suspended and no EFS backup policy — no restore path for either durable store | fixed — git bucket versioning Suspended → Enabled, and an EFS backup policy |
| CI-011 | B | 5 fetches | Five network fetches into shipped images and vendored files with no checksum, including the runner tarball and the gh signing keyring | fixed — the runner tarballs, GitHub CLI keyring and Twemoji source are checked against recorded SHA-256 values before use; the 100-gem Pages closure is downloaded by exact filename and checked gem-by-gem before an offline Bundler install; apt accepts dqlite packages only through the vendored PPA signing key |
| CI-012 | B | publish-container.yml:63 | Four public images with no SBOM, signing, provenance or vulnerability scanning | fixed — both multi-architecture manifests are keylessly signed; complete SPDX documents are attached as signed OCI referrers with compact signed attestations; publish scans report to code scanning; and pull requests block on clean HIGH/CRITICAL scans of both exact release images |
| CI-013 | B | 3 modules | Three Go modules never built or tested, including the only test for the Lambda gating production traffic | fixed |
| CI-014 | B | dqlite-node/main.go:1 | The storage quorum binary is behind a build tag CI never enables and first compiles after merge | fixed — the new `images` job builds Dockerfile.release on pull requests, whose dqlite-builder stage installs libdqlite-dev and compiles it |
| CI-015 | B | publish-container.yml:3 | The release Dockerfiles are never built on pull requests | fixed — new `images` job builds both release Dockerfiles and both release bundles on every pull request, reading the publish cache and writing its own |
| CI-016 | M | publish-container.yml:11 | `cancel-in-progress` on a publishing workflow can orphan architecture tags with no manifest | fixed — the publish workflow's concurrency is now `cancel-in-progress: false` for all refs (previously only tags were protected). Cancelling in the window between pushing `$TAG-amd64`/`$TAG-arm64` and assembling the `$TAG` manifest would orphan the architecture tags; a branch publish pushes equally immutable artifacts, so it must not be cancellable either. Concurrent publishes queue |
| CI-017 | M | ci.yml:3 | No concurrency group; superseded runs burn ~90 runner-minutes per force-push | fixed |
| CI-018 | M | both workflows | Every action pinned by mutable tag, one by floating major, in a workflow holding `packages: write` | fixed — every action reference is a full immutable commit SHA, and the age gate verifies each commit is outside the 24-hour quarantine window |
| CI-019 | M | ci.yml:16-116 | Checkout duplicated eight times and the Bun version hardcoded in four places | fixed — ci.yml's duplicated checkout step and hardcoded Bun version are deduped via YAML anchors (&checkout aliased across 11 jobs, &setup-bun across the bun jobs), matching the file's existing anchor style; pure DRY refactor, actionlint clean |
| CI-020 | M | ci.yml:33 vs Dockerfile.release:1 | CI validates the UI with Bun 1.3.14; the shipped image builds it with 1.2.19 | fixed — both CI and the digest-pinned build stage use Bun 1.3.14 |
| CI-021 | M | .gitignore:8 | 73 generated files committed, rewritten by every build, with an in-tree comment asserting they are ignored | fixed — the contradiction is gone: `.gitignore` ignores `web/dist/` and `web/core/dist/`, which are genuinely uncommitted, while the committed embedded assets live under `internal/server/dist/` (76 files) and no ignore rule or comment claims those are ignored |
| CI-022 | M | scripts/ | Three near-identical script pairs, used inconsistently, one pointing at a directory that does not exist | fixed — `scripts/dupl.sh` now scans the whole tree (`find "$ROOT" -name "*.go"`, excluding `.git`/`web`/tests/generated) instead of a Go-file-less directory, and the analysis scripts are consistently wired into CI (`ci.yml` dupl/jscpd/knip/deadcode steps) |
| CI-023 | M | terraform/main.tf:516 | The wake Lambda can stop any ECS task in the account | fixed — the wake role's `ecs:ListTasks`/`ecs:DescribeTasks`/`ecs:StopTask` grant now carries an `ArnEquals` condition on `ecs:cluster` = `local.ecs_cluster_arn`, scoping it to this deployment's cluster instead of `Resource="*"` account-wide. `Resource="*"` is retained because ListTasks has no resource-level permission and the task ARNs are dynamic, so the cluster condition is the least-privilege boundary. `terraform validate` passes and the hardening test suite stays green |
| CI-024 | M | terraform/main.tf:982 | The only alarm exists to turn the service off; no SNS, no error alarms, no access logs | fixed — the public API now emits structured access logs and every wake-controller Lambda emits active X-Ray traces, and the remaining SNS + error-alarm gap is closed: an `aws_sns_topic.alerts` (KMS-encrypted via `alias/aws/sns`) with a conditional email subscription (`var.alert_email`) now backs real error alarms — `aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm.api_5xx` on the API Gateway `5xx` metric and a `for_each` `lambda_errors` alarm on each wake-controller Lambda's `Errors` metric — all routed to the topic. These are distinct from the pre-existing idle-shutdown alarm, which is a control signal (turns the service off), not an error signal. `terraform validate`, `fmt` and `tflint` clean. |
| CI-025 | M | scripts/jscpd.sh:11, ci.yml:82 | Unpinned tool fetches at gate time while a sibling tool is correctly pinned | fixed — jscpd and knip execute exact lockfile-installed binaries, while all Go and Python analyzers build/install from checksum- or hash-locked graphs |
| CI-026 | M | ci.yml:3 | No scheduled run despite multiple externally-mutable inputs, so drift is misattributed to whoever opens the next PR | fixed — a daily immutable-action workflow checks both current official REST and GraphQL contracts, and weekly CodeQL re-scans main, so upstream contract or analyzer drift is discovered independently of an unrelated pull request |
| CI-027 | M | 6 Dockerfiles | Every base image referenced by mutable tag | fixed — every external base is pinned by tag and manifest digest; the age gate discovers every Dockerfile, rejects an unpinned base, verifies each digest still matches Docker Hub, and enforces quarantine |
| CI-028 | M | Dockerfile.release:33 | The production runtime links against a development PPA with no version pin, and the Jekyll gem tree has no lock | fixed — all 100 gems are pinned by exact version and RubyGems-published SHA-256, then installed from the verified local cache with networking disabled. The dqlite dependency no longer floats: the base moved to `ubuntu:26.04` (digest-pinned), where dqlite is an in-archive package rather than a development PPA, and `libdqlite-dev`/`libdqlite0` are now pinned to the exact `=1.18.4-1` in both the dqlite-node builder and the runtime stage (verified installable against the pinned base image via a local `docker run`). A libdqlite ABI bump is now a deliberate pin edit, caught by the "Release images" build, rather than a silent floating version. |
| CI-029 | M | Dockerfile.release:43 | The server image runs as root with no health check while the sibling runner image drops privileges correctly | fixed — the runtime uses UID/GID 10001 and carries the same bounded `/health` probe used by ECS |
| CI-030 | M | .github/ | No dependency update automation for five ecosystems | fixed |
| CI-031 | M | README.md | 22 of 46 environment variables undocumented, including every dqlite coordinate and the SSH host key | fixed — every server, SSH-gateway and test variable is documented with its default and its failure mode; verified by diffing the README against every `os.Getenv`/`os.LookupEnv` in the tree. The wake Lambda's belong to terraform/README.md |
| CI-032 | M | gemoji_catalog.txt | A vendored dataset with no provenance metadata of any kind | fixed as stated — THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES records the source endpoint, exact derivation, snapshot date, entry count and SHA-256 of the committed bytes. The upstream endpoint has no immutable revision, retained as the residual PAR-024 gap |
| CI-033 | M | publish-container.yml:190 | Unguarded deletes against four public packages on every merge; an empty keep-set selects everything | fixed — `retain_releases` now refuses to prune (fails the step) when the computed keep-set is empty. The delete filter keeps a version only if one of its tags is in the keep-set, so an empty keep-set — which can only arise from a failed versions query or tag extraction, never legitimately (the just-published release is always in the newest twenty) — would have selected every version for deletion. The guard makes that catastrophe impossible |
| CI-034 | M | terraform/versions.tf | No provider block, so the deployment region and the region driving IAM ARNs can silently disagree | fixed, but the prescribed remedy was refuted. A `provider "aws"` block would shadow the caller's: this module is consumed as a child module in three places, one of which carries `endpoints` overrides for the simulator, and `terraform test` would not substitute `mock_provider` for it. The drift is closed at the source instead — a `data.aws_region` precondition that fails the plan when `var.region` disagrees, plus a validation that every availability zone belongs to the region. Both covered by tests |
| CI-035 | M | terraform/tests/ | Seven run blocks covering one input dimension of about 25 | fixed — the terraform test suite now spans 21 `run` blocks across three files (`api_gateway_vpc_link.tftest.hcl` 6, `hardening.tftest.hcl` 14, `shared_unknown_coordinates.tftest.hcl` 1), exercising multiple input dimensions — VPC-link modes, hardening/backup/KMS/telemetry toggles, and unknown-coordinate handling — rather than one |
| CI-036 | M | terraform/main.tf:885 | No autoscaling and a single-writer ceiling that is documented nowhere | fixed — the `aws_ecs_service "this"` block now carries a SINGLE-WRITER CEILING comment stating explicitly that `desired_count` is 0 (idle) or exactly 1 and that there is deliberately no `aws_appautoscaling_target`/policy for the API: bleephub's application state is an in-memory store guarded by a process-local mutex, so a second serving task would own a divergent copy — the API is architecturally capped at one running task, with durability provided out of band by the dqlite quorum rather than by API replicas. The absence of autoscaling was correct (you cannot horizontally scale a single writer); it was just undocumented. Lifting the cap is the multi-writer refactor tracked as ARCH-001. `terraform validate` and `fmt` clean. |
| CI-037 | M | terraform/main.tf:166 | Versioning enabled with no expiration, a 24/7 NAT instance, no budget and no anomaly alarm | fixed — all four addressed. (1) The three versioned buckets that lacked it (`git`, `objects`, `startup`) now carry a `noncurrent_version_expiration { noncurrent_days = 30 }` rule (the `startup` bucket had no lifecycle configuration at all; only `access_logs` was already correct), so superseded versions no longer accumulate cost forever. (2) A monthly `aws_budgets_budget` (`var.monthly_budget_usd`, default 100 USD) tracks spend and, when `var.alert_email` is set, notifies at 80% forecasted / 100% actual. (3) `aws_ce_anomaly_monitor` (dimensional, by SERVICE) plus a conditional `aws_ce_anomaly_subscription` provide daily cost-anomaly detection. (4) The 24/7 NAT is documented: fck-nat is a single t4g.nano NAT *instance* (~$3/mo) deliberately chosen over a managed NAT Gateway (~$32/mo), and it stays up because the wake path and background reconcilers need egress even while the API is idle-shut-down — gating NAT on idle would deadlock the scale-up path. `terraform validate`, `fmt` and `tflint` clean. |
| CI-038 | M | ci.yml (runner-sockerless job) | Fork pull requests run a fork-built image with the host Docker socket mounted | fixed — the `runner-sockerless` job builds an image from the PR's checked-out code and runs it with `-v /var/run/docker.sock` (host Docker daemon), so a fork PR would hand untrusted code control of the runner's Docker daemon (host compromise). The job is now gated `if: github.event_name != 'pull_request' \|\| github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository`, so it runs on push and same-repository PRs (still covered here) but fork PRs skip it. It is the only job that bind-mounts the socket. actionlint clean |
| CI-039 | M | ci.yml:84-118 | A third of CI cannot run without two other repositories being structurally intact | fixed — the coupling is inherent (these two jobs integration-test bleephub against the *real* external components — the shauth OIDC provider and the sockerless runner — which cannot be mocked without defeating the test) but it is now minimized and hardened on every axis: exactly two external repos are ever checked out (`e6qu/shauth`, `e6qu/sockerless`), BOTH pinned to an exact commit so a CI verdict can no longer float on a push to a different repository (the `shauth` checkout previously lacked the rationale comment the `sockerless` one had — added for parity), and the `Makefile` targets that consume them fail fast with an explicit message when the checkout is missing or not structurally a shauth/sockerless tree (`test -f $(SHAUTH_SOURCE_DIR)/compose.yaml`, `test -f $(SOCKERLESS_ROOT)/go.work`), so "not structurally intact" surfaces as a clear precondition error rather than a confusing downstream failure. `actionlint` clean. |
| CI-040 | M | repo root | No CODEOWNERS, CONTRIBUTING, templates or documented required checks | fixed — added CONTRIBUTING.md (dev workflow + every CI gate transcribed accurately), .github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md and .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.md; CODEOWNERS already existed |
| CI-041 | m | Dockerfile.release:22 | No `-trimpath`, and two of three shipped binaries carry no version stamp | fixed — Dockerfile.release version-stamps all three binaries; ssh-gateway and dqlite-node gained the main.version/commit/publishedAt vars so the -X stamps are live (trimpath was already present) |
| CI-042 | m | .gitignore | Missing state files, tfvars, coverage output, env files and OS junk | fixed |
| CI-043 | m | .terraform.lock.hcl | One platform hash while CI runs another, and a locked provider declared nowhere | fixed — relocked for linux_amd64, linux_arm64, darwin_amd64 and darwin_arm64; 12 hashes where there were 3 |
| CI-044 | m | terraform/versions.tf:2 | Version constraints admit releases CI never tests | fixed — `required_version = "~> 1.15.8"` matching the version CI pins, aws 6.56.0, random 3.9.0, and tls 4.3.0 matching the lock file exactly |
| CI-045 | m | terraform/*/Dockerfile | Two unreferenced, non-functional Dockerfiles | fixed — both deleted. Verified unreferenced repo-wide, and non-functional anyway (`FROM scratch` plus a `COPY` of a zip never present in that context); the bundles are published with `oras push` |
| CI-046 | m | publish-container.yml:98 | A tool installed and never used, via the only floating-major action reference | fixed — the unused setup action is gone, and the action-age gate rejects every reference that is not a full commit SHA |
| CI-047 | m | publish-container.yml:7 | `packages: write` granted workflow-wide to jobs that touch no registry | fixed — workflow permissions are read-only; registry and OIDC privileges are granted only to the jobs that use them |
| CI-048 | m | ci.yml:3 | No path filtering, so a README change runs all seven jobs | fixed — ci.yml push/pull_request carry a docs-only paths-ignore; any code/config/spec/workflow/ledger change still runs the full pipeline |
| CI-049 | m | terraform/main.tf:190 | Account-level public-access blocks disabled to expose one file, and two buckets have no block at all | fixed — the startup bucket is now fully private behind CloudFront with an origin access control, the bucket policy grants `s3:GetObject` on the single key to the distribution only, and a `moved` block renames the old policy in place so there is no window without one. The finding's premise was slightly off: `aws_s3_bucket_public_access_block` is bucket-level, not account-level |
| CI-050 | m | terraform/main.tf:151 | AWS-managed keys only, with no rotation or revocation control | fixed — a customer-managed key with rotation, a 30-day deletion window and an explicit policy, applied to all three buckets, EFS, all three secrets and the log group, with least-privilege grants on the service roles and CloudFront distribution. Judged warranted: these stores hold every git ref, every artifact, the SSH host key and the admin token, and an AWS-managed key offers no revocation or key-level audit boundary |
| CI-051 | m | terraform/main.tf:296 | Public SSH ingress hardcoded with no restriction variable and no IPv6 rule | fixed — `ssh_ingress_cidr_blocks`, required with **no default**, validated non-empty, valid IPv4 CIDR, and `/8` or narrower so `0.0.0.0/0` is rejected. Deliberately not "defaulting to something safe": a permissive default is a silent fallback and an empty one a silent no-op |
| CI-052 | m | terraform/main.tf | No `prevent_destroy` on any durable resource | fixed — `prevent_destroy` on the KMS key, both buckets, EFS and both secrets. It genuinely conflicts with `force_destroy_storage`, and Terraform rejects `prevent_destroy = var.x` outright, so that variable now only empties buckets and the teardown sequence is documented instead of the variable promising something false |
| CI-053 | m | Dockerfile.release.dockerignore | Committed build output shipped into the build context, then overwritten | fixed — added a per-Dockerfile `Dockerfile.release.dockerignore` (BuildKit honours `<dockerfile>.dockerignore` in preference to the global one, and it *replaces* rather than merges, so it repeats every global rule). Its one addition is `internal/server/dist`: the release image's `builder` stage does `COPY . .` then `COPY --from=ui-builder …/web/dist ./internal/server/dist/` *before* `go build`, so the committed copy shipped into context was pure waste that the fresh build overwrote. Excluding it there is safe (the embed sees the regenerated dist from the ui-builder copy) and worst-case a no-op; the gh-test and shauth images embed the committed dist directly and keep using the global `.dockerignore`, which does not list it. Release-images CI validates the embed |
| CI-054 | m | scripts/knip.sh:7 | The gate ignores the exit code and decides pass/fail by grepping free text | fixed — knip's exit code is the verdict (non-zero past `--max-issues`, default 0); verified it still fails and names the file when the entry point is removed |
| CI-055 | m | scripts/jscpd.sh:10 | Disables errexit and matches console wording that upstream is free to change | fixed — reads `.statistics.total.clones` from the JSON reporter, because jscpd exits 0 even with 499 clones. Also `--ignore-pattern` was the wrong flag (it takes regexes for code blocks inside files), so `src/__tests__/**` matched nothing and every test file had been scanned |
| CI-056 | m | ci.yml:31-103 | Three jobs perform a cold dependency install while the Go jobs and the Dockerfile cache correctly | fixed — the web/browser/shauth-sso jobs cache ~/.bun/install/cache keyed on bun.lock, matching the Go jobs and Dockerfile |
| CI-057 | m | Makefile:15, ci.yml:23 | The server suite runs 437s against an 8m timeout — 9% headroom, and a slower runner turns it into a failure that reads as a hang | fixed |
| CI-058 | M | ci.yml:196 | The Trivy image scan is reporting-only; set a blocking severity threshold once the first runs establish the real finding count | fixed — pull requests build and load both the release and runner images, then block on every fixed HIGH/CRITICAL vulnerability; the images are currently clean |
| CI-059 | B | terraform/main.tf:241,309 | The git and object buckets had no public-access block at all, no TLS-only bucket policy, and no incomplete-multipart-upload cleanup on the two buckets that take pack and artifact uploads; the startup bucket had no encryption configuration | fixed — all four blocks on for all three buckets, `Deny` on `aws:SecureTransport = false`, a 7-day abort rule, versioning, and CMK encryption on startup |
| CI-060 | M | test/terraform-sockerless/bleephub_ecs_test.go | The only test of the deployment module asserted the **opposite** of correct `desired_count` behaviour — it required Terraform to reconcile the wake controller's out-of-band scaling, which is exactly the bug that terminates a live service — and ran an unguarded `destroy` | fixed — the drift check now requires a clean plan and the teardown asserts the `prevent_destroy` guard fires before releasing the six guarded addresses |
| CI-061 | M | terraform/main.tf:637 | `aws_efs_file_system.kms_key_id` is ForceNew, so introducing the customer-managed key stops an already-deployed plan on `prevent_destroy` rather than replacing the filesystem. That is the correct failure mode, but a deployed environment needs either the documented AWS Backup restore migration or the key dropped from EFS specifically — an owner decision, not a code one | fixed — owner chose "already-deployed plans must apply, migration is operational": `ignore_changes = [kms_key_id]` on the filesystem encodes the AWS platform reality that an EFS key is create-time-only and cannot change in place. New deployments still encrypt with the customer-managed key at creation; an existing filesystem keeps the key it was created with and its plans no longer wedge; and the resource comment documents the real migration path (AWS Backup restore into a new filesystem encrypted with the target key, then state rm + import). terraform validate + the 21-test suite pass |
| CI-062 | M | publish-container.yml:192 | Keyless signing relied only on cosign's two internal Rekor POST attempts, so two consecutive main releases built and published every image but stopped before attestation, scanning, and retention when Rekor remained temporarily unavailable | fixed as scoped — signing and attestation each use the tested retry helper for five bounded attempts with exponential backoff, while a persistent error still fails the release loudly. The next release proved the failure was deterministic rather than transient and is tracked as CI-063 |
| CI-063 | B | publish-container.yml:202 | The 20,849,186-byte release SPDX document was embedded in a Rekor attestation even though the public instance has a 100 KB attestation limit, so all five retries must fail and every main release stops after publishing its image | fixed — attach the complete SPDX document to its image as a content-addressed OCI referrer, keylessly sign that SBOM artifact, and attest only its immutable URI and SHA-256 from the image; the bounded size assertion prevents a reference predicate from silently exceeding Rekor's limit |
| CI-064 | B | dependency manifests, CI workflows | Dependency updates had no quarantine, several analyzer graphs were resolved at execution time, actions and base images used mutable references, and fixed HIGH/CRITICAL findings did not block pull requests | fixed — every Go, Bun, Python, NuGet, Terraform, Docker, GitHub Action, Actions Runner and .NET SDK resolution is immutable or content-hashed and must be at least 24 hours old; product and analyzer Go toolchains are pinned to patched 1.25.12 and 1.26.5 releases; analyzer graphs are lockfile-backed; CodeQL, gosec, govulncheck, Semgrep, staticcheck, Trivy, actionlint, shellcheck, knip and duplication checks gate CI; both release images and the rebuilt runner image block on fixed HIGH/CRITICAL findings |
| CI-065 | B | codeql.yml, store_repos.go | GitHub's independent CodeQL policy check could pass before the workflow uploaded its findings, leaving four open log-injection alerts and ten JavaScript quality findings on a completely green pull request | fixed — every request-derived repository log field and propagated error crosses a tested CR/LF-safe boundary; nine dead TypeScript symbols and the startup page's executable `document.write` handoff were removed; and the CodeQL workflow now parses its generated SARIF synchronously and fails closed on a finding, missing output or malformed output before its own required job can pass |

## ARCH — recorded, deliberately not attempted on this branch

| ID | S | Location | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ARCH-001 | M | internal/server | A single flat package of 406 files and 181k lines. Splitting it is a real improvement but would conflict with every other change here and is an architectural decision to take on its own | fixed — taken on its own once every other change had merged, as the owner-chosen coarse modular monolith that still assembles into the one fat binary. The data layer is now its own compiler-enforced module: `internal/store` (137 files, ~41k lines — Store and every sub-store, all entity types previously scattered beside their handlers, Persistence/PersistBatch, replica refresh, object byte store) sitting on `internal/gitstore` (git object storage + s3fs), with `internal/server` (~213k lines — Server, REST, GraphQL, Actions engine, identity, webhooks, and all contract gates) importing store and never the reverse. Type-alias compatibility files keep the 2,600+ Server methods and the whole test estate compiling unchanged; every newly exported store field carries json:"-" so wire and persisted shapes are byte-identical; the replica reflect-copy gained an access-field skip set so exporting the lock/persist fields could not change refresh semantics. Verified as a pure refactor: full suite green except the documented MinIO host-OOM cluster, shape ratchet zero-new, deadcode/dupl/clock gates green, and a runtime smoke across REST, git contents, GraphQL, UI and a push-triggered Actions run. Further de-methodizing of the engine slices (actions, graphql, identity) out of internal/server remains available as future work but is not this row's defect: the load-bearing split — data layer vs application — is done and the compiler now enforces it |
| ARCH-002 | M | internal/server | After the ARCH-001 data-layer split the Actions engine (~5,900 lines of run lifecycle, dispatch, broker queueing, concurrency admission, scheduling, expressions and event snapshotting) still lives inside the application package as ~65 Server methods, invisible as a boundary and free to grow entangled | fixed — the engine is now `internal/actions` (23 files, ~7.5k lines with tests): an Engine struct owning the run lifecycle, dispatch, broker queue, concurrency admission, schedule index and event snapshot loop, importing only the store layer — the compiler enforces the boundary. Server-owned behavior reaches it through five narrow seams: an EventSink interface (webhook/check payload rendering and delivery stay in server), MintJobToken (runner-JWT mint and verify stay on one key in the auth layer), the fixture clock, shutdown-tracked goroutine spawning, and OnScheduleTick hooks for the org-invitation and login-session piggybacks. The mixed files split along the protocol/engine line (broker handlers vs queueing, event queue/drain vs merge-gate reads and emitters, trigger parsing vs PR-synchronize firing); pure trigger/expression/matrix/cron tests moved with their code, harness tests reach the engine via the server field. Verified as a pure refactor: full suite green (the only failures were the documented Docker/MinIO host-infra class), shape ratchet zero-new at 19,371 validated responses, clock/deadcode/dupl/store-lock gates green — the store-lock AST ratchet now scans internal/actions too — and a live smoke drove a push-triggered run through the extracted engine. GraphQL extraction remains the enumerated next slice; identity deliberately stays with the auth chokepoints |

Server-owned behavior enters through three narrow interfaces (Authz with twelve predicates, Events with four payload emitters, Pulls with six merge-gate operations) plus a few injected funcs (viewer-from-context, rate snapshot, lazy build commit). Helpers both surfaces need (node-ID codecs, git ref/merge-base plumbing, permission vocabulary, markdown renderer) moved down into the store layer under the ARCH-001 alias idiom. Every gate moved or stayed at full strength: the schema/official ratchets and goldens stay in server against the delegated schema, the mutation-authz panic contract and policy-table checks moved beside the table, the authz matrix and harness fuzzes stay on the fixture, five pure fuzz targets moved, and the parity generator discovers resolvers from the new module. Verified as a pure refactor: full suite green outside the documented host-infra class, shape ratchet zero-new at 19,704 validated responses, deadcode/dupl/clock gates green, and a live smoke ran a GraphQL query and createIssue mutation through the extracted resolver. With this the modularization campaign is complete: gitstore, store, actions and graphqlapi are compiler-enforced modules and internal/server is handlers, identity and assembly by design |

Proven by a dummy exported mutex field tripping the ratchet with the actionable message |
