## Refuted

Checked and found not to be defects. Recorded so they are not actioned later.

| Claim | Verdict |
| REST-189 | B | gh_security_advisories.go, gh_dependabot.go | a repository's Dependabot alerts and its still-embargoed draft advisories were readable by any authenticated account on a PUBLIC repository, because both surfaces gated on repository readability alone — so the exact vulnerable dependency versions an instance is running, and every advisory under embargo, were open to anyone holding a token | fixed — a shared `viewerHasRepoSecurityAccess` predicate asks security_events at read plus push standing on the repository, which is what GitHub requires on public repositories exactly as on private ones. The alert routes refuse with 404 rather than 403 so the refusal is not an oracle for whether findings exist; the advisory list filters embargoed rows out for a caller without security access while still serving published ones, since a published advisory is public. GraphQL's `Repository.vulnerabilityAlerts` asks the same predicate and answers null rather than an empty connection, so silence cannot be read as "this repository is clean". Verified: a REST isolation test proving the stranger is refused on both surfaces and the owner is not, plus the GraphQL half over public and private repositories |
| REST-190 | B | store_security_advisories.go | `repository-advisory-create` and `-update` decoded the CVSS vector from `cvss_vector` and had no `cve_id` member at all, but the documented request spells them `cvss_vector_string` and `cve_id` — so every GitHub SDK's vector and CVE identifier were silently discarded and the advisory came back with both null | fixed — both members carry their documented names on create and update, and `start_private_fork`, `collaborating_users`, `collaborating_teams` and per-vulnerability `vulnerable_functions` are accepted and stored rather than dropped. `collaborating_users`/`collaborating_teams` and `vulnerable_functions` were previously hardcoded empty arrays in the response; they now render what the request asked for, teams through the shared `teamToJSON` so a team here is byte-identical to one from `/orgs/{org}/teams`. Verified by a go-github conformance operation that writes each member and reads it back, and by a round-trip test that also clears the collaborator list with `[]` |
| REST-191 | M | gh_security_advisories.go, gh_global_advisories.go | `cvss.score` was served as null whenever the author supplied only a vector, which is all the documented create request accepts — so an advisory carrying a "critical" vector reported no score, and the `/advisories` `ecosystem` and `affects` filters rejected every advisory outright on the stale premise that repository advisories carry no package coordinates | fixed — the score is computed from the vector with the CVSS v3.0/v3.1 base formula, including the specification's Roundup rule rather than the naive ceiling that rounds a representable value up a tenth; a v4 vector is refused rather than scored with v3 arithmetic, because its score comes from a lookup table. The two filters now match against the advisory's vulnerabilities, with `affects` accepting `package@version` and testing the version against the vulnerable range under that ecosystem's ordering. Verified: nine published CVSS vectors with their known scores, the refusal cases, and conformance operations asserting both filters include a matching advisory and exclude a non-matching one |
| REST-192 | M | gh_dependabot.go | `dismissed_by` was a hardcoded null on every Dependabot alert response, so an alert a person had dismissed came back looking anonymous | fixed — the member renders the dismissing account through the shared user shape, or null when the alert stands. Verified by a go-github conformance operation that dismisses an alert and asserts the client decodes an account |
| REST-193 | M | dependabot_ingest.go, store_advisories_versions.go | Dependabot alert derivation compared package versions with one generic comparator for every ecosystem, so it both missed and invented alerts: a PEP 440 post-release read as a prerelease of its own release, a Maven service pack read as older than the release it patches, and a Composer `dev` build sorted above `alpha` | fixed — the comparison is ecosystem-aware, with SemVer 2.0.0 for npm/Go/Cargo/Hex/pub/SwiftPM/Actions, PEP 440 for pip, Maven's ComparableVersion for maven, Gem::Version for rubygems, four-part NuGetVersion for nuget and PHP's version_compare stability order for composer. A version dialect no comparator can read still raises the alert rather than silently ruling the dependency safe. Ecosystem spellings are folded, so a purl's `pypi`/`cargo`/`golang` matches an advisory's `pip`/`rust`/`go` instead of never matching. Verified: a table of orderings per ecosystem including three version pairs that two ecosystems place in opposite orders, plus antisymmetry on every case |
| REST-194 | M | gh_advisories_events.go | no `dependabot_alert`, `repository_advisory` or `security_advisory` webhook was ever delivered, and an alert's state only ever moved when a person moved it — a repository that had upgraded past every vulnerable range still reported all of its alerts open | fixed — derivation emits `created`, and a reconciliation pass over the repository's current dependency set closes an alert whose package is no longer declared at a vulnerable version (`fixed`) and reopens one whose vulnerable version returned (`reintroduced`), leaving a dismissed alert alone because a person already ruled on it. The REST alert update and the GraphQL dismissal both deliver through one payload builder, so a subscriber cannot tell which surface moved the alert. `repository_advisory` carries `reported` and `published`; `security_advisory` carries `published`, `updated` and `withdrawn` and fans out to the repositories actually holding an alert against the advisory. An advisory under embargo produces nothing. Verified: a receiver test walking created → dismissed → reopened → fixed → reintroduced and asserting each payload's members |
| GQL-083 | B | gh_advisories_graphql.go | the entire security-advisory, vulnerability-alert and dependency-graph GraphQL surface was absent — `securityAdvisory`, `securityAdvisories`, `securityVulnerabilities`, `Repository.vulnerabilityAlerts`, `Repository.dependencyGraphManifests`, `Repository.hasVulnerabilityAlertsEnabled` and `dismissRepositoryVulnerabilityAlert` — so any client selecting one failed document validation on an unknown field | fixed — the whole family is registered against the vendored schema: `SecurityAdvisory` with its identifiers, CWE connection, CVSS, classification, withdrawal and vulnerabilities connection; `SecurityVulnerability` with its package, range and first patched version; `RepositoryVulnerabilityAlert` with its state, dismisser, reason, advisory and vulnerability, dependency scope and relationship; `DependencyGraphManifest` and `DependencyGraphDependency`; `CWE`, `CVSS`, `CvssSeverities`, `EPSS`, `DependabotUpdate`, `DependabotUpdateError`, the `RepositoryNode` interface, ten enums, three input objects and every Relay connection and edge. The four Node implementors resolve through `Query.node` behind the same access check their fields use. The mutation has a `graphqlMutationAuthz` row at security_events/push — the entitlement the REST alert update demands, so GraphQL cannot become the way around it — with refusal and entitled coverage, and the introspection snapshot regenerated. Verified: the global-database queries, the derived-alert lifecycle, the manifests connection, node dispatch, ordering and cursor pagination, and cross-tenant isolation over public and private repositories |
| GQL-084 | M | gh_advisories_graphql.go | `RepositoryVulnerabilityAlert`'s nullable object members were written as nil Go maps, and a nil map boxed in an `interface{}` is not a nil interface — so an alert nobody had dismissed rendered a non-null `User` shell and failed the whole query with `Cannot return null for non-nullable field User.login`; separately the advisory and vulnerability source maps referred to one another, so any reflective walk over one recursed until the stack was gone | fixed — the four nullable members go through a helper that yields an untyped nil, and `SecurityVulnerability.advisory` resolves on demand from the vulnerability's GHSA id instead of embedding its parent, which removes the cycle and renders the advisory only when the field is selected. Verified: the alert query is exercised against an undismissed alert in every advisory test, which is what reproduced the failure |
| STORE-082 | M | global_advisories.go | `ListGlobalAdvisories` returned the live advisory rows rather than detached snapshots (STORE-021), and alert derivation walks that list holding no lock while reading each advisory's vulnerability slice | fixed — the listing clones each advisory and returns a snapshot slice, and the deep copy now also detaches the per-vulnerability `vulnerable_functions` slice the STORE-021 sweep predated |
| REST-195 | M | gh_repos_rest.go, store_repos.go | a new repository was created with no labels at all; github seeds nine (`bug`, `documentation`, `duplicate`, `enhancement`, `good first issue`, `help wanted`, `invalid`, `question`, `wontfix`), and `gh label list`, go-github `listLabelsForRepo` and PyGithub `get_labels` all read them | fixed — seeded in the same transaction as the repository row from every creation path including fork and generate-from-template, with the colours and descriptions taken from the live API rather than the docs table, which lists different prose and a tenth label the API does not return. `ListLabels` also sorted by map iteration order and now sorts by id |
| REST-196 | M | gh_pagination.go, gh_search.go | search responses carried no `Link` header, so `octokit.paginate` over `/search/issues` returned one result of seven and go-github saw no next page — silent truncation, where the client receives a plausible short answer and cannot tell | fixed — the same `buildLinkHeader` every other collection uses now applies to all seven search endpoints through the envelope writer, capped at github's 1000-result window so `rel="next"` never walks a client into the documented refusal |
| REST-197 | M | gh_pulls_rest.go, gh_repos_commit_reads.go, gh_repos_compare.go | `gh pr diff` printed the pull request object as JSON: the media-type matcher tested for `application/vnd.github.diff` and never matched gh's versioned `application/vnd.github.v3.diff`. The same miss silently disabled `.diff`/`.patch` on commits and compare, whose bodies were already implemented | fixed — one matcher accepts both spellings; the pull-request diff is built on a source extracted from the existing changed-files path rather than a second diff implementation |
| REST-198 | M | gh_repos_objects.go, gh_media_types.go | `Accept: application/vnd.github.raw` on the contents API answered `application/octet-stream` and still reported `X-GitHub-Media-Type: …format=json`, so octokit handed callers a binary buffer where github sends `text/plain; charset=utf-8` | fixed — content type and media-type header are handler-set per requested media type for contents and readme; `/git/blobs` keeps octet-stream (it addresses a hash with no filename) but now reports its parameter. The XSS-safe `/{owner}/{repo}/raw/{ref}/{path}` route is untouched and guarded by a test |
| REST-199 | m | gh_gists_rest.go | gist `html_url` was `/{owner}/{id}` and the git URLs carried the owner segment; github keys all three on the id alone (`/gist/{id}`, `/gist/{id}.git`) | fixed — verified every gist URL member against the vendored examples; the four api.github.com-shaped members already matched |
| REST-200 | M | gh_branch_protection.go, branch_protection.go | `required_status_checks.contexts` was never populated although `status-check-policy` requires both `contexts` and `checks`, so the `/contexts` sub-resource read empty and `POST` answered with only the value just added | fixed — the two are one set, and writing either populates both |
| REST-201 | M | gh_deployments.go, gh_checks_rest.go | every documented deployment filter (`sha`, `ref`, `task`, `environment`) was ignored, `check_name` was ignored on both check-run listings, and the suite listing ignored `status` and `filter`. `latest` also keyed on the suite alone, so a suite with several named checks collapsed to one | fixed — filters applied on both endpoints and `latest` re-keyed on (suite, name) |
| REST-202 | m | secrets.go | go-github's only typed environment-secret methods use `/repositories/{id}/environments/{env}/secrets`, which bleephub does not serve, so an unmodified go-github cannot read environment secrets | fixed — GHES 3.7 officially documents the repository-id environment-secret routes go-github still builds, so its route list is now vendored as a sixth official description (update-github-openapi.sh + github-openapi-routes.txt.gz) and the five routes are cited against `ghes-3.7` in describedOutsideDotcom, a citation the route-allowlist gate verifies against the vendored index. bleephub serves them by rewriting the id-addressed path to the canonical owner/name handler, so an id-addressed request cannot acquire access the named form would refuse. | REST-203 | M | gh_marketplace.go | the `marketplace_purchase` webhook emitted only purchased/changed/cancelled, so a scheduled downgrade or a paid cancellation produced no event, and a scheduled change could not be revoked | fixed — `pending_change` and `pending_change_cancelled` with `effective_date` and upcoming-versus-current purchase objects, plus a revoke path and a browser control |
| REST-204 | M | gh_copilot.go | `/orgs/{org}/copilot/billing` reported a hardcoded plan and feature policy, every seat reported `last_activity_at: null` with no way to record activity, and `/copilot/metrics` was permanently empty | fixed — org policy, per-seat activity and a usage ledger are persisted and reported; the 1-day report's shape was corrected to `report_day`, which had been failing the openapi shape ratchet |
| REST-205 | M | gh_org_billing.go, gh_labels_rest.go, store_issues.go, OrgSettingsPage.tsx | the pinned GitHub REST description had drifted past its provenance guard, adding expiring user-scoped organization budgets and archived labels; simply refreshing the schema would have left both accepted-but-ignored, while archived labels could still be attached through REST, GraphQL, issue creation and delayed suggestions | fixed — organization budgets now round-trip user scope and future expiration dates, validate membership and scope, clear expiration explicitly, and expose the complete journey in the browser; archived labels remain readable and removable but every assignment path shares the same refusal. That two-way test caught an over-refusal of ten routes on the first attempt |
| AUTH-123 | M | gh_repo_redirects.go | the new git rename redirect built its `Location` from the client-supplied `Host`, a high-severity open redirect in code added the same day | fixed — same-origin relative path assembled from server-chosen parts only, which also fixes proxy deployments |
| AUTH-124 | M | gh_oauth.go | the device-flow endpoints parsed form encoding only and answered a JSON body with `incorrect_client_credentials`, so octokit's own `auth-oauth-device` strategy could not complete the grant | fixed — both encodings accepted on every leg; `unsupported_grant_type` also bypassed `Accept` negotiation and `Cache-Control: no-store` |
| AUTH-125 | M | gh_wiki.go, gh_wiki_git.go | wiki writes were hard-wired to push access, so github's restrict-editing-to-collaborators setting could not be relaxed and the git lane had no wiki policy at all | fixed — one predicate both lanes ask; the stored flag is inverted so the zero value is github's restricted default, and unrestricting relaxes the principal without relaxing the credential's grant |
| AUTH-126 | M | gh_enterprise_policy.go, gh_marketplace.go | `membersCanMakePurchases` was stored and served but governed nothing | fixed — gated at the purchase and plan-change write paths, resolving the enterprise from the buying account; both-directions test asserts the store is unchanged on refusal |
| AUTH-127 | M | gh_enterprise_graphql.go, gh_projects_v2_graphql.go | `organizationProjects` fed only the `has_organization_projects` field and gated no creation path | fixed — gates `createProjectV2` for organization owners. Classic org projects have no creation path to gate: github retired them from the REST contract, so adding one would violate the real-route gate |
| AUTH-128 | B | gh_sudo_mode.go, store_login_sessions.go | `proofOfPresenceRequired` had no implementation at all — there was no sudo mode, so a hijacked session could change security settings, delete repositories and manage keys without ever re-proving presence | fixed — per-session sudo timestamp with a three-hour window, a challenge honouring the schema's MFA/REAUTH distinction, and enforcement at twelve sensitive operations. The challenge reuses the second-factor verifier so the disallowed-methods policy applies to it too |
| AUTH-129 | M | gh_user_ip_allow_list.go, gh_enterprise_policy.go | `ipAllowListUserLevelEnforcementEnabled` had no user-scoped allow list to enforce | fixed — a user-owned list enforced in the API middleware when the enterprise enables it. Its management surface sits deliberately outside the enforced scope so an account that locks itself out with a bad range can still undo it |
| AUTH-130 | M | account_security.go | `twoFactorDisallowedMethods` had nothing to disallow | fixed — a truthful closed catalogue of the methods that exist; recovery codes are classed insecure and refused, unspent, at every second-factor chokepoint. Github's `INSECURE` targets SMS, which bleephub does not implement, so that method is absent from the catalogue rather than listed and silently unenforced |
| AUTH-131 | M | store_enterprise_accounts.go, gh_enterprise_verified_domains.go | `notificationDeliveryRestrictionEnabled` restricted nothing and had no verified-domain model to restrict against | fixed — enterprise verified domains built; selecting email delivery for an address outside every verified domain is refused and the served preferences are clamped, so an account never claims a delivery that cannot happen. The outbound send itself is ungovernable because no mail transport exists in the process |
| GQL-085 | B | gh_issues_graphql.go, gh_pulls_graphql.go, gh_projects_v2_types_graphql.go, gh_repos_graphql.go, gh_discussions_graphql.go | the typed-nil source defect was systemic, not the single instance recorded as GQL-084: a nilable child written straight into an `interface{}` source member is a non-nil interface holding a nil map, which graphql-go reports as present, so the child's non-null fields fail and the panic discards a whole subtree. An audit over the full suite found 85 affected field paths reducing to five reachable defects — `Issue.milestone` and `Issue.issueType` on any issue without one, `IssueComment.editor` when the editor's account is gone, `PullRequest.mergedBy` on every unmerged pull request, and `projectV2(number:)` for a number that does not exist — plus roughly forty more of the same shape not reachable by the current fixtures. Not one of them failed a test | fixed — two helpers replace the ad-hoc predecessor, collapsing the declare-then-conditionally-assign dance the defect hides in into one safe expression so the shortest way to write the field is the correct one; every site converted, and a schema-local test pins the failure mode against the pinned graphql-go so a library upgrade speaks up |
| GQL-086 | M | gh_sponsors_graphql.go | github Sponsors was absent from GraphQL entirely — no `Sponsorable` interface, no listing, tier, goal or sponsorship types — so every Sponsors field a client selected was an unknown-field error | fixed — 66 types with `Sponsorable` on both User and Organization, `Query.sponsorables`, and all nine mutations, each with a policy row plus refusal and entitled cases. Private sponsorships are gated by one predicate that `includePrivate` cannot override |
| GQL-087 | M | gh_marketplace_graphql.go, store_marketplace_categories.go | `Query.marketplaceListing(s)`, `marketplaceCategory(-ies)` and both types were absent, and listings carried no category taxonomy | fixed — github's 19-slug taxonomy seeded per instance with a per-listing profile; every `viewerCan*`, `viewerHasPurchased` and `installedForViewer` answered from real ownership, purchases and installations, and a delisted listing reads as absent to non-publishers even under `allStates` |
| GQL-088 | m | gh_copilot_graphql.go | `User.copilotEndpoints` was absent, so a Copilot client could not resolve the instance's service endpoints | fixed — served from the instance's own URLs and scoped to the authenticated user as github scopes it |
| GQL-089 | M | gh_migrations_graphql.go | the whole importer GraphQL surface was absent — no `Migration` interface, no `RepositoryMigration`, `OrganizationMigration` or `MigrationSource`, no `Organization.repositoryMigrations`, and none of the seven mutations | fixed — 22 types and 7 mutations at exact github signatures, each behind a policy row with refusal and entitled cases, all resolving the migrator predicate the REST lane already used |
| GQL-090 | M | gh_search_repositories_graphql.go | `SearchType.REPOSITORY`, `SearchResultItemConnection.repositoryCount` and `Repository` in the `SearchResultItem` union were all absent | fixed — all three served, with a Go test alongside the conformance operation |
| GQL-091 | M | gh_rulesets_graphql.go | `Repository.rulesets` was absent although rulesets are served over REST, so `gh ruleset list` failed with a field error | fixed — the GraphQL view reads the existing ruleset data |
| GQL-092 | M | gh_account_surface_graphql.go and eight sibling files | Repository, User and Organization served 64, 37 and 34 of github's fields, so a client rendering a repository, profile or organization page hit validation errors for settings, community files, collaborators, deployments, teams, the follow graph and pinned items | fixed — 131 fields closed across the three types and 25 more on Team, each backed by existing store data, with per-field authorization tests covering secret teams, private membership and private repository content. En route: a latent typed-nil in the commit-comment author, and seven enums minted as unshared locals that collided once a second consumer named them |
| GQL-093 | m | gh_mutations_activity_graphql.go, gh_users_org_fields_graphql.go | `Repository.stargazers` and `User.starredRepositories` reported the repository createdAt as a stand-in for the non-null starredAt because stargazers were a timestamp-less set | fixed — `Repo.Stargazers`/`User.StarredRepos` now map to the star instant (set together in `StarRepo`, cloned in the snapshot, persisted); both edges render the real per-user starredAt via `RepoStargazersAt`/`StarredReposAt`, verified with a frozen-clock test |
| GQL-094 | m | gh_users_org_fields_graphql.go | `User.status` and `Organization.memberStatuses` need a user-status model (emoji, message, expiry, organization scope) that does not exist | fixed — `store.UserStatus` carries emoji/message/expiry/organization and `User.status`, `Organization.memberStatuses` and `Team.memberStatuses` all resolve from it (gh_users_org_fields_graphql.go, gh_teams_fields_graphql.go); the finding predated the model |
| GQL-095 | m | gh_final_residue_graphql.go | `packages` on Repository, User and Organization is unserved although the package store holds the data; it needs the `Package`/`PackageVersion`/`PackageStatistics` type graph | fixed — the `Package`/`PackageVersion`/`PackageStatistics`/`PackageFile` graph resolves from the package store, and `PackageVersion.files` now returns the stored files once `store.PackageFile` gained the `updatedAt` its non-null `DateTime!` field requires |
| GQL-096 | m | gh_audit_log_graphql.go | `Organization.auditLog` needs github's roughly one hundred typed audit-entry objects; the store keeps one generic entry row | fixed — `auditLog` dispatches each entry to its concrete typed `*AuditEntry` object (not a generic row); every recorded action with a GitHub `OrganizationAuditEntry` union counterpart is typed (`org.invite_member` added). The remaining union members model audit events bleephub does not record |
| GQL-097 | m | gh_advisories_graphql.go, gh_repos_git_fields_graphql.go | `Repository.branchProtectionRules` was a two-field shell and `vulnerabilityAlert(number:)` was unserved | fixed — `BranchProtectionRule` is the full 31-field object with `Repository.branchProtectionRules` and create/update/delete mutations; `vulnerabilityAlert(number:)` resolves the alert via `GetDependabotAlert(repo.FullName, number)` under the same security-access gate as the connection, verified by test |
| GQL-098 | M | gh_git_writes_graphql.go, graphql_seams.go | the git-write mutations were absent: createRef, updateRef, deleteRef, mergeBranch, createCommitOnBranch, updateRefs and revertPullRequest had no GraphQL surface, and the Repos seam declared six methods nothing implemented — a dangling interface that broke the build when it landed | fixed — all seven mutations, each driving the same branch-protection, secret-scanning and push machinery the REST git-database routes run through six new seam implementations; updateRefs honours beforeOid as a lost-update refusal, a zero afterOid deletes, an absent reference is created; revertPullRequest builds the inverse commit on a revert branch and opens the pull request through the checked constructor with CODEOWNERS collection |
| GQL-099 | m | gh_discussions_graphql.go, store_discussions.go | discussions could not be closed: the store had no closed state, so closeDiscussion and reopenDiscussion could not exist and the Discussion type served neither closed, closedAt nor stateReason | fixed — close state on the store row, both mutations with the documented DiscussionCloseReason vocabulary and REOPENED on reopen, the closed and reopened webhook actions, and the three fields on the type |
| GQL-100 | m | gh_mutations_admin_graphql.go, node_finders.go | updateTeamsRepository, deletePackageVersion and cloneTemplateRepository were absent; teams and package versions had no node-id finders at all | fixed — the three mutations with exact signatures; team grants map github's five-value permission vocabulary onto the stored grant levels and refuse a team from another organization; deleting a package version answers not-yours identically to does-not-exist so the mutation is no existence oracle; template generation runs the same storage copy and owner rule as the REST generate route |
| TEST-096 | M | test/conformance | the conformance matrix carried four skips, and a skip counted as neither pass nor failure — 'no SDK can produce this' was asserted where the deployment could in fact produce it | fixed — zero skips: artifacts are produced by a genuinely dispatched workflow run and read back through each SDK's typed methods, gh attestation verify runs against a bundle the deployment serves with a custom trusted root, and the one subcommand gh refuses to send to a non-github.com host was replaced by a covering octokit operation that exercises linked branches for real; 698 of 698 operations pass |
| STORE-083 | M | store_sponsors.go | a recurring sponsorship had no billing cycle, so an upgrade could not bill and a downgrade or cancellation could not take effect at the end of a paid period | fixed — pending changes and cancellations applied on the cycle boundary, upgrades billed the prorated difference in integer cents with the multiplication before the division, and every reported money figure derived from the invoice ledger rather than stored separately to drift |
| STORE-084 | B | repos_wiki.go, store_wiki_git.go | the wiki page store and the wiki git repository were two independent copies of the same content, so a push and a browser edit could silently diverge | fixed — git is the single source of truth and the page map is a tip-stamped memo of a pure projection, rebuilt on any read whose tip moved, so a stale memo is detected by comparing object ids rather than invalidated by a writer that has to remember. The former persistence buckets were removed outright |
| STORE-085 | M | store_migrations_state.go | a migration's source lock was recorded and never honoured, so an importer migration's source stayed writable while it was being read | fixed — folded into the single predicate every repository write asks, so it holds across REST, GraphQL and both git transports, and a terminal migration releases it |
| CORE-034 | B | git_http.go, git_ssh.go, gh_wiki_git.go | `{owner}/{repo}.wiki.git` answered 404 on both git transports, so no unmodified client could clone or edit a wiki — and git is the only interface github gives wiki content | fixed — resolved to storage keyed on the `.wiki.git` suffix, which no repository can own because repository names ending in `.git` are refused, and served by the same upload-pack and receive-pack as any repository, so protocol v2, shallow, partial clone, thin packs and LFS all apply |
| CORE-035 | M | gh_wiki_git.go | a wiki write emitted no `gollum` webhook, the one Actions trigger the parity inventory recorded as having no producer | fixed — emitted from both the push and the browser lane with github's documented payload; a regression also asserts a wiki push raises no `push` event |
| CORE-036 | M | object_bytes_local.go, gh_repos_lfs.go | `git lfs` failed with a 501 unless object storage was configured, so a default deployment had no working LFS at all, which real github always has | fixed — a filesystem-backed byte store under the data directory, following the package-data precedent, with the object-storage path unchanged when configured and the persistence guard still refusing a persistent deployment without it |
| CORE-037 | B | gh_migrations_gei.go, server.go | the importer store layer existed but nothing ran it: there was no repository or organization migration worker, so a migration could only sit queued, and the resume call in the serving path referenced a function that was never written | fixed — both workers follow the export worker's pattern, claiming before working under the server lifetime, and the resume path runs ahead of the listener so a migration interrupted by a restart completes |
| CORE-038 | M | store_codespaces.go, gh_codespaces.go | Codespaces stopped containers with Docker's deprecated `--time` flag and gave start, stop and removal only 60 seconds; under ordinary concurrent lifecycle load Docker killed the commands at that boundary, returning 500 after the runtime operation had already begun | fixed — every Codespaces lifecycle operation shares a two-minute upper bound, stop uses the current `--timeout` flag, and a regression pins the exact CLI argument vector so the obsolete spelling cannot return |
| TEST-092 | M | graphql_source_audit_test.go | the typed-nil source class was found twice independently and neither instance failed a test, because the defect is invisible unless a query happens to select the absent child | fixed — a ratchet wraps every object field resolver of every test server and walks each resolved source map against the schema, so reaching a type audits all of its members rather than only the selected one. The walk is type-directed, so a nil map behind a leaf scalar is not mistaken for the defect; two self-tests keep it from going vacuous, and reverting a known fix reproduces the original error while reverting a newly-found one leaves every test green and fails only the ratchet |
| TEST-093 | M | fourteen assertions across eight files | the rename-redirect and secret-upsert fixes had landed while tests still pinned the pre-fix contract — 404 on a vacated repository name, 204 on secret creation — and one test pinned the installation-token deviation itself, so three separate sweeps reported landed work as broken | fixed — assertions moved to the documented contract. A test asserting the old behaviour is why a landed fix can still read as failing |
| TEST-094 | m | check-test-clock-dependencies.py | the clock gate scanned raw lines, so a comment explaining why a test avoids wall-clock reads counted as one — documenting the rule tripped the rule | fixed — whole-line comments are skipped, and a trailing comment marker is deliberately not stripped so a real call sitting after a URL cannot hide. The sponsors end-to-end fixture also named an organization from the clock, which made a failure unreproducible by re-running; it now derives from the worker and retry index |
| TEST-095 | M | test/conformance | nothing measured whether unmodified SDKs and CLIs work against bleephub, so the claim was asserted rather than tested | fixed — a harness boots a server and drives real gh, go-github, octokit, PyGithub and git through 698 operations, asserting each client's own parse rather than a status code, published as a machine-readable report and ratcheted so a pass can never silently become a failure. Its work directory is exclusive-locked because concurrent runs corrupted two scoreboards |
|---|---|
| `gh_code_scanning.go:49` registers an unreachable route without the `/api/v3` prefix | Reachable and deliberate. `gh_middleware.go:82-86` activates the API middleware for the `/repos/` and `/code-scanning/` prefixes and cites the reason: the official CodeQL Action posts to the uploads host. Cited provenance for an uploads-host path |
| `math/rand` use is a security weakness | Not a defect. The only non-test use picks a zen quote. All 18 token, secret and ID generation sites use `crypto/rand`, and UUIDs are crypto-backed |
